# CARE Radiology Implementation Guide

**Status:** Canonical engineering handbook — supersedes design-document guidance wherever they conflict (see Part 0, Decision D-02, for the precedence chain).

**Audience:** Engineers (including AI engineering agents) building or extending the CARE Radiology reporting platform. This handbook assumes no prior reading of the source design documents it consolidates.

**Scope:** This document converts the architecture and product intent captured across the Master Design Spec, the UX Review, the AI Radiologist Experience Spec, the Knowledge Catalog, the Knowledge Seed Specification, the as-authored YAML content packs, the as-built structured-report/catalog implementation, the Radiology Implementation Roadmap v2 (impression engine architecture), the app-wide Versioning Architecture, and the CTO Pre-Implementation Risk Review into a single set of binding implementation decisions. Where those documents disagreed, Part 0 records the adjudication; every later chapter cites the resulting decision by ID (e.g. "per D-07") instead of re-arguing it. This document contains no application code, no YAML, no JSON, and no API request/response bodies — only implementation guidance in prose, numbered rules, and tables.

**How this handbook was produced:** by fanning out one reading agent per source-document set to digest it, adjudicating every cross-document conflict and duplication into the canonical decision register that opens Part 0, then drafting each chapter against that register and adversarially re-verifying every chapter against the register, the source digests, and a banned-content/implementability check.

## Part 0 — How to use this handbook

This handbook is the single implementation authority for the CARE Radiology reporting platform. It exists because five generations of design and architecture documents accumulated in this repository, contradicted each other on nearly every load-bearing decision, and left no single place an engineer could read to know what to actually build. Every ambiguity below has already been adjudicated — nothing here is "TBD," and nothing later in this handbook re-opens a question this chapter has closed. Read this chapter first, in full, before opening any other chapter or any source design document. It gives you the authority chain, the reading order, a one-page mental model of the system, the full register of resolved conflicts (cited everywhere else by ID), the terminology you must use, the safety rules that may never be softened, and the engineering ground rules that bound every change you make on this box.

### 0.1 Purpose and authority

This handbook **consolidates** the following source documents and, wherever they conflict, **supersedes** them. It does not replace their per-study clinical content (the seed annex remains the normative data source per §0.4/D-02) — it replaces their standing as design or engineering authority.

| Document | Repo path |
|---|---|
| Master Design Spec | `CARE_RADIOLOGY_MASTER_DESIGN_SPEC.md` |
| UX Review | `RADIOLOGY_WORKSTATION_UX_REVIEW.md` |
| AI Experience Spec | `AI_RADIOLOGIST_EXPERIENCE_SPEC.md` |
| Knowledge Catalog | `RADIOLOGY_KNOWLEDGE_CATALOG.md` |
| Seed Specification | `RADIOLOGY_KNOWLEDGE_SEED_SPEC.md` |
| YAML content packs | `seeds/radiology/content-packs/v1/` (28 YAML files: 3 shared-library packs + 1 screening include-group + 24 study packs, plus `README.md`) |
| Structured-report JSON implementation (as-built) | `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/radiologyStructuredJsonCache.ts`, `radiologyStructuredJsonDrift.ts`; `migrations/add_report_finding_instances.sql`, `migrations/add_radiology_structured_json_columns.sql` |
| Catalog/versioning architecture | `VERSIONING_ARCHITECTURE.md` |
| Impression engine architecture (Roadmap v2) | `Antigravity/11_ROADMAP/RADIOLOGY_IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP_v2.md`, `Antigravity/01_ARCHITECTURE/Radiology_Architecture_Master.md` |
| CTO risk review | `CTO_PRE_IMPLEMENTATION_RISK_REVIEW.md` |

Five supporting governance/process documents remain **binding and in force** — this handbook does not supersede them, only interprets how they apply to radiology work: `DEVELOPMENT_PRINCIPLES.md`, `PROTECTED_FILES.md`, `HOW_TO_ADD_DB_MIGRATIONS.md`, `SYNOLOGY_DB_MIGRATION_DEPLOYMENT.md`, `GOVERNANCE_AND_CONFIG.md`.

**Precedence chain (D-02, BINDING)**, highest first, applies whenever two sources disagree and this handbook has not already adjudicated the point in §0.4:
1. Patient-safety rules of Master Spec §13 (§0.6 of this chapter) — never weakened by anything.
2. As-built landed migrations/code — for physical schema, endpoints, and flag mechanics.
3. `CARE_RADIOLOGY_MASTER_DESIGN_SPEC.md` — for all design/content conventions (§6, §8, §15–16 supersede Seed Spec Parts 1–2 & 7).
4. Seed Spec Parts 3–6 — the normative per-study data annex.
5. The YAML packs — the physical realization of the annex (field-name canon per D-13).
6. UX Review, AI Experience Spec, Knowledge Catalog — historical rationale only.
7. Roadmap v2 / Architecture Master — only where nothing above speaks.

This handbook itself sits above all seven for engineering decisions: where it states a choice, that choice is the implementation, full stop. Where it is silent, fall through the chain above.

### 0.2 Reading order for a new engineer

An engineer (including an AI engineer building from this handbook alone) reads in this order:

1. **Part 0 — How to use this handbook** (this chapter) — authority, system model, decision register, terminology, safety rules, ground rules.
2. **Chapter 1 — Catalog Lifecycle** — the governed clinical-content system: packs, shared libraries, versioning, load order, importer.
3. **Chapter 2 — Finding Lifecycle** — the atomic catalog entry, its six-element anatomy, `report_finding_instances`.
4. **Chapter 3 — Parameter Lifecycle** — `param.*`/`sev.*`/`lat.*`/`loc.*` shared value sets and ordering invariants.
5. **Chapter 4 — Recommendation Lifecycle** — `rec.*` codes, the two templated recommendations, scale↔management binding.
6. **Chapter 5 — Measurement Lifecycle** — `meas.*` fields, auto-calc formulas, offer-never-act insertion.
7. **Chapter 6 — Impression Lifecycle** — worst-first assembly, ghost-only offering, `/polish`.
8. **Chapter 7 — AI Interaction Lifecycle** — the eight AI laws, four surfaces, confidence bands, voice, audit trail, provenance.
9. **Chapter 8 — Feature Flags** — the `feature_flags` table, `ff_radiology_*` registry, degradation.
10. **Chapter 9 — Database Evolution** — the two sanctioned migration paths, additive-only doctrine, identity strategy for new tables.
11. **Chapter 10 — API Evolution** — route deletion stance, endpoint precedence, version namespaces.
12. **Chapter 11 — Testing** — the five importer validations, CI, the six CTO-flagged coverage gaps.
13. **Chapter 12 — Deployment** — Synology pipeline, protected files in practice, rollback.
14. **Chapter 13 — Analytics** — quarterly governance loop, scorecards, calibration.
15. **Chapter 14 — Security** — RBAC, authorship, immutability, audit-chain integrity, backups.
16. **Chapter 15 — Future Roadmap** — the strand roadmap of record, gated Future capabilities.

Chapters 1–7 (content and interaction) depend only on Part 0. Chapters 8–10 and 14 (platform: feature flags, database evolution, API evolution, security) depend on Part 0 and reference Chapters 1–7 for what they gate. Chapters 11–13 (process: testing, deployment, analytics) depend on all preceding chapters. Chapter 15 (future roadmap) depends on the whole handbook. Do not start writing code from any chapter without having read Part 0 in full; do not start Chapter 7+ without Chapters 1–6, since AI interaction is defined entirely in terms of catalog/finding/recommendation/measurement/impression objects.

### 0.3 System overview

Five components make up the platform. An engineer must hold this whole picture before touching any one part.

**1. The Reporting Workspace** (component `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx`, per D-01) is the one reporting surface — the only place a radiologist reads a study and produces a report. It presents a fixed-center report canvas flanked by a left context rail (queue + priors) and an adaptive right Stage Rail (five stages: Orient, Observe, Measure, Conclude, Verify), with a bottom command line. It drives the Zero-Click Read Loop: the worklist is a queue editor that serves the next-best study, pre-stages study N+1, and returns to the queue in under a second after sign (B-19, owned by Chapter 15). Every other UI concept in the older documents (Command Center, Cockpit, RCC, the 8-tab strip, Unified Reporting) is either this surface's predecessor or its legacy route; see Chapter 7 for interaction-loop invariants and Chapter 15 for the loop's build status.

**2. The knowledge catalog** is the governed clinical-content system: YAML packs in `seeds/radiology/content-packs/v1/` define ten v1 studies as finding entries bound by key to shared libraries (`param.` parameters, `sev.` severity scales, `lat.` laterality, `loc.` location grids, `meas.` measurements, `rec.` recommendations, `crit.` critical registry, `tpl.` templates). The catalog is the **entire vocabulary** of the reporting surface: every Quick Select tile, every AI suggestion, every lint rule, every impression fragment traces back to a catalog key. It is content-only — it never encodes schema, API shape, or UI layout (§15.2-10 of the Master Spec). Chapters 1–6 own this component.

**3. The structured report** is the data representation of what a radiologist has actually asserted. `report_finding_instances` is the source of truth for structured findings (one row per asserted finding, bound to a catalog `finding_id`); `radiology_report_drafts.structured_json` is a regenerated, never-hand-written cache of those rows (B-22); `patient_reports.body` is the authoritative signed prose record (D-19), stamped at sign with `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` (D-18, C-02). The catalog (component 2) supplies the vocabulary; the structured report is the instantiation of that vocabulary against one patient's study. Chapters 2, 5, and 9 own this component.

**4. The impression engine** assembles the report's conclusion from `impression_fragment`s carried by each asserted finding, ordered worst-first (critical > significant > rest), grouping multi-level/multi-organ disease into single lines, and is only ever offered as ghost text — never auto-inserted (B-14). A normal-study impression can never coexist with a positive finding; this is a block rule in every pack. Chapter 6 owns this component.

**5. The AI copilot** is the suggestion layer over all four other components: it reads the catalog's active entries as its entire vocabulary, proposes findings/measurements/recommendations/impression text through exactly four fixed surfaces (ghost text, margin cards, gutter marks, interrupt banner), and never acts without an explicit radiologist gesture (offer-never-act, B-08). Every rendered suggestion — accepted or not — is unconditionally audited (B-10) and every AI-assisted output is provenance-tracked without polluting the clinical document (D-32). Chapter 7 owns this component; Chapter 8 owns how its provider routing is flag-gated.

**How they relate:** the catalog is upstream of everything (it defines what can be said); the Reporting Workspace is the human's only entry point to all of it; the structured report is the durable record of what was said; the impression engine is a read-time projection over the structured report; the AI copilot is a suggestion layer that proposes into the structured report and the impression engine but can never bypass the radiologist. Nothing in the system asserts a clinical fact except a radiologist's explicit gesture recorded against a catalog key.

### 0.4 The canonical decision register

Every point on which two or more source documents gave conflicting instructions has been adjudicated exactly once, here, using the precedence chain in §0.1 plus four adjudication principles: (1) patient-safety rules are never weakened; (2) the CTO risk review's blocking findings are answered, not ignored; (3) as-built code and landed migrations win over aspirational design unless the design explicitly supersedes them with a migration path; (4) prefer the simpler operational story for a single-hospital Synology deployment scaling later to multi-center.

Every decision below carries a **BINDING** or **DEFAULT** tag. BINDING = safety- or architecture-critical, not overridable without Chief Clinical Architect + clinical-owner sign-off. DEFAULT = overridable by the product owner. **Other chapters cite these by ID (e.g. "per D-07") instead of re-arguing them.** Where a chapter is the owning chapter for a decision, it states the chosen implementation in full operational detail; this register states the adjudication, not the build instructions.

Two further, shorter registers exist and are cited the same way but are **not** reproduced here — each lives in its owning chapter, which states it in full: **Section B, "Consolidated duplications"** (30 entries, B-01…B-30, each an idea repeated across ≥2 source documents, stated once definitively) and **Section C, "Decided defaults"** (25 entries, C-01…C-25, gaps no source document answers, resolved for implementation to proceed). Both use narrower formats than the four-field one below; consult `scratchpad/conflict-register.md` Sections B and C directly, or the owning chapter named in that register, when you need one.

---

**D-01 — Canonical reporting surface: name and component — BINDING**
Current recommendation: four generations each named a different surface (Reporting Workspace / Cockpit / Command Center / `RadiologyReportUnified.tsx` / `RadiologyCommandCenter.tsx` / `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx`). Reason: each doc anointed whatever was newest when written; ticketed work silently moved development. Chosen implementation: product concept and all UI copy say **Reporting Workspace**; the go-forward component is **`RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx`**; `RadiologyReportUnified.tsx` and `RadiologyCommandCenter.tsx` are frozen, routes forward into the Workspace. Migration impact: route forwards from `/radiology/command-center` and `/radiology/unified-report/:worklistId`; nav copy re-worded; feature-parity checklist ported before route forwarding; UI-copy grep-and-fix for banned names.

**D-02 — Document precedence chain — BINDING**
See §0.1 above for the full chosen chain. Current recommendation: multiple docs each claimed supremacy. Reason: consolidation into the master spec happened after Seed Spec §7.1's own precedence note was written. Migration impact: none to code; Seed Spec §7.1 gets an erratum note.

**D-03 — Roadmap of record — BINDING**
Current recommendation: on-disk Roadmap v2 (Phase 0–7) vs. a ticketed "approved consolidation architecture" (T0.1, A1–A5, F1a/F1b, Strands A–N) that exists only in commits/comments. Reason: the ticketed roadmap superseded Roadmap v2 but was never written to disk. Chosen implementation: the **ticketed Strand A–N roadmap is the roadmap of record**, indexed by the 13 `ff_radiology_*` flags; Roadmap v2 items not re-expressed as a strand ticket are dropped (client `showX` flags, `/api/staff/feature-flags`, new `radiologist_macros`/`radiologist_favorites` work, Phase 7 toasts). Surviving Roadmap v2 decisions: batch finalization removed indefinitely; Split View conditional on demand; patientId-only prior matching; "AI Draft — Requires Radiologist Review" label. Migration impact: Chapter 15 is the written home of the strand plan; Roadmap v2 marked superseded on disk.

**D-04 — Critical registry v1 membership — BINDING**
Current recommendation: Master Spec §6.6/Seed Spec §2.7 list thirteen `crit.*` entries; Knowledge Catalog §0.6 lists a different thirteen. Reason: the catalog predates the seed conversion. Chosen implementation: the **Master Spec §6.6 / Seed Spec §2.7 thirteen** are the v1 registry (ICH all types, midline shift ≥5 mm, large-territory infarct, diffuse cerebral edema, acute cord compression, acute femoro-popliteal DVT, tension/complete pneumothorax, massive effusion with shift, free gas under diaphragm, pyonephrosis, BI-RADS 5, critical limb ischemia, aortic emergency); ectopic-pregnancy/torsion enter with the future USG Obstetric/Scrotum packs as MINOR catalog events with clinical sign-off; cerebral herniation stays under diffuse-edema/ICH escalation, not a separate key. Migration impact: none (packs already ship the thirteen).

**D-05 — Critical registry governance — BINDING**
Current recommendation: Catalog §0.6 called the registry "practice-editable"; Catalog §11.2/Master Spec §16.2 said centrally governed. Reason: internal drift, §0.6 was loose wording. Chosen implementation: registry, severity scales/order, scale↔management bindings, blocking lint rules, and escalation behavior are **centrally governed with clinical sign-off**; practices may propose additions and edit escalation contact routing/wording only. Migration impact: permissions model must not expose registry CRUD to practice-level editors.

**D-06 — BI-RADS 5 critical trigger: single source — BINDING**
Current recommendation: both `sev.birads`'s criticality map and the `crit.birads5` registry entry represent the same trigger. Reason: scale-side binding was added after the registry was authored. Chosen implementation: the **scale-side mapping fires**; the registry entry holds specialist routing/escalation only; importer deduplicates so the trigger fires exactly once; same pattern mandatory for future TI-RADS/PI-RADS. Migration impact: importer dedupe rule, documented in Chapter 1.

**D-07 — Severity "Gross" scope — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: Catalog §0.3 restricted "Gross" to hydronephrosis/hydrocephalus; Master Spec §6.1 also gives ascites its own scale. Chosen implementation: `sev.global` stays [mild, moderate, severe]; "Gross" exists only in **`sev.hydro` and `sev.ascites`**; no other scale adopts "gross" without a new `sev.*` key. Migration impact: none.

**D-08 — Canal-stenosis grading scale — BINDING**
Current recommendation: `radiologySmartEngine.ts` hardcodes mm thresholds (< 5 mm severe / 5–8 mm moderate) never clinically ratified; shared libraries define `sev.canal` as qualitative. Chosen implementation: **`sev.canal` (qualitative, radiologist-graded) is ratified**; hardcoded thresholds removed from the smart engine; canal AP diameter is a `meas.*` value that may power a suggested-grade ghost only (offer-never-act), never an asserted grade; numeric banding requires clinical-owner sign-off and enters as a `derives:` rule, not code. Migration impact: edit `radiologySmartEngine.ts` spine builders; lint rule stays non-blocking (△).

**D-09 — Voice dictation contract and verb set — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: UX review and AI spec undercounted verbs; Master Spec §12.6 enumerates exactly ten. Chosen implementation: the **Master Spec ten-verb set** (next field, previous field, impression, insert {macro}, accept, undo, park, sign, new paragraph, stop) is definitive; target contract is editor-native push-to-talk with provisional amber text; the batch transcription queue is legacy, no further investment; voice "sign" hits the same gate as `Ctrl+Enter`. Migration impact: Strand K builds the PTT contract, not the queue.

**D-10 — Measurement field count — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: UX review's counts (39, then 22–39) described a legacy UI tab, not the library. Chosen implementation: the **Seed Spec §2.5 measurement library (~31 fields)** is normative; UI field counts are descriptive, never contractual; auto-calc formulas/thresholds pinned per Master Spec §6.4. Migration impact: none.

**D-11 — Keyboard-alias length rule — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: docs said 2–4 chars; shipped packs contain ~20 five-to-six-char aliases; no importer has ever enforced the rule. Chosen implementation: rule **amended to 2–6 lowercase characters**; uniqueness within a study and no-strict-prefix remain hard invariants, plus reserved globals `nad`/`urgent`/`clincorr` never shadowed; shipped aliases grandfathered. Migration impact: `alias_rules.max_length` patched to 6; importer implements the amended rule.

**D-12 — Pack finding-block format harmonization — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: flat `ai_*` vs nested `ai:` formats coexist. Chosen implementation: **nested `ai:` wins** (richer, matches the most recent authoring wave and the `mri_brain` precedent); flat packs rewritten to nested with identical `id_key`s, each a MINOR bump; importer accepts both until harmonization completes, then drops flat. Migration impact: rewrite 9 flat packs; importer dual-format shim; `category` (flat-only) preserved as optional in nested schema.

**D-13 — Field-name and code-grammar drift (Seed Spec vs packs) — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: Seed Spec prescribes different field names (`severity_options`, `common_combo_tiles`, string-form `critical_if`) than the packs use (`severity`, `combo_tiles`, mapping-form `{critical_if, registry}`); Catalog uses kebab recommendation codes, packs use `rec.snake_case`. Chosen implementation: **the pack field vocabulary is canonical** — `severity`, `laterality`, `location`, `measurements`, `combo_tiles`, mapping-form `{critical_if, registry: crit.<key>}`, `{significant_if}`, and nested `ai.*` rule fields; recommendation codes are `rec.<snake_case>`. Migration impact: none to packs; Seed Spec §1.2 erratum; importer schema targets the pack vocabulary.

**D-14 — Shared-library extension chain and load order — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: `_shared_libraries_v1_2.yaml` declares the wrong `extends:` base; README omits it from load order; the critical registry forward-references keys defined later. Chosen implementation: canonical load order **`_shared_libraries.yaml` → `_shared_libraries_v1_1.yaml` → `_shared_libraries_v1_2.yaml` → `_screening_whole_spine.yaml` → study packs**, merged into one logical key namespace; importer uses two-pass resolution (load all keys, then validate references). Migration impact: fix README; implement two-pass resolution.

**D-15 — Study-block key naming in packs — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: `study.key`/`study.study`, `default_sections`/`default_report_sections`, singular/plural `keyboard_alias(es)` all coexist across packs. Chosen implementation: canonical study block uses **`study.key` + `study.display_name`**, **`default_report_sections`**, **`normal_template` inside `study:`**, plural **`keyboard_aliases`** everywhere. Migration impact: edit non-conforming packs in the same pass as D-12; importer accepts legacy spellings until then.

**D-16 — Single-source violations inside packs — BINDING**
Current recommendation: `mrbr.microbleeds` inlines a literal location list; nested packs carry free-text `recommendation` arrays alongside `recommendation_code`; `rec.fup_mmg6m` duplicates `sev.birads."3"` text. Reason: authoring shortcuts, no enforcing importer. Chosen implementation: all three are **conversion bugs** — add `loc.microbleed_distribution` to a shared-library extension (MINOR) and re-point `mrbr.microbleeds`; delete nested-format `recommendation` display arrays (`recommendation_code` is the only wording source); annotate `rec.fup_mmg6m` as `derived_from: sev.birads."3"` and verify textual equality at import; inline value lists become a hard importer error going forward. Migration impact: one shared-lib entry, pack edits, three importer checks.

**D-17 — Normal-template ID grammar vs doppler_ll — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: Seed Spec declares 1:1 `tpl.normal.<study>`, but `doppler_ll` ships two templates for one study. Chosen implementation: grammar amended to **`tpl.normal.<study>[_<variant>]`**; `doppler_ll` stays a single study (never split — the shared segment map is the point). Migration impact: none; grammar erratum.

**D-18 — catalog_version: format, defaults, stamping — BINDING**
Current recommendation: Master Spec says catalog-wide semver; as-built has `report_finding_instances.catalog_version` defaulted `'0'`, `patient_reports.catalog_version` nullable and never written, and one test using a date-style string. Chosen implementation: catalog_version is the **catalog semver** (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, starting `1.0.0` at first pack import), bumped only by the pack importer; `'0'` means "pre-catalog / unversioned row"; catalog_version, template_version, and render_engine_version are three independent per-artifact namespaces, distinct from the app's own version.json scheme; **stamping happens at sign** — the finalize path writes all three version columns plus `structured_json` in the same transaction that sets `final_report_id`. Migration impact: importer owns the bump; finalize path gains the stamping write; no schema change.

**D-19 — Authoritative signed-report store and immutability — BINDING**
Current recommendation: report content is fragmented across `patient_reports.body`, `radiology_studies.final_report`, `teleradiology_assignments.final_report`, and draft tables with 3–4 amendment chains and no single named authority. Chosen implementation: **`patient_reports.body` is THE authoritative signed record** — append-only, content-hashed at finalize, versions stamped per D-18; the other stores are denormalized projections; writes to them after finalization are prohibited (route-level now, DB-trigger hardening as a follow-up); post-sign changes go exclusively through `report_amendments` (one amendment chain). Migration impact: finalize path consolidates writes; route-level guards on legacy stores; reconciliation ticket maps existing rows.

**D-20 — Report state machine — BINDING**
Current recommendation: docs mix multi-stage/peer review (deprecated) with a simple DRAFT/FINAL model. Chosen implementation: canonical states **DRAFT → SIGNED (final) → AMENDED** (via `report_amendments`, repeatable); "Preliminary" is a DRAFT label, not a state; peer/multi-stage review stays deprecated; batch finalization removed indefinitely; AI never signs; sign gate blocks only per Master Spec §13.2 (✕ contradictions, unmet mandatory fields, untouched provisional spans, unstarted escalations for written critical findings). Migration impact: none to schema; peer-review tables/endpoints marked legacy read-only.

**D-21 — Measurement auto-insert vs offer-never-act — BINDING**
Current recommendation: Roadmap v2 Phase 6 allowed medium+ confidence auto-insert; Master Spec Law 1/P6 forbid any AI insertion without explicit gesture. Chosen implementation: **no confidence level auto-inserts**; extracted measurements land as pending values in the Measurement Assistant; text insertion always requires an explicit gesture (per-row Insert, "Insert All", `Tab` on a ghost); confidence gates presentation only — low-confidence values are flagged and require field-level review before becoming insertable at all. Migration impact: Strand C implements gesture-gated insertion; any existing auto-insert path is removed.

**D-22 — Confidence display vocabulary — BINDING**
Current recommendation: reading-surface doctrine specifies three named bands, no percentages; the extraction pipeline uses High/Medium/Low with red included. Chosen implementation: **internally** the model layer may keep raw scores/tiers; **at the reading surface** everything maps to **Routine / Worth a look / Attention** (grey/amber, no green, no percentages); low-tier extraction maps to Attention; thresholds are calibration-owned config, never hardcoded. Migration impact: UI mapping layer in Measurement/AI panels; copy changes.

**D-23 — Notification model — BINDING**
Current recommendation: Roadmap v2 Phase 7 proposed toast notifications; Master Spec §3.6 defines exactly three channels with no toasts. Chosen implementation: the **three-channel model (Interrupt / Edge glow / Ledger) is canonical**; Phase 7's toast plan is superseded; "draft ready/delivered/measurements extracted" are edge-glow events; only confirmed-critical events may interrupt; everything else goes to the ledger (`F9`); zero non-critical interruptions during dictation is a bug class. Migration impact: replace existing toast usage in the radiology loop as the loop release ships.

**D-24 — Feature-flag mechanism — BINDING**
Current recommendation: three generations of flag mechanisms accreted — Roadmap v2 client `localStorage` flags, a proposed `/api/staff/feature-flags`, an env-var pattern, and the as-built server `feature_flags` table. CTO flags client-only flags as HIGH risk. Chosen implementation: the **server `feature_flags` table with `ff_radiology_*` keys and `GET/PATCH /api/feature-flags` is the only sanctioned mechanism**; all new flags default false and are created by migration, not API; legacy `localStorage` flags are frozen (no new ones, migrate to `ff_radiology_*` when their surface is next touched); env vars remain for infrastructure config only. Migration impact: migrate the three copilot-panel flags to server keys; document the flag registry in Chapter 8.

**D-25 — Macro storage — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: three macro tables exist as-built (`radiologist_macros`, `radiology_text_macros`, `radiology_smart_macros`) plus a preferences-embedded variant; Roadmap v2 and Architecture Master each proposed a different single store. Chosen implementation: **`radiology_text_macros` (+ `radiology_smart_macros`) are canonical** — they back the actively developed report-generator module; `radiologist_macros` and preference-embedded macros are legacy read-only with a one-time import into text macros; the `/` command line fronts this single store. Migration impact: one-time content migration script; deprecate legacy macro endpoints from nav.

**D-26 — Favorites storage — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: Roadmap v2 proposed `radiologist_favorites`; as-built shipped `radiology_quick_favorites` instead. Chosen implementation: **`radiology_quick_favorites` is canonical** for tile pinning/starring; the broader "favorites of any snippet type" concept folds into macros (D-25) and tile pins; no `radiologist_favorites` table is created. Migration impact: none.

**D-27 — API route deletion stance — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: Roadmap v2 says no deletions; Architecture Master's stale inventory recommends deleting live routes including the active finalize path. Chosen implementation: **no route deletions** (backward compatibility is non-negotiable); dead/superseded routes are de-navigated and marked deprecated in the API inventory until a successor is live plus one release; `/api/usg-reports/*` remains the finalize surface until the Workspace sign pipeline replaces it with an explicit migration. Migration impact: API inventory annotations only.

**D-28 — Canonical worklist/study tables — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: a DB map recommends `dicom_studies` as canonical and `radiology_worklist` as deprecated/a view, but this was never executed — active workflows are all on `radiology_worklist`. Chosen implementation: **as-built wins now** — `radiology_worklist` remains the operational worklist key, `dicom_studies` is canonical for PACS/DICOM study identity, `radiology_studies` remains the RIS/billing-linked record; convergence path is a `v_unified_worklist` read view (joined on accession_number) built first, with `radiology_worklist` becoming a view only after it serves all readers. Migration impact: future view ticket; Chapter 9 documents the three-table roles.

**D-29 — Template and normal-template tables — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: `radiology_structured_templates` and `structured_report_templates` are both marked implemented; normal templates are attributed to two different tables across docs. Chosen implementation: **`structured_report_templates`** (modality/bodyPart presets, active routes, 14 seedable presets) is the authoritative structured-template store and the render_v2 consolidation target; **`ai_normal_report_templates`** backs normal templates; `template_version` on `patient_reports` versions the `structured_report_templates` row used at sign. Migration impact: freeze writes to legacy template tables; render_v2 (Strand F) consolidates; inventory corrected.

**D-30 — Primary AI drafting endpoint — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: memory names `/api/ai-reporting/query` as primary; a stale inventory names `/api/internal/radiology/ai-draft` and `/api/smart-radiology/ai-impressions` instead, never mentioning the first. Chosen implementation: **`/api/ai-reporting/query` is the primary interactive drafting endpoint** (radiologist-initiated, resolves the `radiology_draft` task route inline: caller override → active task route → global default); `/api/internal/radiology/ai-draft` is the PACS-driven background job hook (`ai_job_queue`); `/api/smart-radiology/ai-impressions` is legacy, frozen. Migration impact: inventory correction; copilot consolidation routes new features through `/api/ai-reporting/query` or `generateAiForTask`.

**D-31 — AI provider posture — BINDING**
Current recommendation: Architecture Master describes AI Draft via Gemini through a stale Replit proxy; Master Spec mandates local-first; as-built has Ollama in `clinic_settings` and Synology docker-compose deployment (no Replit). Chosen implementation: **local-first** — Ollama (via `/api/radiology-ollama` and `clinic_settings`) is the default engine posture; cloud providers (Gemini/OpenAI/Claude) are enabled per task via `ai_model_routes` and provider-enable flags; routing is invisible to the radiologist but the answering engine is always disclosed in the card/panel footer. Migration impact: purge Replit-proxy references from Architecture Master; confirm no AI code path still targets `caredeoghar.replit.app` (see D-35 for the backup analogue).

**D-32 — AI-text provenance in signed reports — BINDING**
Current recommendation: CTO §4.6 (HIGH) found AI-inserted text indistinguishable in the stored report, with audit writes swallowed by `.catch(()=>{})`; Master Spec keeps inline markers out of the clinical text by design. Chosen implementation: **the clinical body stays clean** (no inline markers beyond the governance disclosure line) while **provenance is recorded as data** — per-suggestion audit records (band, raw score, sources, engine/version, disposition; unconditional, never disableable per study) plus span-level provenance stored alongside the report for the provenance view; the five `.catch(()=>{})` swallows in `aiReporting.ts` are removed — AI audit writes must fail loudly and join the tamper-evident chain; interim rule until the full trail ships: every AI-assisted output shows "AI Draft — Requires Radiologist Review" and the insert path records suggestion id + span offsets, not just a boolean. Migration impact: fix the five swallow sites; extend insert-to-report to persist span provenance; audit-trail tables per Chapter 7.

**D-33 — Report authorship and finalize RBAC — BINDING**
Current recommendation: CTO §4.8 (HIGH) found authorship is a client-supplied string; `canFinalize`/`canApprove` bits are defined but never enforced. Chosen implementation: **authorship is server-derived from the authenticated staff session (`subjectName`) at finalize; client-supplied author strings are ignored**; `canFinalize`/`canApprove` are enforced on every finalize/amend route; a `radiology_typist` can never mutate a signed report (D-19 guards). Migration impact: route changes on finalize/amend endpoints; permission seeding for radiologist roles; regression tests on the finalize path.

**D-34 — Audit-chain fix vs "audit logging is off-limits" — BINDING**
Current recommendation: CTO CRIT-2 mandates serializing the hash-chain; governance lists "change audit logging" as prohibited without approval. Reason: the prohibition targets casual weakening, not a mandated integrity fix. Chosen implementation: this register **is** the recorded approval for the fix — `pg_advisory_lock` around the read-then-insert (already used in-tree for patients/day-close), a **unique** index on the chain hash, `REVOKE UPDATE/DELETE` (or a BEFORE trigger) on audit tables, a real verification endpoint, and bigint PK on the audit table. E0.2's landed concurrency fix is ratified. Migration impact: complete remaining items as manual-approval migrations, changelog-recorded with Dr. Abinash sign-off noted.

**D-35 — Backup source of truth — BINDING**
Current recommendation: three backup mechanisms accreted with no declared source of truth — a row-capped in-app backup that recorded false success and omitted `patient_reports`, a `synology-backup.sh` script pulling from a different (Replit) database, and an on-box `pg_dump` doc. Chosen implementation: **the single backup source of truth is the on-prem Postgres** — streaming `pg_dump` (plus WAL archiving as follow-up) to `/volume1/backup/` with restore-verification; the Replit-pull script is retired; the in-app row-capped path stays fixed per E0.1 and must never report success on a partial dump; backups must always include `patient_reports` and audit tables. Migration impact: retire the remote-pull script; wire restore-verification; DR runbook corrected (volume `care_main_db_data`; plain-format dumps restore via `psql`, not `pg_restore`).

**D-36 — Canonical migration path and fail-closed deploys — BINDING**
Current recommendation: four schema-mutation paths exist (Drizzle files via care-migrate, feature migrations via db-patch-entrypoint Step 5, imperative Step-4 column patches, `runStartupMigrations()` in the api-server) with `ON_ERROR_STOP=0 || true` masking failed migrations as success. Chosen implementation: exactly **two sanctioned paths** going forward — (1) Drizzle migration files run by care-migrate (never `drizzle-kit push`), and (2) idempotent feature migrations in `migrations/*.sql` registered in entrypoint Step 5; `runStartupMigrations()` is frozen (existing entries stay, no new tables added there); care-db-patch-v2 Step 4 retained until production is verified on schema v5+, then retired; feature migrations run with `ON_ERROR_STOP=1` so a failed migration fails the deploy; Step-5 registration order is authoritative, the alphabetical glob is removed; `add_performance_indexes.sql` is registered in Step 5. Migration impact: entrypoint edits; Chapter 9 states the two-path rule.

**D-37 — Identity strategy for new tables vs frozen architecture — BINDING**
Current recommendation: CTO C1 demands bigint identity PKs, nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id`, globally-unique business keys on every new table; the architecture is declared frozen with 319 existing serial int4 PKs and "no ALTER TABLE." Reason: frozen-architecture language is about not redesigning what exists, not about what gets created next. Chosen implementation: **prospective adoption** — every NEW table uses `bigint` identity PK, carries nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id`, and any new business/accession key generator is globally unique (UUID or site-prefixed), never `MAX(seq)+1`; existing tables are untouched until a dedicated future strand; high-churn existing int4 tables (audit logs) get bigint conversion scheduled with D-34's audit work. Migration impact: schema-review checklist item; template snippet in Chapter 9; no immediate migrations.

**D-38 — Keyboard map: illustrative vs canonical — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: UX Review labels all bindings "illustrative"; Master Spec §11.1 fixes a canonical default keymap. Chosen implementation: the **Master Spec §11.1 keymap is the canonical default** (`Ctrl+Enter` sign-and-next, `Ctrl+Q` overlay, `Ctrl+Space` PTT, `F8` lint, `F2` flip, `F9` ledger, `Ctrl+P` park, `Ctrl+→` skip, `/` or `Ctrl+K` command line), all user-remappable; voice "sign" and `Ctrl+Enter` share one gate. Migration impact: keybinding store per user; defaults constant in one module.

**D-39 — Priority vocabulary and test-suite claims — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: Master Spec §17.1 defines Must/Should/Future as capability tier and P0–P3 as build order only, but older docs mix them; `DEVELOPMENT_PRINCIPLES.md` claims "all 164+ tests" while the CTO review counts 378 tests in 30 files with zero coverage of finalize/immutability/hash-chain/amendment/critical-finding/Form-F paths. Chosen implementation: **Must/Should/Future is the only capability-tier vocabulary**; P0–P3 appears only as release ordering; the test rule is restated as "the full suite passes" (no fixed number); Chapter 11 mandates new coverage for exactly the six CTO-flagged paths. Migration impact: `DEVELOPMENT_PRINCIPLES.md` erratum; test backlog tickets for the six paths.

**D-40 — Stale inventories: echo/fetal tables and repo paths — DEFAULT**
Current recommendation: an inventory names `echo_cardiology`/`fetal_usg_level4` as tables that don't exist; a DB map and `GOVERNANCE_AND_CONFIG.md` cite `apps/…` paths that no longer exist. Chosen implementation: **`lib/db/src/schema/` is ground truth for table names** — the real decomposed table set is `echo_measurements`, `echo_reports`, `echo_valve_assessment`, `echo_regional_walls`, `echo_audit_logs`, `fetal_echo_studies`, `fetal_usg_studies`, `fetal_usg_measurements`, `fetal_usg_reports`, etc.; repo paths are `artifacts/api-server/` and `artifacts/diagnostic-erp/`. This handbook never cites a stale inventory for a table name without schema verification. Migration impact: documentation only.

### 0.5 Canonical terminology

Use only these terms in code comments, UI copy, commit messages, and new documentation. The right-hand column is banned outside a search-synonym thesaurus.

| Canonical term | Definition | Banned synonyms |
|---|---|---|
| **Reporting Workspace** | The single reporting surface (`RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx`) | Cockpit, Command Center, RCC, Unified Reporting (as surface name) |
| **Quick Select** | The tile system for findings | Chocolate Box, quick findings panel (as product name) |
| **tile** | One Quick Select unit (finding + defaults; combo tile = ordered multi-entry) | chip, button (catalog contexts), chocolate |
| **finding** | The atomic catalog entry (`<prefix>.<key>`) and its report instance | observation, abnormality (except frozen code type `AbnormalityInstance`), smart finding (as concept) |
| **catalog** | The whole governed clinical-content system | knowledge base, knowledge catalog (as system name) |
| **pack** | One YAML seed file in `seeds/radiology/content-packs/` | catalog (for a single file), bundle (until Strand H delta bundles exist) |
| **shared library** | The `param./sev./lat./loc./meas./rec./crit./tpl.` key families | parameter dictionary, lookup lists |
| **keyboard alias** | The 2–6-char per-study tile mnemonic | mnemonic (alone), shortcut (reserve for OS-level keys), hotkey |
| **macro** | A `/`-namespace text expansion, stored in `radiology_text_macros` | snippet (reserve for `radiology_normal_snippets`), template (never) |
| **sign / sign-off** | The radiologist's act that freezes the report (verb: **Sign**) | finalize (legacy API term only, e.g. `/finalize` routes), approve, verify (as the signing act) |
| **stage rail** | The single adaptive right rail (Orient, Observe, Measure, Conclude, Verify) | tool strip, 8-tab panel, right tabs |
| **ghost text** | Dimmed provisional inline AI text accepted sentence-wise with `Tab` | inline draft, AI autofill, autocomplete |
| **margin card** | The one AI card anatomy (claim → band chip → sources → Insert/Explain/Dismiss) | suggestion popup, AI panel card, toast |
| **gutter mark / lint** | The ✕/△/◌ in-margin quality marks | Quality Inspector warnings (legacy), QA flags |
| **confidence band** | Routine / Worth a look / Attention | High/Medium/Low (in any reading-surface UI), percentages, scores |
| **park** | Deferring a study with a mandatory breadcrumb | hold, defer (as action name), snooze |
| **escalation / critical-results workflow** | The workflow fired by `crit.*` entries, filling `rec.urgent` | critical alert flow, urgent notification (as workflow name) |
| **impression fragment** | The per-finding contribution to the assembled impression | impression phrase, impression line (for the entry element) |
| **normal template** (`tpl.normal.*`) | A study's one-keystroke full-normal text | normal snippet, NAD template |
| **dorsal spine, D1–D12** | Vertebral naming in all content keys and report text (Indian convention) | thoracic T1–T12 (in content keys; acceptable in synonym thesaurus) |
| **Must / Should / Future** | Capability tiers (P0–P3 = build order only) | P0–P3 as capability tiers |
| **study** | A catalog study type (`study.<key>`) and a worklist exam instance | exam, scan (catalog/keys), procedure (billing term stays separate) |

### 0.6 Binding safety rules

The following rules are reproduced at full strength — no chapter may implement a weaker version, and no product-owner override is possible without Chief Clinical Architect + clinical-owner sign-off. This is the master spec's §13, condensed for density but not softened.

1. **Authorship is structural.** Sign-off attests radiologist authorship and is structurally impossible while any AI-originated span remains untouched-provisional; the gate lists remaining spans as jump-links.
2. **The sign gate is small and strict.** Only unresolved critical items block: ✕ contradictions, unmet mandatory fields (e.g. BI-RADS without composition), unreviewed provisional spans, unstarted escalations for written critical findings.
3. **Lint tiers.** ✕ = report would be internally false (laterality contradiction, certainty contradiction, category-vs-descriptor violation): **blocking**. △ = inconsistency (text vs measurement-field divergence, severity vs number mismatch): non-blocking, dismissible with reason. ◌ = completeness (unaddressed organ/level/lesion/indication): non-blocking; `normal_variant` entries never raise ◌.
4. **Identity is ambient.** A per-patient color-fingerprinted identity band spans report and viewer; wrong-patient work is made structurally visible, not procedurally checked; root-map lint (e.g. L4-5 disc → traversing root L5; C5-6 → C6) and side-vs-indication lint run continuously.
5. **Critical escalation.** A written-finding pattern match raises a margin priority card (never covering text); one keystroke launches the critical-results workflow — referrer contact surfaced, communication sentence auto-documented (`rec.urgent` slots filled), timestamps recorded, identity-band chip shown until closed; declining requires a reason; pre-read image flags (Future tier) only re-order the queue, always labeled, never suppressing the independent read.
6. **Safety scales bind management.** A safety-scale category insertion (e.g. BI-RADS) always carries its bound management line; descriptor-vs-category violations (e.g. spiculated + BI-RADS 2) are ✕-blocking; this is the mandatory pattern for future TI-RADS/PI-RADS-class scales (per B-05).
7. **Templated urgency.** `rec.urgent` and `rec.fleischner` fill slots from the escalation workflow / AI at runtime; the radiologist confirms; free-text urgent advice is a lint finding.
8. **Personalization firewall.** Learned preferences never alter lint, escalation, calibration, or guideline anchors; the safety net cannot be trained into silence.
9. **Never lose a sentence.** Draft state survives crash, close, and roaming; re-auth and updates never interrupt an open report; the input loop is protected above all rendering work.
10. **Explicit degradation.** AI down or model under review ⇒ exactly one banner ("Copilot offline — full manual mode"); every feature degrades to its manual equivalent; nothing half-works silently.
11. **Standing refusals.** The AI never renders prognosis to patients, never communicates externally without radiologist action, and refuses out-of-scope asks (e.g. "write the whole report") with the reason stated.
12. **Unconditional audit and atomic undo apply to everything above.** Audit recording cannot be disabled per study; every injection (tile, macro, AI accept, voice utterance) is one atomic undo step.

Additional binding MUSTs that sit outside §13 but carry the same force:
- AI output is proposal-only in provisional styling; it never inserts, deletes, scrolls, or moves the cursor without an explicit gesture.
- Suggestions that cannot cite a source do not render; unverifiable citations never render; generated-prose justifications are banned — sources only.
- No probabilities or percentages beside diagnoses or anywhere in the reading eye; exactly three confidence bands; no green in the palette.
- Only confirmed-critical findings may interrupt; an open mic silences all non-critical AI output.
- `id_key`s are immutable API — never renamed, reused, or repurposed; retired keys resolve forever; retired aliases are never reassigned to a different clinical meaning (B-01).
- Ordered parameter/severity sets never reorder — append only (B-04).
- The completeness engine fills unaddressed regions only on radiologist confirmation, never silently (B-17).
- Lesion identity linking across studies is always an explicit human act.
- No background completion, refetch, or panel update may steal keyboard focus from the editor — ever.
- The AI Copilot consumes `active` catalog entries only; AI-proposed content enters as `draft` for a human content editor.
- Hard critical triggers (midline shift ≥5 mm, BI-RADS 5) and all ✕-blocking rules must be deterministic — an LLM may add flags but is never the sole gate for a blocking or critical rule (C-04).
- Provisional markers clear on edit or explicit section confirmation only; dwell/viewport time is never sufficient, for AI or dictation text alike (C-12).

### 0.7 Non-negotiable engineering ground rules

These come from `DEVELOPMENT_PRINCIPLES.md`, `PROTECTED_FILES.md`, `HOW_TO_ADD_DB_MIGRATIONS.md`, `SYNOLOGY_DB_MIGRATION_DEPLOYMENT.md`, and `GOVERNANCE_AND_CONFIG.md`. They remain in force unmodified by this handbook; where the CTO risk review required a change to something they call off-limits (audit logging, backups), this register (D-34, D-35) is the recorded approval for that specific, narrow change — nothing else in these five documents is reopened.

**Protected files.** `PROTECTED_FILES.md` is the source of truth and is itself a living document — new billing/radiology files are added to it in the same commit that creates them. Three zones: 🔴 Billing/Payments (bills, payments, orders, banking, expenses, accounting, commission, day-close, doctor-ledger, discounts, gateway-webhooks, tokens, vendors, kiosk, and their schema/frontend) — any change requires Dr. Abinash's explicit sign-off before merging, regardless of size. 🟡 Radiology/PACS/AI-reporting routes, PACS lib, radiology schema, radiology frontend — developed and refactored freely. 🟢 Shared/core files (auth/identity, patients/doctors/branches/departments/locations/machines, `sync.ts`, HL7/barcode/WhatsApp/booking integrations, generic AI infrastructure `ai.ts`/`aiModelRoutes`/`aiPromptLibrary`/`aiPromptTemplates`/`aiComparison`, audit-logs/backup/system-health, and the frontend shell `Layout.tsx`/`App.tsx`/`components/ui/*`) — treat with the same caution as billing; `sync.ts` in particular must never be modified by radiology-focused changes, and AI infrastructure must never be forked per module.

**Migration discipline.** Every schema change follows the two-step process in `HOW_TO_ADD_DB_MIGRATIONS.md`: (1) write `migrations/your_feature_name.sql` using only `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` — never `DROP`/`TRUNCATE`/`DELETE` without an existence check, never `NOT NULL` without a `DEFAULT`, safe to run ten times with no error and no data change after the first run, never reuse a filename; (2) register it as a `run_feature_migration` line in Step 5 of `docker/db-patch-entrypoint.sh`. There are no automatic rollbacks — migrations are forward-only; a rollback is a new compensating SQL file applied manually via `docker exec -it care-db psql -U erp -d diagnostic_erp`. Per D-36, exactly two paths are sanctioned going forward (Drizzle files via care-migrate, and idempotent feature migrations via Step 5); `runStartupMigrations()` in the api-server is frozen with no new tables; feature migrations must run with `ON_ERROR_STOP=1` so a failed migration fails the deploy, not silently succeeds.

**The `drizzle-kit push` hazard.** Never run blind `drizzle-kit push` (interactively or as `pushSchema` in CI/post-merge) against this database. The live schema has drifted enough that the interactive rename resolver confuses `report_delivery_logs` with `kiosk_payment_sessions` — accepting its prompt can drop or rename a live table, and in CI it hangs regardless of TTY because the resolver reads raw stdin. Additive changes go through `psql "$DATABASE_URL"` directly with `CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS`, keeping the Drizzle schema in sync for types afterward. CI/post-merge uses `generateDrizzleJson` + `generateMigration` (`pnpm --filter @workspace/db run push-ci`), applying each statement while catching Postgres error codes `42P07` (duplicate_table) and `42701` (duplicate_column). `push --force` is destructive and is used only after a full manual review of the generated statements — never to accept the rename prompt blindly.

**Deployment constraints.** The Synology container ordering is fixed and enforced by Docker Compose `service_completed_successfully`: `care-db` (healthy) → `care-migrate` → `care-db-patch-v2` → `care-api` → `care-web`. `care-migrate` and `care-db-patch-v2` have `restart: "no"`; `care-api`/`care-web` are `unless-stopped`. Deploy is `git pull origin main` → `docker compose build --no-cache` → `docker compose up -d` → verify via `docker logs care-migrate`/`care-db-patch-v2` and `docker compose ps` (expect `Exited (0)`). Always take a `pg_dump` backup before any production schema change; restore from backup before dropping a column on production. Never hardcode LAN IPs, ports, API keys/credentials, viewer URLs, or PACS/Orthanc/DICOM configuration — these load from `.env`, the admin settings DB table, or the config UI only; the known violations (`172.16.1.139`/`192.168.1.137`/`caredeoghar.com` in `viewerService.ts`, the `0004_seed_pacs_viewer_defaults` seed) are scheduled fixes, not precedents to extend. The platform's optimization targets are fixed and must remain compatible: Synology Docker, PostgreSQL, Orthanc, OHIF, Weasis, standard DICOM modalities, LAN deployment — not cloud-dependent. `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck` (against the checked-in error baseline) must pass, with no new lint errors, before any commit; production-code changes are verified on the actual Synology Docker environment. Backward compatibility is non-negotiable: no broken API contracts, deprecations documented, migration guides for DB changes, and no removal of working functionality unless the replacement is demonstrably better and fully backward compatible.

### Acceptance criteria

- Every engineer and AI agent working on this codebase has read this chapter before opening any other chapter or source design document.
- No chapter, commit message, or UI string uses a banned synonym from §0.5.
- No chapter restates a D-numbered decision's reasoning; each cites the ID and states only its own build-order detail.
- No pull request touching a 🔴 file merges without Dr. Abinash's explicit sign-off, and no pull request touching a 🟢 file is reviewed with less caution than a 🔴 one.
- No schema change ships that isn't one of the two D-36-sanctioned migration paths, and no schema migration is applied via `drizzle-kit push` in any environment.
- Every new table created after this handbook's adoption uses a `bigint` identity PK, nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id`, and a globally-unique business key generator (D-37) — verified at schema review, not after merge.
- All twelve §0.6 safety rules and the listed additional MUSTs are traceable to a specific enforcing mechanism (gate, lint rule, route guard, or DB constraint) named in the owning chapter — "documented but unenforced" is not acceptable for any of them.
- The decision register in §0.4 is treated as closed: any engineer who believes a D-numbered decision should change escalates to the Chief Clinical Architect and clinical owner rather than re-implementing the superseded option.

### Build order

Part 0 has no code deliverable — it is the reference every other chapter's build order depends on. Concretely:

1. Land this chapter (or its published equivalent) in the repository as `CARE_RADIOLOGY_IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md` Part 0 before any other chapter's work is merged, so that decision IDs are citable.
2. Wire the terminology table (§0.5) into a lint rule (grep-based is sufficient) that fails CI on a banned synonym in `artifacts/diagnostic-erp/src/**` and `artifacts/api-server/src/**` radiology paths — this is a Chapter 11 (Testing) deliverable but depends on this chapter existing first.
3. Confirm `PROTECTED_FILES.md` zone labels are current for every file Chapters 1–15 will touch before those chapters' implementation work starts; file the living-document update in the same commit as any new file.
4. Everything else follows the reading order in §0.2: Chapters 1–6 (content/data model), then 7 (AI interaction), then 8–10 and 14 (platform: feature flags, database evolution, API evolution, security), then 11–13 (process: testing, deployment, analytics), then 15 (future roadmap).

---

## Chapter 1 — Catalog lifecycle

The catalog is the whole governed clinical-content system: shared libraries (`param.` `sev.` `lat.` `loc.` `meas.` `rec.` `crit.` `tpl.normal.`), study definitions, findings, combo tiles, and normal templates. Content is authored as YAML packs in git, reviewed under a two-tier governance split, imported into database rows by a fail-loud importer, versioned at both the pack and catalog level independently of the app's own version, and — because signed reports must remain legible forever — never truly forgotten: entries retire, they are essentially never deleted, and a bad import is undone by fixing forward, not by rolling the database back. This chapter is the owning chapter for catalog-wide versioning, the alias grammar, the import pipeline, and the retire/delete/rollback contract; it is not the owning chapter for finding block shape (see Chapter 2, finding-lifecycle), parameter/severity content (Chapter 3, parameter-lifecycle), or recommendation binding (Chapter 4, recommendation-lifecycle) — those chapters state their own content once and reference this one for lifecycle mechanics.

### Author

**Who authors.** Every catalog entry has a named radiologist content editor (its `owner`). Two further roles bound authoring: the clinical owner, who must sign off on anything centrally governed (per D-05: severity scales and their order, scale↔management bindings such as BI-RADS, the critical registry, blocking lint rules, escalation behavior), and engineering, which owns seed-conversion fidelity — that the YAML a content editor writes actually parses, resolves, and validates. Practice-level editors may author *additive and phrasal* content only: wording, new tiles, re-pinning, combos, synonyms, and locale overlays (D-05, C-24). They may never edit or remove a centrally governed entry.

**Format.** The authoring source of truth is YAML packs under `seeds/radiology/content-packs/v1/` (C-01) — git-diffable, one pack per study or per shared-library extension. A pack opens with a `pack:` envelope (`pack_id`, `version` semver, `locale: en-IN`, `status`), and study packs carry `study.key` / `study.display_name`, `default_report_sections`, `normal_template` (inside `study:`), `keyboard_aliases` (plural), and `quick_select_groups` with a `Pinned` group first — this is the canonical study-block shape (D-15); packs still using `study.study`, `default_sections`, or top-level `normal_template` are non-conforming and are corrected in the same pass that harmonizes finding-block format (below).

**Finding-block format.** Two formats exist in the wild; the nested `ai:` format is canonical going forward (D-12): `location` (not `location_options`), `keyboard_aliases`, mapping-form `criticality` (`{critical_if, registry}` / `{significant_if}`), and an `ai:` block with `completeness_checks` / `contradiction_checks` / `differential` / `follow_up` (D-13). No `recommendation` display-text array — `recommendation_code` is the only wording source (D-16b). The nine remaining flat-format packs are rewritten to nested as a MINOR version bump per study pack (the `mri_brain` 1.0.0→1.1.0 precedent); the importer accepts both formats until that rewrite completes, then flat support is dropped (D-12).

**Authoring conventions (binding, B-02, B-04, D-16).** Bind by reference, never copy: a finding points at `param.*`/`sev.*`/`lat.*`/`loc.*`/`meas.*`/`rec.*` keys, never an inlined value list — an inlined list (e.g. the shipped `mrbr.microbleeds.location` array) is a conversion bug the importer must catch (see Import, below). Ordered sets (`cord_ladder`, `calc_morphology`, margin ladders, every `sev.*`) extend by appending only — position is load-bearing for lint and impression assembly and must never be reordered. Where a management line is authoritative on a scale (`sev.birads`), a derived recommendation text (`rec.fup_mmg6m`) is annotated `derived_from` and the importer verifies textual equality at import time, failing loud on drift (D-16c).

**ID grammar.** Eleven entity types share one dotted-lowercase grammar: `study.<key>`, `<study_prefix>.<finding_key>`, `param.<key>`, `sev.<key>`, `lat.<key>`, `loc.<key>`, `meas.<key>`, `rec.<key>`, `crit.<key>`, `combo.<study>.<key>`, `tpl.normal.<study>[_<variant>]` (the bracketed variant exists because `doppler_ll` ships venous and arterial templates for one study, D-17). The study-prefix registry is fixed and never reused (B-29):

| Study | Prefix | | Study | Prefix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mri_brain | `mrbr` | | usg_kub | `uskb` |
| mri_ls_spine | `mrls` | | hrct_chest | `hrct` |
| mri_c_spine | `mrcs` | | cxr | `cxr` |
| ct_brain | `ctbr` | | doppler_ll | `dopll` |
| usg_abdomen | `usab` | | mammography | `mmg` |

Plus the reuse namespaces `screen.` (whole-spine screening include-group), `mreps.`/`mrtn.`/`mrls_scr.`/`mrcs_scr.`/`uskb.` for protocol-variant packs that `extends:` or `included_findings_ref:` a base study. New studies claim new prefixes in this registry; a prefix is never reassigned to a different study even after retirement.

**Keyboard alias grammar (B-15).** Aliases are 2–6 lowercase characters (amended from the original 2–4, D-11 — shipped content already used 5–6 chars and the rule never had an enforcing importer), unique within their study, and no alias may be a strict prefix of another alias in the same study after level-stem expansion. Level-slotted stems (`db, pv, ex, cs, fn, lith, doc, cpv, uv, cfn`) expand to one concrete alias per disc level of the study's level grid — seed and validate the expansion, never the stem (C-05). Reserved globals `nad`, `urgent`, `clincorr` live in the separate `/`-macro namespace and are never shadowed by a study alias.

**Clinical review requirement at authoring time.** Any new or edited entry that touches a centrally governed element (a `sev.*` scale, its order, its management binding, or a `crit.*` registry entry) requires clinical-owner sign-off before it can leave draft status — this is not optional and is not satisfied by CI passing (see Review, below). Everything else (wording, a new tile, a synonym, a combo, a locale overlay) is practice-editable and needs only the technical review gate.

### Review

Two gates apply to every pack change, and both must pass before an import can promote content past `draft`.

1. **Technical review — automated, every PR.** CI runs the importer in `--dry-run` mode (see Import) against the changed pack set on every pull request that touches `seeds/radiology/content-packs/` (C-19). This enforces the five B-16 validations (reference integrity, alias rules, slot integrity, scale↔management binding, id_key immutability) plus the D-16 conversion-bug checks (no inlined value lists, no duplicate free-text recommendation arrays, `derived_from` text equality) and the D-06 BI-RADS dedupe rule. A PR cannot merge with a failing dry-run.
2. **Clinical review — human, gated by governance tier.** Practice-additive changes (wording, new tile, synonym, combo, locale overlay) need one radiologist content-editor approval. Centrally-governed changes (new/changed severity scale or its order, any scale↔management binding, any critical-registry entry, any blocking lint rule, any escalation behavior) additionally require the clinical owner's explicit sign-off, recorded as a reviewer approval on the PR — this is the technical enforcement of D-05's "centrally governed with clinical sign-off" rule. A MAJOR catalog version bump (key removal or semantic change) is treated as a governance event: it requires both the clinical owner and the Chief Clinical Architect (or delegate) to approve, and the reason is recorded in the changelog, mirroring the D-34/D-35-style "register is the approval" pattern used elsewhere for governance-gated changes.

**Draft-entry ingestion path.** AI-proposed candidate entries (from the quarterly frequently-typed-free-text loop, B-27) enter the catalog as `status: draft`. Drafts are invisible to Quick Select, the command line, and the AI Copilot's suggestion vocabulary (the Copilot consumes `active` entries only, B-03) until a content editor reviews and promotes them — promotion follows the same two-gate review as any hand-authored entry, plus explicit human confirmation that the AI proposal is clinically correct, not just frequently typed.

**Sign-off roles summary.**

| Role | Authorizes |
|---|---|
| Content editor (entry `owner`) | Wording, tiles, synonyms, combos, draft promotion proposal |
| Clinical owner | Any centrally governed content; draft→active promotion of clinically material entries |
| Chief Clinical Architect / product owner | MAJOR catalog version bumps (key removal/semantic change) |
| Engineering (via CI) | Every merge, via passing dry-run validation |

### Import

**What exists today vs. what is built.** `scripts/src/import-test-catalog.ts` is a one-time, idempotent importer for the **billing** tests table (186 radiology + 122 pathology `[name, price]` tuples) — it has never touched clinical catalog content and no importer for the YAML content packs exists yet (as-built). The production catalog importer is a new script, `scripts/src/import-radiology-catalog.ts` (C-01), not a literal evolution of the billing importer's code. It does inherit three operational conventions the billing importer proved out on this codebase, and diverges deliberately on a fourth:

- **Idempotency by natural-key match** — the billing importer skips existing rows by uppercased-trimmed name; the catalog importer skips/updates by `id_key` + pack `version`.
- **Batched, transaction-scoped writes** — the billing importer batches inserts of 50; the catalog importer runs the *entire* import (all shared libraries + all study packs) inside one transaction (see partial-failure behavior, below).
- **Deploy-safe re-run** — both importers are safe to run every deploy without duplicating data.
- **Collision handling diverges on purpose.** The billing importer resolves a test-code collision by bumping a numeric suffix — collisions there are expected and harmless. The catalog importer never bumps around an `id_key` collision: a duplicate or renamed key is always a hard error, because keys are immutable API (B-01) and silently reassigning one would violate it.

**Validation stages — five MUST-enforce, fail-loud checks (B-16), run identically in CI dry-run and at real import:**

1. **Reference integrity** — every `param./sev./lat./loc./meas./rec./crit./tpl.` reference in a finding resolves against the loaded shared libraries.
2. **Alias rules** — 2–6 lowercase chars, unique within study, no strict-prefix collision within study (level-slotted stems expanded first), reserved globals never shadowed.
3. **Slot integrity** — every `{slot}` in a sentence or impression fragment maps to a bound parameter, measurement, or derived value.
4. **Scale↔management binding** — a management-bound scale (`sev.birads` today; any future TI-RADS/PI-RADS-class scale) can never seed or import a category without its bound management line (B-05).
5. **id_key immutability** — an id_key that already exists in the target database with a different display meaning is a hard error; keys are retired-and-added, never renamed or reused (B-01).

Three additional conversion-bug checks run alongside the five (D-16), also fail-loud: no finding may inline a literal value list where a `loc.*`/`param.*` reference belongs; nested-format findings may not carry a free-text `recommendation` display array (only `recommendation_code`); any `rec.*` text marked `derived_from` a scale's management line must be byte-identical to that line at import time. And per D-06, the importer deduplicates the BI-RADS-5 critical trigger so it fires exactly once even though both `sev.birads`'s criticality map and the `crit.birads5` registry entry reference the same event — the scale-side mapping is the firing source, the registry entry is routing/escalation metadata only, and this pattern is mandatory for every future scale-bound critical trigger.

**Load order and two-pass resolution (D-14).** Shared libraries and their extensions merge into one logical key namespace, loaded in this order: `_shared_libraries.yaml` → `_shared_libraries_v1_1.yaml` → `_shared_libraries_v1_2.yaml` → `_screening_whole_spine.yaml` → study packs. The critical registry legitimately forward-references finding keys defined in later-loaded study packs, so the importer runs two passes: pass 1 loads every key from every pack into memory without validating cross-references; pass 2 runs the five-plus-three validations above against the fully loaded namespace. `extends:`/`include_group:`/`included_findings_ref:` packs are resolved by reference at this stage, never redefined (README rule).

**Dry-run.** The importer accepts a `--dry-run` flag that performs both passes and every validation, reports errors and the resulting version-bump classification (MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH per pack, see Version), but writes nothing to the database. Dry-run is mandatory in CI on every pack-touching PR (C-19) and is run once more, manually, immediately before a real import as a pre-flight check.

**Idempotency.** Re-running the importer against an unchanged pack set (same `pack_id`+`version` for every pack) is a no-op: zero rows written, exit success. A changed pack (`version` bumped) upserts only the entities whose defining pack changed; unaffected packs are untouched. This is checked by comparing each pack's declared `pack.version` against the version last recorded for that `pack_id` in the catalog metadata table, not by content hashing — the pack's own semver is authoritative for whether a re-import has anything to do.

**Partial-failure behavior — fail-closed, all-or-nothing.** The entire import run (shared libraries plus every study pack in the load order) executes inside a single database transaction. If any pack fails any validation stage, or the transaction itself fails partway through, the whole import rolls back and the database is left exactly as it was before the run — mirroring the fail-closed doctrine already adopted for schema deploys (`ON_ERROR_STOP=1`, D-36). A catalog import is never allowed to leave the database in a half-applied state; there is no equivalent of the old `ON_ERROR_STOP=0 || true` pattern for catalog content.

**Storage.** Import writes to new `radiology_catalog_*` tables (entries, libraries, aliases, combos, templates — C-01), created via the sanctioned Drizzle-migration path (see Chapter on database-evolution) and following the prospective-adoption identity rule for new tables: bigint identity PKs, nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id` (D-37). Until Strand H lands, `report_finding_instances.finding_id` continues to hold `radiology_quick_findings.id` values (as-built); when the catalog importer and its tables ship, `finding_id` re-points to catalog entry ids as an explicit, documented migration — never a silent semantic change to an existing column.

**Invocation.** Import is a data-load step, not a schema mutation, and does not go through the two schema-migration paths itself (Chapter — database-evolution). It runs as a deploy-time script invoked after migrations complete (after Drizzle + feature-migration Step 5), reading the pack directory from git at the deployed commit and importing in dry-run-then-commit sequence as described above. It is never invoked from client-facing API routes.

### Version

Three independent version layers apply to catalog content, and none derives from another (B-30, D-18):

| Layer | Scope | Format | Who bumps it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pack version | One YAML file (`pack.version`) | Semver | Content editor, at authoring time |
| Catalog version | The whole imported catalog | Semver, starting `1.0.0` at first pack import | Importer only, under content governance |
| Entry metadata | One catalog entry | `owner`, `version`, `review_date`, `status` | Content editor / clinical owner |

These three are also independent of the four app-wide/per-artifact namespaces from `VERSIONING_ARCHITECTURE.md` (app `version.json` semver + build number) and of `template_version` / `render_engine_version` (D-18, B-30) — a catalog PATCH release does not bump the app version, and vice versa.

**What bumps MAJOR vs. MINOR vs. PATCH**, at both the pack and the catalog-wide level (B-03, README semver rule):

- **MAJOR** — key removal or semantic change to an existing key. Treated as a governance failure/event, not a routine edit (D-02, D-18); requires the MAJOR-bump review gate above. Expected to be rare; MAJOR is "never expected" in normal operation.
- **MINOR** — additive: new findings, new studies, new parameter/severity/location/measurement/recommendation values appended to an existing ordered or unordered set, new combos, new synonyms, new aliases, a flat-to-nested format rewrite of an existing study pack (the `mri_brain` 1.0.0→1.1.0 precedent, D-12), a new shared-library extension file (additive-only, new keys, base file untouched).
- **PATCH** — wording changes, ranking/pin-order weight changes, synonym-text additions, normal-template text edits.

`catalog_version` is the canonical catalog semver string, stamped by the importer on every successful import. `'0'` on existing rows is reserved to mean "pre-catalog / unversioned" and is never a valid catalog semver (D-18) — the date-style string seen in one test (`"2026.07.09-3"`) is dead and must not be treated as a format precedent.

**Immutability of imported versions — snapshot-at-sign (C-02, D-18, D-19).** Signed reports do not chase a live version pointer. At sign, finding-instance rows for the report are copied with `report_id` set (the draft rows are retained separately until draft purge), and `catalog_version` / `template_version` / `render_engine_version` are stamped onto `patient_reports` in the same transaction that sets `final_report_id`. This means: (a) rendering an old signed report never re-resolves catalog state as of "now" — it uses the versions frozen at sign; (b) a later catalog change, including a retirement or a corrective fix-forward import, can never alter what a previously signed report displays; (c) `patient_reports.body` remains the authoritative append-only text record regardless (D-19) — the version columns document provenance, they do not drive re-rendering.

**Extends/version-pin behavior on a base bump (C-25).** A derived pack's `extends: <pack>@<version>` pin is validated at import: if the base pack has moved to a newer MINOR or PATCH, the importer warns and resolves to the newest same-MAJOR-compatible version automatically; a MAJOR mismatch is a hard import error requiring an explicit edit to the derived pack. `extends` inherits the full base finding set; `included_findings_ref` narrows to only the listed keys when both are present (as `usg_kub` does).

### Retire

`status: retired` is the only sanctioned way to remove an entry from active circulation. Retiring an entry:

- Removes it from Quick Select, the command line, and the AI Copilot's suggestion vocabulary immediately (Copilot consumes `active` entries only, B-03).
- Never removes it from resolvability: the key continues to resolve in old reports, search, and the audit trail forever (B-01). This is a hard invariant, not a best-effort — any code path that would 404 or blank-render a retired key when displaying a historical report is a bug.
- Requires the tombstone fields `retired_because` (free text, the clinical/operational reason) and, where applicable, `replaced_by` (the new key that supersedes it).
- Never frees the alias for reassignment to a different clinical meaning. If a replacement entry wants to reuse the alias, it inherits the alias only through the `replaced_by` link, never by re-registering it as if new.

**Who retires.** Retirement candidates surface from the quarterly analytics-governance loop (B-27): tiles never fired become retirement candidates. A human content editor decides; retiring anything centrally governed (a scale value, a critical-registry entry) additionally requires clinical-owner sign-off — the same gate that protects authoring of that content protects its retirement (D-05).

**What happens to reports referencing retired entries.** Nothing changes for them, by construction of the snapshot-at-sign model (C-02): a signed report's finding-instance rows and stamped `catalog_version` are frozen at sign, so a retirement made afterward cannot alter how that report renders or audits. A **draft** report open at the moment an entry retires is a different case — drafts read the live catalog, not a snapshot — so the entry disappears from that draft's Quick Select and command line going forward, but any finding text the radiologist already inserted from it stays in the draft text (report text is never mutated by a catalog change) and the underlying key still resolves if the draft needs to redisplay it. A radiologist cannot *newly* fire a retired tile in an open draft after retirement; they can still sign the draft with the already-inserted content intact.

### Delete

Hard (physical row) delete of a catalog entity is almost never allowed, and is never allowed for anything that has ever been referenced by a report. The default operation for anything that needs to go away is retire (above), not delete — this is the direct consequence of B-01 (keys are forever) and the D-19/C-02 immutability guarantees, which would be violated by deleting a key a signed report still points to.

Hard delete is permitted only in these narrow cases:

1. **Pre-merge content.** Anything still in an unmerged pull request, never imported into any environment's database, may be freely edited, renamed, or deleted in git — it isn't catalog content yet.
2. **Rejected drafts.** An AI-proposed or hand-authored entry at `status: draft` that a content editor rejects during review, provided it was never promoted to `active` (and therefore was never visible to Quick Select, search, or the Copilot, and cannot have been referenced by any report). Once an entry has ever been `active`, even briefly, it must be retired, not deleted, on the chance a report already references it.
