3. **Practice-level overlay content only.** A practice's own additive tiles, combos, aliases, or wording overlays — never shared-library or centrally governed content — may be hard-deleted by the practice, and only after a check (a query against `report_finding_instances`/finding references) confirms no report in that practice has ever used it. If any reference exists, the overlay is retired instead.

Everything else — any key that ever reached `active` in the shared or a study-pack namespace, any centrally governed content, anything referenced by even one report — is retire-only, permanently.

### Rollback

Reverting a bad import or a bad version is a **fix-forward** operation, never a database rollback of catalog rows. Two safety properties make this both sufficient and necessary:

- The import transaction is all-or-nothing (see Import): a failed import — one that trips a validation — never partially commits, so most "bad imports" are caught before they ever touch the database and require no rollback procedure at all.
- Signed reports are immutable snapshots (C-02, D-19): a bad import that *did* commit, and even one that was subsequently used to sign real reports before the mistake was noticed, cannot retroactively corrupt those already-signed reports — their `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` and copied finding-instance rows are frozen. This is what makes fix-forward safe: correcting the catalog going forward never rewrites the past.

**Operational procedure for a bad import that committed (validations passed, content is clinically or operationally wrong):**

1. **Detect.** CI dry-run should have caught structural problems; a wrong-but-structurally-valid import (e.g. incorrect management wording, a mis-bound recommendation) is caught by clinical review escaping notice, by the quarterly safety review (D §16.5 pattern: every addendum caused by a missed lintable/content error triggers a rule-gap review), or by a radiologist report.
2. **Prepare the correction in git**, not in the database — either `git revert` the offending pack commit or author a new patch. A correction to a MINOR/PATCH-level mistake (wrong wording, wrong rank, wrong synonym) is itself a PATCH or MINOR bump of the affected pack. A correction that requires actually removing or renaming a key is a MAJOR governance event and goes through the MAJOR-bump review gate (Review, above), because "undo the mistake" is not an exemption from key immutability — the corrected content enters under a new key, the mistaken key is retired with `retired_because` recorded.
3. **Dry-run the correction** exactly as any normal pack change (CI + manual pre-flight dry-run).
4. **Import for real**, producing a new catalog PATCH/MINOR/MAJOR version as classified in step 2. This is the only DB-facing action; no row is deleted or reverted in place.
5. **Verify** with a smoke check that the corrected tile/finding/recommendation fires and renders as intended.
6. **Record the incident**: what broke, why the review/validation gates didn't catch it, and what new check (importer validation, review-checklist item) closes that gap — mirroring the master spec's rule-gap-review discipline.

**Data guarantees during and after rollback:**

- No signed report is ever affected by a catalog rollback/fix-forward, by construction of snapshot-at-sign.
- Open drafts see the corrected content the next time they load Quick Select/command-line data; any already-inserted (now-stale) text in the draft is left exactly as the radiologist typed or accepted it — a fix-forward import never reaches into draft or signed report text.
- The catalog's own DB rows are never mass-reverted, restored from a prior backup, or `DROP`ped to "undo" an import — this would violate id_key immutability the instant any report (draft or signed) has come to depend on the interim state, and it contradicts the platform-wide forward-only-migrations doctrine that already governs schema changes on this box. If a catastrophic import somehow bypassed the all-or-nothing transaction (a bug in the importer itself), the correct response is to fix the importer bug, then fix-forward the content — never a manual `DELETE`/`pg_restore` against the catalog tables in production.

### Acceptance criteria

- Every catalog entity (finding, param/sev/lat/loc/meas/rec/crit entry, combo, normal template) carries `owner`, `version`, `review_date`, and `status ∈ {draft, active, retired}`.
- The importer runs in `--dry-run` mode with zero DB writes and reports pass/fail for all eight validations (five B-16 + three D-16 conversion-bug checks) plus the D-06 dedupe rule.
- CI blocks merge of any pack-touching PR whose dry-run fails.
- Centrally-governed content (severity scale/order, scale↔management binding, critical registry, blocking lint rules, escalation behavior) cannot merge without a recorded clinical-owner approval; MAJOR bumps additionally require Chief Clinical Architect / product-owner approval.
- Re-running the importer against an unchanged pack set writes zero rows.
- A validation failure anywhere in a run leaves the database in its pre-run state (no partial commit).
- `catalog_version` on a fresh import is a valid `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` string starting at `1.0.0`; `'0'` never appears as a value written by the importer.
- Signing a report stamps `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` and copies finding-instance rows in the same transaction as `final_report_id`; no later catalog change alters an already-signed report's rendering.
- Retiring an entry removes it from Quick Select/command-line/Copilot vocabulary but the key still resolves when rendering any historical report or audit record.
- No entity that has ever reached `active` status, and no entity ever referenced by any report, is ever physically deleted from the database.
- Every rollback of bad catalog content is executed as a new git commit + re-import producing a new catalog version; no production incident is resolved by direct row deletion or table restore against the catalog tables.

### Build order

1. `radiology_catalog_*` schema (entries, libraries, aliases, combos, templates) via the sanctioned Drizzle-migration path, bigint identity PKs, nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id` — depends on the database-evolution chapter's migration conventions.
2. Importer core: two-pass loader/resolver honoring the D-14 load order, plus the eight fail-loud validations and `--dry-run`.
3. CI wiring: run importer `--dry-run` on every PR touching `seeds/radiology/content-packs/`.
4. Deploy-time import invocation: idempotent, transactional, fail-closed, run after migrations complete.
5. Nine-flat-pack → nested-`ai:` harmonization (D-12) and the study-block key-naming fixes (D-15), each landing as the corresponding MINOR pack bump.
6. Sign-time stamping of `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` plus finding-instance snapshot-copy on finalize — depends on the finding-lifecycle chapter's sign/finalize state machine (Chapter 2) and the report-authoritative-store rules (D-19).
7. Retire/status tooling and the quarterly governance loop (candidate surfacing, clinical sign-off UI) — depends on the analytics chapter for the underlying telemetry (tile-fire counts, free-text repetition, recommendation-deletion counts).
8. Rollback runbook and any drift/quality monitoring that helps detect a bad-but-valid import sooner than the next clinical report.

Steps 1–4 must land before any content pack can be imported into a real environment; step 6 must land before `catalog_version` stamping can be relied on by any other chapter's acceptance criteria.

---

## Chapter 2 — Finding lifecycle

A finding is the atomic reportable unit: one clinical observation, bound to a study, carrying a rendered sentence plus its structured slot values. This chapter follows one finding instance from the gesture that creates it through to the analytics event its transitions emit. The physical home of a structured finding is the `report_finding_instances` table (B-22): it is the single source of truth, and everything else — the draft's `structured_json` cache, the rendered report prose, the signed snapshot — is derived from it or frozen from it. This chapter owns the finding-instance state machine, the critical-escalation contract (B-07), and consent-gated completeness fill (B-17); it defers structured slot *content* (parameter/severity/laterality/location/measurement value sets) to Chapter 3 (parameter-lifecycle), recommendation binding to Chapter 4 (recommendation-lifecycle), impression assembly ordering to the impression-lifecycle chapter, and the AI ghost/margin-card/gutter-mark mechanics themselves to the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter — this chapter states only how those mechanics touch a finding instance's state.

**Canonical states, exhaustive.** A finding instance occupies exactly one of five states at any moment; there is no sixth. "Rendered" and "Analytics" from the mandated scope are not separate states — Rendered is a continuously-recomputed *property* of an instance (is its text currently present in the report's text state), and Analytics is a side-channel of events emitted at every transition below, not a resting place.

| State | Meaning | Entered by / trigger | Who or what triggers it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Draft-Unconfirmed** | Row exists in `report_finding_instances` (`draft_id` set, `report_id` null, `confirmed=false` — the column's DB default, applied uniformly regardless of `source`); its text may or may not currently be present in the report canvas. | Tile fired, command-line insertion, macro/combo fired, or an AI suggestion accepted via `Tab`/`Enter`/voice "accept" (`source: "ai"`) — every entry mechanism creates the row with `confirmed=false`; none is confirmed at creation. | Radiologist gesture (all four entry mechanisms funnel through one gesture requirement, P6/B-08: nothing is inserted without an explicit act). |
| **Draft-Confirmed** | Same row, `confirmed=true`. | Radiologist edits the rendered sentence (breaks exact-match, C-12) — the only mechanism wired today, and it applies to any `source` — or, once Strand M (`ff_radiology_ai_assist`) wires the confirmation UI, an explicit confirm gesture. Until Strand M ships, an untouched non-AI instance simply remains `confirmed=false` indefinitely; this is harmless because the sign gate's untouched-provisional block (B-09) applies only to `source: "ai"` spans. | Radiologist edit or explicit confirm; never elapsed time or scroll/dwell (C-12). |
| **Removed** | Row is physically deleted; not a soft-delete flag. | Radiologist deselects a Quick Select tile, deletes/overwrites the inserted text, or otherwise excludes the finding from the next save. | Radiologist gesture, executed at the *next* save-draft dual-write cycle (replace-not-append, see Structured). |
| **Signed** | A snapshot copy of the row exists with `report_id` set, frozen permanently; the original draft-scoped row is retained separately until draft purge. | Sign gate passes (`Ctrl+Enter` or voice "sign") and the finalize transaction commits. | The signing radiologist; never AI (B-25). |
| **Amended** | A correction recorded through `report_amendments` after sign; the original Signed snapshot rows are never rewritten in place. | An authorized amendment action on an already-signed report. | Radiologist with `canFinalize`/`canApprove` permission (D-33); never a typist. |

Transitions are one-directional except Draft-Unconfirmed ⇄ Draft-Confirmed (an edit can occur any number of times before sign) and Signed → Amended (repeatable, D-20). There is no path from Removed, Signed, or Amended back to Draft-Unconfirmed: a removed finding that the radiologist re-adds is a *new* instance, not a resurrected one, and a signed instance is never un-signed.

### Draft

A finding instance is born from exactly one of four entry mechanisms, all of which converge on the same client-side state and the same save-draft payload:

1. **Quick Select tile.** Firing a tile inserts the finding text and its impression fragment at the insertion point in one gesture; parameters are then tab-through editable inline. Client state: `selectedQuickIds: Set<number>` plus `quickInstances: Map<number, AbnormalityInstance>`, mutated by the pure helpers `toggleQuickSelection`, `setQuickInstance`, `deleteQuickInstance`, `patchQuickInstance`, and `seedQuickInstance(side)` (which seeds a fresh instance from the global side selector). `source: "quickselect"`.
2. **Command line** (`/` in the editor, `Ctrl+K` anywhere). Unifies tiles, macros, templates, AI actions, measurement fields, and priors behind one frequency-ranked search; selecting a finding through it fires the same tile-insertion path as (1) — the command line is an access unification, not a separate storage or state mechanism (UX review §3.1).
3. **Macro** (`/`-namespace text expansion, `radiology_text_macros` / `radiology_smart_macros`, D-25). A macro can expand into finding text; if the macro is measurement-aware (`is_measurement_macro`) it participates in the same structured-instance creation as a tile fire.
4. **AI suggestion accepted.** Ghost text `Tab`-accepted, a margin-card `Insert`, or voice "accept" creates the instance with `source: "ai"`. Before acceptance, nothing exists in `report_finding_instances` — the proposal lives entirely in the Copilot's ephemeral suggestion surface (ghost text / margin card), which is ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter territory; this chapter's Draft state begins only at the accept gesture.

**Client-side state model (as-built).** Every abnormality/finding on the client is an `AbnormalityInstance` — the frozen code type for the interim structured slot shape (see Structured, below). `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx` owns the React state (`useState`/`useRef`, including `insertedTextRef`); all *decision* logic is extracted into pure, UI-independent helpers re-exported through `renderEngine.ts` (Strand F, Tickets F1a/F1b) so it can be unit-tested without a DOM: `abnormalityEngine` (`fillTemplate`, `renderAbnormality`, `parseProperties`, `EMPTY_INSTANCE`), `sideSwap` (`applySide`, `swapSides`, `hasSideWords`), and `quickFindingsMerge` (`mergeBlock`/`removeBlock`/`mergeImpression`/`removeImpression`). `renderEngine.ts` exports `RENDER_ENGINE_VERSION = "1.0.0"`, named to mirror `patient_reports.render_engine_version` (no stamping is wired yet — see Signed).

The state-transition entry point is `applyRenderedTransition(state, prev, next)`: a pure reducer that exact-removes whatever text was previously rendered for the instance, then dedupe-merges the newly rendered text. This is what makes **manual-edit protection** work: once a radiologist edits a generated sentence, it no longer matches exactly, so a later removal (deselect, or a parameter change re-render) skips it and the radiologist's wording is never silently overwritten. This same exact-match discipline is why editing a sentence is what moves an instance from Draft-Unconfirmed to Draft-Confirmed (C-12) — the render engine and the confirmation model share one mechanism.

**Regression discipline (Bug-001, binding convention).** A local `function removeImpression(index)` once shadowed the imported pure `removeImpression(lines, line)` inside the workspace component, silently breaking Quick Select deselect (an instance would toggle off in the UI but its impression bullet would never leave the text). The fix set two permanent rules any engineer extending this surface must follow: (a) `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx` must never declare a local function whose name collides with a `renderEngine` export — the by-index deletion handler is permanently named `deleteImpressionLineAt`; (b) `renderEngine.ts` imports pure functions under aliased names (e.g. `_removeImpression`) specifically to make a future collision impossible to introduce by accident. This is regression-guarded by source-text structural tests (`removeImpressionShadowing.test.ts`), because the component itself cannot be unit-tested (the root vitest config cannot resolve `@/` aliases) — this is *why* the pure-logic extraction into alias-free lib files exists at all, and it is why new render-affecting logic belongs in `renderEngine.ts`/its siblings, not inline in the workspace component.

### Structured

**Slot representation, as-built vs. target.** Today, every finding instance's structured payload is the fixed `AbnormalityInstance` shape: `side` (`Side | ""`), `severity` (`"" | "mild" | "moderate" | "severe"`), `chronicity` (`"" | "acute" | "chronic"`), `level` (string, e.g. `"L4-L5"`), `value` (string, a measurement). Which chips a given tile exposes is controlled by the comma-list `properties` column on `radiology_quick_findings`. Template placeholders `{side} {severity} {chronicity} {level} {value}` render through `fillTemplate`; an empty property strips its placeholder together with its grammatical chunk (`"at {level}"`, `"on the {side} side"`, `"measuring {value} mm"`), and legacy templates lacking `{side}` instead get whole-word left/right/bilateral substitution. This fixed five-field set is deliberately narrower than the catalog's rich shared-library model (`param.*`/`sev.*`/`lat.*`/`loc.*`/`meas.*`, Chapter 3) — it is the interim structured representation until the catalog importer (Chapter 1, C-01/Strand H) lands and `report_finding_instances.finding_id` re-points from `radiology_quick_findings.id` to a real catalog entry id. Do not build new features against the assumption that `structured_json` already carries the rich parameter model: as written today by the dual-write, `category`, `modality`, `anatomic_zone_id`, and `structure_id` are left at their defaults (`''`/`null`) and `catalog_version` is `'0'` ("pre-catalog/unversioned", D-18) unless a real import has run.

**Persistence mechanics.** `report_finding_instances` columns beyond the slot payload: `finding_id` (NOT NULL — every instance references a catalog/quick-finding entry), `draft_id`/`report_id` (mutually exclusive across the Draft/Signed boundary), `category`/`modality` (text, default `''`), `structured_json` (jsonb, the `AbnormalityInstance` params blob), `catalog_version` (text, default `'0'`), `source` (`manual | quickselect | voice | ai | ohif | weasis | dicom_sr`), `confirmed`/`confirmed_by`/`confirmed_at` (the landing spot for the Strand M human-confirmation gate — not yet set by any code path today). Writing happens through the save-draft dual-write (Ticket A3.2): the payload's `findings[]` array is written **replace-not-append** — every existing row for the draft is deleted, then the current set is inserted, inside one transaction — gated on `rest.findings !== undefined`, never on length. This distinction is load-bearing: an old client that omits the `findings` key must leave existing rows untouched, while a current client explicitly sending `findings: []` (deselect-all) must clear every row. Dual-write failure is caught and logged only; it must never turn an already-successful draft save into a failed response.

The draft's `radiology_report_drafts.structured_json` is a **denormalized cache**, never a source of truth, regenerated from `report_finding_instances` (ordered by `id ASC`) by `regenerateDraftStructuredJson` (Ticket A4). Zero findings produces a cache value of `null`, never `[]` — "no structured findings" has exactly one representation. A read-only drift detector (Ticket A5) compares cache against source-of-truth rows (`in_sync | missing_cache | stale_cache | empty_findings_stale_cache`; deep-equality ignoring jsonb key order but respecting array order, since finding order is meaningful) and never writes; repair is an explicit, separately-invoked admin action (C-15: re-run the A4 regeneration for the drifted draft), never automatic and never on a cron.

**Completeness.** Two completeness mechanisms attach to a finding instance's structured state. First, per-organ/per-region checklist completeness: a study whose report *is* the organ checklist (e.g. USG Whole Abdomen) requires every region addressed or explicitly limitation-lined; a limitation finding (`impression_fragment: null`, `normal_variant: true`) satisfies the checklist without contributing to the impression. Second, per-measurement completeness: a finding that implies a measurement (e.g. prostatomegaly implying PVR, three diameters entered implying ABC/2) raises a non-blocking gutter mark until the measurement is captured. Both surface as the ◌ (completeness) glyph — full glyph semantics and dismissal mechanics are ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter territory (B-11) — but the finding-instance-level rule owned here is binding and simple: **a `normal_variant: true` instance never raises a ◌ mark**, and consent-gated normal-line fill (below, under Rendered) is the only way an unaddressed region resolves itself without the radiologist writing a sentence.

**Criticality and the escalation contract (B-07, owning chapter).** Every finding entry carries a `criticality` tag: `none`, `significant`, `{critical_if, registry: crit.<key>}`, or `{significant_if}` (no registry). A `crit.*`-linked instance is the trigger for the critical-results workflow: it pre-fills the templated recommendation `rec.urgent` (specialist + communication timestamp slots), surfaces a margin priority card that never covers report text, and one keystroke launches the escalation workflow — referrer contact surfaced from the doctors table, communication sentence auto-documented, timestamps recorded, an identity-band chip persisting until closed (C-22). Hard measurement-triggered criticals fire deterministically and can never depend on model behavior (C-04): midline shift ≥5 mm (`meas.midline_shift.critical_at: ">= 5"` → `crit.midline_shift`) is the canonical example, and BI-RADS 5 fires from the scale-side mapping on `sev.birads`, never redundantly from the registry entry (D-06 — the registry entry is routing/escalation metadata only, and the importer deduplicates so the trigger fires exactly once). **The sign gate blocks while escalation is unstarted for a written critical finding** (D-20/B-07) — this is enforced per-instance: an instance whose `criticality` resolves to a `crit.*` key and whose report text contains the matching pattern cannot coexist with a report in Signed state unless the escalation workflow shows a started/documented communication. Declining escalation requires a reason, itself audit-trailed. The scorecard target for this contract is a median written→communicated interval under 5 minutes (§4.6), tracked by the analytics events in the Analytics section below. `significant`/`significant_if` (C-13) is deliberately weaker: it affects impression ordering (worst-first tier 2, owned by the impression-lifecycle chapter) and sets a worklist significance flag, but triggers no escalation and no sign-off block — `significant` and `critical` are not points on one severity continuum, they are two different consequences, and an implementer must not conflate them.

**Measurement values entering an instance.** Whether extracted by AI or typed/dictated, a measurement value never auto-inserts into a finding instance's `value`/`meas.*` slot at any confidence level (D-21, binding) — insertion is always an explicit radiologist gesture (per-row Insert, "Insert All", `Tab` on a ghost). Confidence gates presentation only: high/medium-confidence extractions render as insertable rows in the Measurement Assistant; low-confidence renders flagged and requires field-level review before it is even insertable. Full measurement mechanics belong to the measurement-lifecycle chapter; the rule stated here — no confidence auto-inserts into a finding instance — is the one invariant that chapter cannot weaken.

### Rendered

Rendering is not a stored state; it is a deterministic, continuously-recomputed function from the set of Draft-state finding instances to the four report text sections. `renderAbnormality`/`fillTemplate` (abnormalityEngine, re-exported byte-for-byte through `renderEngine.ts`) turn one instance into a `RenderedAbnormality { finding, impression, technique, recommendation }`; the report's live text is `ReportTextState { rawFindings: string, impression: string[], technique: string, recommendation: string }` — impression is modeled as an array of bullet strings, the other three as flat strings. Every instance update (a parameter edited, a tile deselected, a fresh instance selected) runs through `applyRenderedTransition`'s remove-then-merge cycle: exact-remove what this instance previously contributed, then merge in what it now renders to. This is why manual edits are safe (see Draft) and why an instance's row and its on-canvas text can transiently disagree — the row is the source of truth, the canvas is a projection recomputed on every relevant change, and only the next save-draft cycle reconciles the row set to whatever is currently selected.

**Ordering rules.** Two independent ordering rules apply, at two different scopes, and they must not be confused:
- **Section placement** — which of the four render sections (and, within Findings, which organ/region sub-section) an instance's sentence lands in is fixed by the finding's structural binding (its study section / organ, e.g. USG Whole Abdomen's organ-by-organ sections), not by firing order. A study whose `default_report_sections` is an organ list renders each organ's findings into that organ's slot regardless of the order tiles were fired.
- **Within-section order** and **impression order** are different rules. Within a Findings sub-section, sentences append at the natural insertion point (cursor location) in firing order. The Impression is not append-ordered at all: it assembles from each instance's `impression_fragment`, ordered **worst-first** (critical > significant > rest), grouping multi-level/multi-organ disease into single lines (B-14) — full assembly mechanics are the impression-lifecycle chapter's territory; the finding-lifecycle contribution is that every instance's `criticality` tag is the sort key that chapter consumes.

**Normal-statement interaction, shadowing, and suppression.** Every study seeds exactly one `*.normal_study` finding whose `default_sentence` is `{{tpl.normal.<study>}}` (the study's one-keystroke full-normal template, `normal_variant: true`). This instance carries a hard, non-negotiable block rule: **a normal-study impression must never coexist with any positive finding** — if any other, non-`normal_variant` instance is Draft-state (present) in the same report, the normal-study line is internally false and is a ✕-blocking contradiction at the sign gate, not merely a stylistic issue. The renderer does not silently drop the normal line on the radiologist's behalf (that would be an act, forbidden by P6/offer-never-act) — it is surfaced as a blocking lint mark that the radiologist must resolve before signing, by removing either the normal-study instance or the conflicting positive finding.

Separately, **per-region normal-line completeness fill is consent-gated** (B-17, owning chapter): for a study whose report is organized by region, the completeness engine can fill unaddressed regions with their standard normal line, but only **on explicit radiologist confirmation** — never silently, never as a default, never triggered by dwell time or navigation. `/nad` (the global macro, resolving to "No significant abnormality detected.") is the manual equivalent of the same act performed one region at a time. This is the mechanism by which "shadowing/suppression of normals" is implemented safely: a normal line is either explicitly confirmed (fill) or explicitly written by the radiologist — it is never inferred and inserted without a gesture, mirroring the same offer-never-act discipline that governs AI suggestions.

Finally, the Bug-001 discipline from Draft applies here too: because rendering is remove-then-merge over exact-match text, any future change to the merge/removal pure functions must preserve byte-for-byte behavior of the re-exported `abnormalityEngine`/`quickFindingsMerge` functions, or normal-statement suppression and manual-edit protection both silently regress together.

### Signed

Signing (verb: **Sign**, `Ctrl+Enter` or voice "sign" — both hit the identical gate) is the act that freezes a finding instance. Two things happen to structured findings in the same transaction that sets `final_report_id`:

1. **Snapshot copy.** Finding-instance rows belonging to the draft are copied with `report_id` set (C-02). The original draft-scoped rows are retained separately until draft purge — copy, not move, so a draft that is somehow reopened after a promotion does not silently lose its working rows.
2. **Version stamping.** `catalog_version`, `template_version`, and `render_engine_version` are stamped onto the parent `patient_reports` row (D-18). These three are independent namespaces from each other and from the app's own `version.json` scheme (B-30) — a catalog PATCH release never bumps `render_engine_version`, and vice versa.

**Snapshot semantics vs. catalog references — this is the load-bearing guarantee of this section.** A signed finding instance does not chase a live pointer into the catalog or the shared libraries. It is a frozen copy: whatever `finding_id` resolution scheme, parameter values, and rendered text existed at the moment of sign are exactly what that instance shows forever after, regardless of later catalog changes, retirements, or corrective fix-forward imports (Chapter 1, Rollback). Concretely: (a) rendering an already-signed report never re-resolves "what does this key mean *now*" — it uses the frozen `structured_json` and the stamped versions; (b) a catalog entry retiring, or even a MAJOR governance-event key change, cannot alter what a previously signed report displays; (c) `patient_reports.body` remains the authoritative append-only text record regardless of any of this (D-19) — the version columns and the snapshot rows document *provenance*, they never drive re-rendering of already-signed text.

**Sign gate.** The gate is deliberately small and strict (D-20, §13.2): it blocks only on ✕ contradictions, unmet mandatory fields (e.g. a BI-RADS category inserted without its bound management line), unreviewed provisional spans, and unstarted escalations for a written critical finding. Per finding instance, this means: no instance may remain Draft-Unconfirmed with `source: "ai"` (an untouched AI span blocks sign structurally, B-09); no `crit.*`-linked instance may have unstarted escalation (B-07, above); and the normal-study contradiction described in Rendered is itself a ✕. Green path (no unresolved critical items) signs instantly and runs the PDF/PACS/notify pipeline in the background, serving the next pre-staged study; the gated path renders the unresolved items as a keyboard-navigable jump-list, never a modal wall.

**Authorship and RBAC.** Authorship is server-derived from the authenticated staff session at finalize (`subjectName`); a client-supplied author string is never trusted (D-33). `canFinalize`/`canApprove` permission bits are enforced on every finalize/amend route — a `radiology_typist` role can never mutate a signed report, structurally, not just by convention. AI never signs, under any circumstance (B-25) — there is no code path, flag, or confidence threshold that authorizes an automatic sign.

**Amendment.** A signed report can be corrected only through `report_amendments` — one chain, repeatable (D-20). The original Signed snapshot rows in `report_finding_instances` are never rewritten in place; an amendment that changes structured findings is a new recorded delta linked to the amendment, not an `UPDATE` against the frozen rows. (The structured-data write path for amendments is not yet built as of this writing: `report_amendments` today records the amended text and metadata, but no code path writes a corresponding delta row against `report_finding_instances`; until that ships, an amendment that changes structured findings must be tracked as prose in `report_amendments` only, and this is the chapter's second-highest-priority open build item after sign-time version stamping — see Build order, step 6.)

### Archived

There is no separate archive store for structured findings; "archived" names the retention regime that begins the instant a finding instance's snapshot row is written with `report_id` set, not a physical migration to different storage. Two retention rules apply, and they differ sharply by which side of the sign boundary a row sits on:

- **Signed snapshot rows (`report_id` set) are retained permanently.** This follows directly from the Chapter 1 delete doctrine extended to this table: once a row is referenced by a signed report, it can never be hard-deleted, full stop — there is no purge job, retention-expiry sweep, or storage-reclamation path that may ever target a row with `report_id` set. This matches the medico-legal retention floor already adopted for the AI audit trail (≥7 years, C-11) and the general rule that `patient_reports.body` is the permanent, append-only authoritative record (D-19): a report that is never deleted cannot have its structured findings deleted out from under it either.
- **Draft-scoped rows (`draft_id` set, `report_id` null) are retained only until draft purge.** A draft that is abandoned, superseded, or promoted has its working rows eligible for cleanup by the database-evolution chapter's draft-lifecycle policy; that policy is out of this chapter's scope, but the row-level rule owned here is that purge logic must never touch a row that has ever had `report_id` set, even transiently.

**Storage evolution, not a retention change.** `report_finding_instances` ships non-partitioned (a simple bigserial PK); partitioning by `created_at` is a later, separate, sentinel-gated migration (Strand N, `ff_radiology_scale_partition`) that exists purely to keep the table performant at scale, and it must never be bundled with any other migration (B-23). Partitioning must preserve every retention guarantee above exactly — it is a physical storage change, never a semantic one.

### Analytics

Every state transition in the table at the top of this chapter emits an event; these are the raw material the quarterly content-governance loop (B-27, owned by the analytics chapter) consumes, and they are the finding-lifecycle chapter's full contribution to that store. An implementer building the emitter should treat this table as the event contract:

| Event | Fires on transition | Key dimensions |
|---|---|---|
| `finding.fired` | (creation) → Draft-Unconfirmed | `finding_id`, study/modality, `source`, `draft_id`, actor, timestamp |
| `finding.confirmed` | Draft-Unconfirmed → Draft-Confirmed | `finding_id`, prior `source`, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at` |
| `finding.removed` | Draft-Unconfirmed/Confirmed → Removed | `finding_id`, `draft_id`, actor, timestamp |
| `finding.signed` | Draft-Confirmed → Signed | `finding_id`, `report_id`, `catalog_version`, `template_version`, `render_engine_version`, criticality tier |
| `finding.escalation_started` / `finding.escalation_completed` | (sub-event of a `critical`-tagged instance in Draft or Signed state) | `crit.*` key, specialist, communication timestamp, declined-with-reason flag |
| `finding.amended` | Signed → Amended | `finding_id`, amendment id, actor, permission bits checked |

Dimensions common to every event: the finding's catalog/quick-finding key (never a display label — keys are the stable join dimension, per B-01), study and modality, and the actor's staff identity (server-derived, per D-33 — never a client-supplied string). These events are what feed the quarterly loop's two finding-lifecycle-relevant nominations: never-fired tiles become retirement candidates, and the critical-escalation events above are the source data for the median written→communicated scorecard target (§4.6, <5 minutes). Wording-review nominations (frequently deleted recommendations) and free-text-repetition nominations (draft-tile candidates) are sourced from recommendation-lifecycle and catalog-lifecycle events respectively, not from this table — this chapter emits finding-instance transitions only. As-built, the closest existing surface is `radiology_user_item_usage_logs` (tile usage/recents); it predates this event contract and should be treated as the migration source, not the target shape, when the analytics chapter's ingestion pipeline is built.

### Acceptance criteria

- Every finding instance is created via exactly one of the four entry mechanisms (tile, command line, macro, AI-accept) and carries the corresponding `source` value; there is no fifth creation path.
- `report_finding_instances` remains the sole source of truth; `radiology_report_drafts.structured_json` is regenerated only, is never hand-written, and is `null` (never `[]`) when a draft has zero findings.
- The save-draft dual-write is replace-not-append and is gated on `findings !== undefined`, not on length: `findings: []` clears all rows for the draft; an absent `findings` key leaves existing rows untouched.
- No instance with `source: "ai"` can be part of a report that reaches Signed while still Draft-Unconfirmed (untouched-provisional); this is enforced structurally by the sign gate, not by convention.
- No instance whose `criticality` resolves to a `crit.*` key can be part of a Signed report while its escalation is unstarted.
- Signing copies finding-instance rows with `report_id` set and stamps `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` on `patient_reports` in the same transaction as `final_report_id`; no code path re-resolves live catalog state to render an already-signed report.
- No finding-instance row with `report_id` set is ever hard-deleted, by any purge, cleanup, or reclamation job, under any circumstance.
- A `normal_variant: true` (`*.normal_study`) instance never coexists with a positive finding in a Signed report; the conflict is a ✕-blocking contradiction, never a silent auto-removal.
- Deselecting/removing a finding before sign deletes its row at the next save-draft cycle; editing a rendered sentence protects it from exact-match auto-removal on the next re-render.
- Every transition in the canonical state table emits its corresponding analytics event with the dimensions specified.

### Build order

1. Client entry mechanisms (tile, command line, macro, AI-accept) feeding one `AbnormalityInstance` state model, plus the `renderEngine.ts` pure-helper facade (F1a) and the `applyRenderedTransition` remove-then-merge reducer (F1b) — largely built as-built; new work here must preserve the Bug-001 no-shadowing convention and the byte-for-byte re-export guarantee.
2. Dual-write of `findings[]` into `report_finding_instances` behind `ff_radiology_structured_core`, replace-not-append, gated on `findings !== undefined` — built (A3.x); extend to wire `confirmed`/`confirmed_by`/`confirmed_at` for the Strand M human-confirmation gate.
3. Structured completeness (organ-checklist, measurement-implied) and the criticality/escalation contract (B-07) — depends on Chapter 1 (catalog import, for the richer parameter model) and on the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter for the ◌/✕/△ glyph mechanics this chapter only anchors.
4. Rendered ordering and normal-statement suppression — depends on the impression-lifecycle chapter's worst-first assembly rule and on Chapter 1's `tpl.normal.*` template keys.
5. Sign-time snapshot copy plus `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` stamping in the same transaction as `final_report_id` — depends on Chapter 1's Version section (catalog semver) and on the D-19 report-authoritative-store rules; this is currently unwired (`RENDER_ENGINE_VERSION` exists client-side but nothing stamps it) and is this chapter's single highest-priority open build item.
6. Amendment structured-data write path: a new recorded delta against `report_finding_instances`, linked to its `report_amendments` row, so a structured-finding amendment is queryable the same way a signed finding is, instead of living only as amendment prose — depends on step 5 (amendments reuse the same version-stamping transaction shape) and is this chapter's second-highest-priority open build item.
7. Archive/retention guarantees (no-delete-if-`report_id`-set, partitioning as a pure storage change) — depends on the database-evolution chapter's migration conventions and Strand N's sentinel-gated partitioning ticket.
8. Analytics event emission — depends on the analytics chapter's ingestion/store design; the event table in this chapter is the contract that design must satisfy for finding-lifecycle events specifically.

Steps 1–2 must land before any other step is meaningful (there is no finding instance to transition without them); step 5 is the current gap between "structured findings are captured" and "signed reports are provably reproducible," and should be prioritized accordingly.

---

## Chapter 3 — Parameter lifecycle

`param.*` is the shared library of clinical slot-value sets that every finding's `{parameter}` placeholders bind to: size/count/course/change/margin/enhancement, modality-specific descriptors (echotexture, CT density, MR signal), and the ordered clinical ladders (`cord_ladder`, `calc_morphology`, tear/cartilage grades) that read almost like severity scales but are not `sev.*`. This chapter states, end to end, how a `param.*` entry is authored, why a study pack is structurally forbidden from ever defining its own, how a fired tile's parameter slots get a value at all (default pre-fill vs. practice/locale overlay vs. the radiologist's own keystroke), and how the whole chain can keep changing forever without ever altering a report that has already been signed. It is the owning chapter for `param.*` content, the parameter-value resolution order, and the practice/locale override mechanism used by parameter and finding wording alike; it is not the owning chapter for the shared severity ladders themselves (`sev.*` structural rules and scale↔management binding — Chapter 4, recommendation-lifecycle), for the six-element finding anatomy and criticality tag (Chapter 2, finding-lifecycle), for `meas.*` field definitions and auto-calculations (measurement-lifecycle), or for catalog-wide import/version/retire mechanics (Chapter 1, catalog-lifecycle), which this chapter invokes by decision ID rather than restating.

### Shared parameter library

**What lives here.** `param.*` is one of the eight shared-library families (alongside `sev.*`, `lat.*`, `loc.*`, `meas.*`, `rec.*`, `crit.*`, `tpl.normal.*`) defined once and bound by findings by key, never copied (B-02). Every `param.*` entry has exactly one of two types: `measurement` (a size/count-style field with `units`, `dims` 1–3, and a `render` format string, e.g. `param.size`: `"~{v} mm / {a} x {b} cm"`) or `choice` (a fixed, named value list). Choice entries may additionally carry `ordered: true`, in which case list position is load-bearing for lint and impression assembly and the set may only ever be extended by appending (B-04) — `param.cord_ladder`, `param.calc_morphology` (`benign_cutoff_index: 4`, 0-based per C-06), `param.tear_grade`, and `param.cartilage_grade` are the shipped examples. A choice entry may also carry `derive_from`/feed a `sev.*` scale's `derive_from` (`param.cartilage_grade` → `sev.cartilage`); when it does, the parameter entry is functionally part of a severity chain even though it lives in the `param.` namespace.

**Physical location.** All `param.*` entries live in exactly three files, loaded in the fixed order established by D-14: `_shared_libraries.yaml` (v1.0.0, the base set — universal descriptors, spine/Doppler/mammo/USG&ICU highlights), `_shared_libraries_v1_1.yaml` (v1.1.0, additive — MSK and neuro/spine-screening params: `tear_grade`, `meniscus_pattern`, `cartilage_grade`, `tendinosis`, `effusion_joint`, `marrow_edema`, `enhancement_pattern`, `tb_activity`, `scoliosis_dir`, `aneurysm_shape`), and `_shared_libraries_v1_2.yaml` (v1.2.0, additive — chest/Doppler/mammography params: `lung_distribution`, `nodule_character`, `bronchiectasis_type`, `tube_position`, `waveform`, `thrombus_age`, `calc_morphology`, `calc_distribution`, `mass_shape`). All three merge into one logical `param.*` namespace at import (D-14); a study pack references any of them interchangeably regardless of which file defined the key.

**Who may add a `param.*` entry, and under what sign-off.** A content editor (the entry's `owner`) authors new keys or appends values to an existing ordered list inside a new shared-library extension file (never inside a study pack — see Study-specific, below) or a new version of an existing extension file; the base file (`_shared_libraries.yaml`) and every already-shipped extension file are never edited in place once active (additive-only, D-15.6/B-04). Two review tiers apply, both mandatory before merge (mirrors Chapter 1's two-gate review, applied specifically to `param.*`):

1. **Technical review, always.** CI dry-run (Chapter 1, Import) validates reference integrity, that no ordered set has been reordered or had a value removed, that `benign_cutoff_index`-style structural fields stay internally consistent, and — new for this chapter — that the change lives in a `_shared_libraries*.yaml` file, never inside a study pack.
2. **Clinical-owner sign-off, when the entry is severity-bearing.** A `param.*` entry is treated exactly like a `sev.*` scale for review purposes — requiring the same clinical-owner approval as D-05 mandates for severity content — whenever either is true: (a) `ordered: true` is set (position drives lint/impression assembly, so a wrong order or a wrongly-inserted value is a clinical-safety-adjacent defect), or (b) the entry is the target of a `derive_from` link from any `sev.*` scale, or a source of a `critical_at`/`critical_registry` trigger. Unordered, non-derivation-linked choice lists (`param.echotexture`, `param.mr_signal`, `param.tube_position`, and similar plain descriptors) need only the technical-review gate and a content-editor approval, the same tier as wording/tile changes elsewhere in the catalog.

This is a parameter-lifecycle-specific extension of D-05's "centrally governed with clinical sign-off" principle: D-05 names `sev.*` scales explicitly, but `param.cord_ladder` and `param.calc_morphology` carry identical clinical weight ("the cervical severity spine of the report") while living outside the `sev.` prefix purely for historical/authoring reasons. Treating them identically closes that gap rather than reopening D-05.

**No `param.*` entry, ever, is practice-editable.** Master Spec §16.2's practice-editable list is wording, tiles/pins, combos, synonyms, additional aliases, recommendation phrasing, and locale/practice overlays — it does not include parameter value lists. A practice may never add, remove, or reorder a `param.*` value, and may never introduce a study-specific `param.*` key of its own (see Overrides, below, for what a practice *can* touch: default wording and practice-settable thresholds via the overlay mechanism).

### Study-specific parameters

**The rule: never.** A study pack may only ever *bind* to `param.*` by reference (`parameters: [param.size, param.margin]` on a finding); it may never define its own `parameter_library` block, and no shipped study pack does. This is enforced structurally, not just by convention: `param.*` has no study-prefix variant in the ID grammar (Chapter 1) — the eleven legal entity-type patterns are `study.<key>`, `<study_prefix>.<finding_key>`, `param.<key>`, `sev.<key>`, `lat.<key>`, `loc.<key>`, `meas.<key>`, `rec.<key>`, `crit.<key>`, `combo.<study>.<key>`, `tpl.normal.<study>[_<variant>]` — there is no `<study_prefix>.param.<key>` form. A study can own findings, combos, and normal-template variants under its own prefix; it can never own a parameter, severity, laterality, location, measurement, recommendation, or critical-registry key.

**What "study-specific-feeling" content actually does.** The v1.1 extension's MSK params (`tear_grade`, `meniscus_pattern`, `cartilage_grade`) are, as of v1, consumed by only a small number of studies — but they still live in the shared-library extension file, not in a study pack, because the namespace is global and because a second study needing the same concept later (a future MRI Shoulder pack reusing `tear_grade`) must resolve to the *same* key, not a duplicate. The rule for an implementer: if a clinical concept is genuinely reusable across studies (even if only one study currently uses it), it is shared-library content from day one. There is no "promote a study-local param to shared" migration path in this system because study-local params are not a legal state to begin with.

**Importer enforcement (new structural check, owned by this chapter).** The importer's five B-16 validations plus the three D-16 conversion-bug checks (Chapter 1) do not by themselves catch a study pack that illegally carries a `parameter_library` (or `severity_library`/`laterality_library`/`location_library`/`measurement_library`/`recommendation_library`/`critical_registry`/`normal_templates`) top-level block. This chapter adds a sixth MUST-enforce, fail-loud structural check to the importer, run in the same CI dry-run and real-import passes as the other eight: **no pack whose `pack_id` is not one of the three `_shared_libraries*` files may contain any shared-library-family top-level block.** A study pack that trips this check fails import exactly as any other structural violation does (Chapter 1's Import section — fail-closed, whole-transaction rollback).

**Namespacing summary.**

| Entity family | Namespace shape | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| `param.*`, `sev.*`, `lat.*`, `loc.*`, `meas.*`, `rec.*`, `crit.*` | Flat, global — no study prefix, ever | Content editor + review tier above; lives only in `_shared_libraries*.yaml` |
| `<study_prefix>.<finding_key>` | Study-prefixed (B-29 registry: `mrbr.`, `mrls.`, `mrcs.`, `ctbr.`, `usab.`, `uskb.`, `hrct.`, `cxr.`, `dopll.`, `mmg.`, plus `screen./mreps./mrtn./mrls_scr./mrcs_scr.`) | Study pack, references `param.*` etc. by key |
| `combo.<study>.<key>` | Study-prefixed | Study pack |
| `tpl.normal.<study>[_<variant>]` | Study-prefixed, variant-suffixed (D-17, e.g. `doppler_ll` venous/arterial) | Study pack |

### Overrides

Four layers can influence what a radiologist actually sees or types for a given parameter slot, from most specific to least. Only the top layer is a literal, one-off act; the other three are stored, precedence-ordered content:

1. **Per-report (the radiologist's own keystroke).** Once a tile fires, its `default_sentence` inserts with `{slot}` placeholders; the radiologist tab-through-fills or edits each slot inline. Whatever they type is the report text — it is never "resolved" against anything else, is not persisted as catalog or preference state, and always wins over every layer below it for that one instance. An unfilled placeholder left at sign time is a lint class (ux-review §3.5; enforced by finding-lifecycle's gate), never a silent default.
2. **Per-radiologist personalization (ranking and phrasing only).** My Prefs / hanging-protocol data (Master Spec §9.5, §11.3) re-ranks which tiles and grade/level-slotted aliases (`fl1`/`fl2`/`fl3`, `pv45`, …) a radiologist reaches first, and the learning loop's "teach" rules (§12.8) adjust *phrasing and suggestion selection* style. Both are firewalled per B-12: personalization never changes which `param.*` values are legal, never changes an ordered set's position, and never touches a severity/criticality determination. Concretely, this layer changes *which alias the radiologist is likely to fire* (and therefore which value gets pre-filled by that alias), not a separately-stored "this radiologist's default severity is X" override — no source in this system describes the latter, and this chapter does not invent one: no per-radiologist severity/value override exists or is planned, and any future proposal for one is a B-12 firewall change requiring the same review tier as a personalization-firewall amendment, not a parameter-lifecycle decision.
3. **Per-site/practice overlay.** A practice may override *wording* (a finding's `default_sentence`, `impression_fragment`, or a `rec.*` display line) and any threshold explicitly marked practice-settable in its shared-library `flag` text (the shipped example: `meas.pvr`'s significance cutoff, authored as `"significant per practice setting (default > 50 ml)"`). Per C-24, these overrides live in an overlay table keyed by `id_key` + `field` — never as edits to pack content — owned and versioned separately from the catalog import; a catalog re-seed never clobbers an overlay, and a central wording change that lands under an active overlay surfaces as a review-queue item for the practice editor rather than silently applying or silently being shadowed. The overlay table's physical schema belongs to the database-evolution chapter; this chapter fixes its key shape (`id_key`, `field`) and its governance boundary: **an overlay may change wording or an explicitly practice-settable threshold value; it may never change a `param.*` value list, order, unit, `render` format, or a `sev.*`/`crit.*` structural binding.**
4. **Locale pack.** Keyed by `id_key`, holding an alternate-language or alternate-phrasing rendering of the same radiologist-visible string. See Localization, below, for the full mechanics; for override-precedence purposes it sits below the practice overlay and above the catalog reference.

The catalog reference default (the pack-authored `default_sentence`/`render` template/threshold) is the terminus of the chain — see Defaults, next — and always exists for any `active` entry, so the chain is total: it can never fail to resolve.

### Defaults

**How a default is declared.** There is no separate "default value" field on a `param.*` library entry itself — a `measurement`-type entry has no default number, and a `choice`-type entry has no default selected member. Defaults are declared at two levels above the library:

- **Finding level, as an unfilled placeholder.** A finding's `default_sentence` (and `impression_fragment`) carries `{slot}` names matching its bound `param.*`/`meas.*`/derived keys exactly (Chapter 1, ID grammar). Firing the base tile inserts the sentence with those slots as fillable text — this is a *template* default, not a *value* default; the radiologist supplies the value.
- **Alias level, as a pre-selected value.** A level- or grade-slotted keyboard alias (`fl2` = fatty liver Grade II, `pv45` = L4-5 protrusion) is the one mechanism in this system that pre-fills an actual `param.*`/`sev.*` value at fire time, because the alias itself encodes the value. Alias-to-value mappings are seed-time content (Chapter 1, Author/Import), reviewed under the same tier as the parameter or severity they encode (Shared, above).

Omitted fields elsewhere in a finding block take the catalog-wide conversion defaults already fixed by Chapter 1/D-15.2 (`criticality: none`, `normal_variant: false`, empty lists, `tile: true` implied) — this chapter does not restate those; it only adds that an *omitted* `parameters:` list means the finding has no parameter slots at all, not "use the study's usual set" — there is no per-study implicit parameter set to fall back to.

**Resolution order — the exact sequence an implementer codes.** Given an `id_key` and a `field` (a finding's `default_sentence`/`impression_fragment`, a `rec.*` display line, or a practice-settable `meas.*`/`param.*` threshold), the presentation-resolution function runs, in order, and returns on first hit:

| Step | Layer | Source | Terminal? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Per-report | The draft's own already-typed/edited text for this instance | Yes — bypasses 2–5 entirely; not a "resolution," a fact already on the page |
| 2 | Per-radiologist | My Prefs pin order / learned teach-rule phrasing (ranking + style only, B-12-firewalled) | No — influences *which* alias/tile is offered, falls through to 3 for the wording of whatever is offered |
| 3 | Practice overlay | Overlay table row for `(id_key, field)`, C-24 | No — falls through to 4 if no overlay row exists |
| 4 | Locale pack | Locale-pack row for `id_key`, active deployment locale | No — falls through to 5 if no locale row exists or locale is `en-IN` |
| 5 | Reference | The pack-authored `default_sentence`/`render` template/threshold, `en-IN`, as imported | Always — guaranteed present for any `active` entry; total fallback |

Step 5 can never be empty for an `active` entry (the importer's reference-integrity check guarantees every bound key resolves, Chapter 1). This makes the function total: every parameter presentation resolves to *something*, even in a fresh single-locale, no-overlay, no-personalization deployment — steps 2–4 simply pass through and step 5 answers directly, which is exactly today's single-practice, `en-IN`-only Synology deployment.

**What this resolution order is not.** It never decides *which values are legal* for a `param.*`/`sev.*` slot — that set is fixed by the shared library and versioned per Chapter 1, completely independent of this chain. The chain only decides *wording and pre-fill/rank*, never the vocabulary.

**Derived defaults with a missing input.** When a `param.*`/`sev.*` value would ordinarily derive from a `meas.*` field (`sev.prostate` from `meas.prostate_volume`, `sev.cartilage` from `param.cartilage_grade`) and that measurement is absent, the derived value is simply unavailable — the radiologist may pick a value manually, which raises a non-blocking △ inconsistency ("grade asserted without measurement"), never a ✕ block (C-23). This chapter states the rule as it applies to parameter/severity derivation; the full lint mechanics belong to finding-lifecycle.

### Localization

**What is never localized.** Keys, codes, aliases, and slot names are immutable API and are never localized, in any locale (B-20, D §15.7) — `param.margin`, the string `margin`, and the slot name `{margin}` are identical in every deployment regardless of language. This also covers a `param.*` measurement entry's `units` list and `render` format string (e.g. `param.size`'s `"~{v} mm / {a} x {b} cm"`): unit tokens and the numeric-format grammar are part of the shared-library entity's schema, not radiologist-visible prose, so they follow catalog versioning (Chapter 1) exactly like a key would — never a locale-pack override. CARE's unit whitelist (mm, cm, ml, g, %, ratio, seconds, degrees, HU, weeks) is fixed catalog content; there is no unit-system localization (no metric/imperial split) because the reference and every deployed locale to date is `en-IN`, and Indian radiology practice is uniformly metric.

**What does localize.** Radiologist-visible strings — `default_sentence`, `impression_fragment`, `rec.*` display text, normal-template bodies — are locale packs keyed by `id_key`. `en-IN` is both the reference locale and the fallback; a locale pack may only *add* aliases/synonyms in its own language or phrasing, never remove or reassign a reference one (D §15.7). En-IN itself already encodes deliberate Indian clinical idiom as its baseline content, not as a locale override on top of some neutral base: Koch's (⇄ pulmonary TB), NAD, SOL, SVID, E/O, CBNAAT, and dorsal spine numbered D1–D12 (never T1–T12) are simply what the reference locale says (B-20, canonical terminology table). A future non-English locale pack (e.g. a Hindi or regional-language phrasing set) would sit under the same `id_key`-keyed mechanism and the same resolution position (step 4 above) without changing any key, alias, or unit.

**Synonyms are not locale content.** The global synonym thesaurus (search/dictation resolution, e.g. "Koch's", "PIVD", "stone", "NAD" → canonical entry) is reference-catalog content bound to `id_key`, versioned and reviewed exactly like any other shared-library addition (MINOR bump, Shared section above) — it is not locale-scoped and does not participate in the practice→locale→reference wording chain. Do not conflate "add a synonym" (a catalog content change, technical review, content-editor sign-off) with "add a locale phrasing" (a locale-pack row, no catalog version bump).

**Site-specific vocabulary.** A practice wanting its own habitual phrasing for a finding (a different way of saying the same clinical fact) uses the practice overlay (Overrides, step 3) — not a new locale, and not a new `param.*` value. If a practice's "different vocabulary" turns out to actually mean a different legal value or a different clinical concept, that is a shared-library content change (Shared section) requiring the appropriate review tier, never an overlay or locale-pack workaround.

### Versioning without breaking signed reports

**Resolution happens at insertion time, not at display time.** When a tile or alias fires, or a ghost is accepted, the parameter/severity/measurement values it carries are resolved (per the five-step order above) and baked immediately into the draft's report text and into the finding-instance's `structured_json` (Chapter 1 / catalog-lifecycle's snapshot mechanics; as-built precedent: `AbnormalityInstance` and `RenderedAbnormality` are computed once at insertion, not recomputed from a live template on every render). A later change to a `param.*` entry — a new value appended to an ordered list, a corrected `render` format, a new shared-library extension file — never reaches back into text that is already sitting in a draft or a signed report, because nothing in the render path re-resolves catalog state to redisplay existing text.

**Sign freezes the version, not just the text.** At sign, `catalog_version` (and `template_version`/`render_engine_version`) are stamped onto the report in the same transaction that sets `final_report_id`, and finding-instance rows are snapshot-copied (C-02, D-18, D-19, owned by Chapter 1). For `param.*` specifically this means: even in the hypothetical case where some future feature *did* re-render structured data from stored parameter references rather than stored text, the stamped `catalog_version` pins exactly which `param.*` value lists and render formats were in force at sign, so a re-render would reproduce the original — a signed report is never at the mercy of a later parameter-library edit.

**MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH applied to `param.*`** (specializing Chapter 1's general catalog semver rule, B-03/D-18):

| Change | Classification | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Append a new value to an existing choice list (ordered or unordered) | MINOR | Additive; existing values, order, and meaning untouched |
| Add a new `param.*` key in a new shared-library extension file | MINOR | New namespace entry, nothing existing changes |
| Reorder an existing `ordered: true` list, or remove/rename a value | MAJOR (governance failure) — effectively forbidden | Position is load-bearing for lint/impression assembly (B-04); "reordering" is not a maintenance operation, it is a breaking semantic change and must go through the MAJOR-bump governance gate (Chapter 1, Review) if it is ever truly required |
| Change a `measurement`-type entry's `units`, `dims`, or `render` format in a way that changes what already-rendered text means | MAJOR | Semantic change to an existing key; old signed reports remain correct only because they already baked the old render at insertion, never because the key stayed compatible |
| Fix a `render` format's cosmetic formatting (spacing, punctuation) without changing what unit/value it expresses | PATCH | Wording-equivalent change |
| Practice overlay add/edit/remove | Not a catalog version event at all | Overlays are practice-owned content outside the catalog semver, per C-24 |

Because reordering or renaming is functionally forbidden rather than merely discouraged, an implementer should treat any pull request that touches the *order* or *membership* of an existing `ordered: true` `param.*` list as an automatic hold for Chief Clinical Architect + clinical-owner review (Chapter 1's MAJOR-bump gate), not a routine MINOR extension — this is stricter than the general catalog rule precisely because `param.*` ordering has no separate "management binding" safety net the way `sev.*` scales do (B-05); the ordering *is* the safety-relevant content.

### Acceptance criteria

- Every `param.*` entry lives in exactly one of `_shared_libraries.yaml` / `_shared_libraries_v1_1.yaml` / `_shared_libraries_v1_2.yaml` (or a future additive extension file); no study pack file contains a `parameter_library` or any other shared-library-family top-level block, enforced as a fail-loud importer check.
- No `param.*` value list is ever reordered or has a member removed/renamed; every historical addition is append-only and machine-verifiable from the pack's diff history.
- Every `ordered: true` `param.*` entry, and every `param.*` entry linked via `derive_from`/`critical_at` to a severity or critical trigger, carries a recorded clinical-owner approval on its authoring PR; unordered, non-derivation-linked entries carry only a content-editor + passing-CI approval.
- The parameter presentation-resolution function implements all five steps (per-report, per-radiologist, practice overlay, locale, reference) in that order, returns on first hit, and step 5 never fails to resolve for an `active` entry.
- Practice overlay rows are keyed by `(id_key, field)`, never touch a `param.*` value list/order/unit/render format, and survive a catalog re-seed unmodified.
- No unit token, key, alias, or slot name is ever found inside a locale-pack row; locale packs contain only radiologist-visible phrasing keyed by `id_key`.
- A signed report's rendered parameter text is provably unaffected by any `param.*` change made after its `catalog_version` was stamped (regression test: mutate a shared-library extension post-sign, re-fetch the signed report, assert byte-identical `body`).

### Build order

1. Confirm/extend the Chapter 1 importer with the sixth structural check (no shared-library blocks inside study packs) — depends on Chapter 1's importer core (load order, two-pass resolution) already existing.
2. Implement the five-step parameter presentation-resolution function as a single, testable module consumed by tile-fire, alias-fire, and ghost-accept code paths alike — depends on nothing else in this chapter being deployed first, but its step 3 (practice overlay) and step 4 (locale pack) can initially be no-op pass-throughs until their storage lands.
3. Practice overlay table (`id_key` + `field` keyed) — schema owned by database-evolution chapter; this chapter's contract (governance boundary, precedence position) is the input to that design.
4. Locale-pack storage — same dependency shape as step 3; `en-IN` reference content already exists in the packs today, so this step is additive infrastructure, not a content migration.
5. Insertion-time baking discipline (resolve-once-at-insert, never re-resolve-at-display) — verify this is already true of the as-built render path (`abnormalityEngine`/`renderEngine`) before any `param.*` content work proceeds, since every other guarantee in this chapter depends on it; if a future render_v2 (Strand F, Chapter 1) changes this assumption it must preserve it explicitly.
6. Sign-time `catalog_version` stamping and finding-instance snapshot-copy — depends on Chapter 1's Version section landing first; this chapter's "versioning without breaking signed reports" guarantee is only as strong as that stamping actually being wired.
7. Per-radiologist ranking/phrasing personalization surface (My Prefs, hanging protocols, teach-rules) — depends on the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter's personalization firewall (B-12) being implemented, since this chapter requires that surface never touch legal values or ordering.

---

## Chapter 4 — Recommendation lifecycle

A recommendation is never free prose the system invents; it is a `rec.*` shared-library code, bound by reference to a finding, a scale category, a combo, or a critical-registry entry, and rendered into fixed or slot-filled text at draft time. This chapter follows a recommendation from the moment a finding instance fires it, through merge into the report-level recommendation block, through deduplication when several findings independently produce the same or a related recommendation, through conflict resolution when two recommendations genuinely disagree, to how a radiologist's manual edit to recommendation text survives every later regeneration. It owns the two templated codes (`rec.urgent`, `rec.fleischner`), the BI-RADS-style mandatory-management binding pattern, and the merge/dedup/conflict engine that turns N findings' individual recommendation contributions into one coherent block. It depends on Chapter 2 (finding-lifecycle) for the finding-instance state machine, the criticality tag, and the full critical-escalation *workflow* (referrer contact, communication timestamp, decline-with-reason) — this chapter owns only how the resulting `rec.urgent` *text* is generated and merged, not the workflow around it. It depends on Chapter 1 (catalog-lifecycle) for `rec.*` key governance, semver, and practice wording overlays, and on the impression-lifecycle chapter for how `impression_fragment`s (a separate, sibling mechanism) assemble — recommendations and impression fragments are two different report sections generated from the same finding instance and must not be conflated.

### Generation

A finding instance can acquire recommendation text through exactly four generation paths. An implementer adding a fifth path is almost certainly modeling something that belongs in one of the four, not inventing a new mechanism.

1. **Fixed-code binding on the finding entry.** A catalog finding carries a `recommendation_code` array of `rec.*` codes (default empty, per Seed Spec §1.2). This array is static for the finding *key* — it is not conditioned on which severity, laterality, or level parameter value the radiologist picked. Parameters change the *finding* sentence; they do not change *which* `rec.*` codes are bound to that finding. Any content author who wants a different recommendation for a different severity must express it through the scale-bound mandatory-management pattern (below), not by hand-varying `recommendation_code` per parameter value — there is exactly one sanctioned pattern for severity-conditioned recommendations, and it lives on the scale.
2. **Scale-bound mandatory management (B-05, the BI-RADS pattern).** `sev.birads` carries a `mandatory_management` flag set to true: each category value ("0"–"6", including "4a"/"4b"/"4c") has a fixed, structural management sentence — no seed, insert, or sign path may yield a category without its bound line. This is not a `recommendation_code` array on a finding; it is a property of the *scale value itself*, atomic with category selection — the UI must not let a radiologist choose a BI-RADS category without its management line accompanying it. This pattern is mandatory for future TI-RADS/PI-RADS scales (B-05): any new scale with clinically-mandatory follow-up action binds its management text the same way, on the scale, never as a separate `recommendation_code` a radiologist could omit.
3. **Combo-attached recommendations.** A combo tile can carry `rec.*` codes directly as members alongside findings, independent of any individual finding's own `recommendation_code` (e.g., `combo.uskb.colic_classic` includes `rec.spec_uro`; `combo.cxr.tb_active_set` resolves to `[rec.lab_sputum, rec.hrct]`). Firing the combo generates these recommendations exactly once, at combo-fire time, through the same rendering path as a finding-bound recommendation — the merge/dedup engine (below) does not distinguish a combo-sourced recommendation instance from a finding-sourced one; both carry the same `rec.<code>` identity and are deduplicated identically.
4. **Templated recommendations (B-06, exactly two — no third may be added without a Chief Clinical Architect + clinical-owner decision to extend this table).**

| Code | Fixed text pattern | Slot(s) | Filled by | Confirmation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rec.urgent` | "URGENT {specialist} attention is advised — findings communicated to the referring doctor at {time}." | `{specialist}`, `{time}` | The critical-results escalation workflow (Chapter 2, B-07/C-22): specialist from the crit.\* registry's routing metadata, `{time}` is the attested communication timestamp. | Not radiologist-authored text to confirm — the *act* of communicating is the confirmation; the line only renders once the escalation is at least started (Chapter 2's sign-gate rule). |
| `rec.fleischner` | "Follow-up CT chest at {interval} as per Fleischner guidelines." | `{interval}` | AI, computed from `meas.nodule_avg` + risk factors, anchored to **Fleischner 2017** (C-21, the only pinned version; the guideline chip renders its version explicitly) | Radiologist must explicitly accept/edit before insertion — offer-never-act (P6/D-21 analog applies to recommendation text exactly as it does to measurement values: no confidence level auto-inserts a `{interval}` fill). |

Every other `rec.*` code (~37 in the seeded set: the follow-up family `rec.fup_usg6w/3m/6m/fup_cxr2w`, `rec.repeat_ncct24h`, `rec.fup_mmg6m`; the specialist-referral family `rec.spec_uro/neuro/nsx/ortho/pulmo/gyn/onc/surg/vasc/breast`; the further-imaging family `rec.cect/cemri/mrcp/ctkub/hrct/mra_mrv/cta/petct/usg_corr/screening_spine/dexa`; the labs family `rec.lab_lft/rft/urine/sputum`; and the misc singles `rec.clincorr`, `rec.physio`, `rec.echo_corr`, `rec.pft`, `rec.biopsy`) renders **fixed text with no runtime slot-fill logic at all**. Findings reference these codes; they never carry free-text advice — an inline recommendation string on a finding block (as opposed to a `recommendation_code` reference) is an importer hard error (D-16(b)), and AI never free-styles management text outside the two templated codes above (a guideline-anchored suggestion that isn't `rec.fleischner` and isn't a plain `rec.*` code citation does not render — this is the same "must cite or don't render" gate the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter owns, B-13).

**Severity/context gating, stated once, precisely.** There are exactly two ways a condition governs whether a recommendation fires, and no third:
- **Criticality gating.** `critical_if` (deterministic for hard measurement thresholds and scale-side criticality maps, LLM-copilot-evaluated for residual plain-language conditions, per C-04 — never the sole gate for a critical trigger) fires a `crit.<key>` registry entry, which pre-fills `rec.urgent`. `significant_if` (C-13) never gates or alters recommendation attachment at all — it only changes impression tier and sets a worklist significance flag. An implementer must not read `significant` as "attach a stronger recommendation"; it attaches nothing recommendation-side.
- **Scale-category gating.** Only the mandatory-management pattern (BI-RADS-style, path 2 above) conditions recommendation text on a severity/scale *value*. Because `sev.birads` is radiologist-asserted (a direct category pick), not derived from a measurement, there is no "grade unavailable" case for it the way there is for `sev.prostate` (C-23) — the management line is always determinate once a category is chosen.

Recommendation-code **wording** is practice-editable (catalog-lifecycle chapter, C-24 overlay); the **codes themselves, their families, and their templated/fixed status are centrally governed** with clinical sign-off (same governance posture as the critical registry, D-05) — a practice may reword `rec.spec_uro`'s sentence, it may never invent a new `rec.*` code, retarget which findings bind to it, or turn a fixed code into a templated one outside this handbook's registry.

### Merge

**Governing invariant, stated once because everything below depends on it:** dedup and conflict resolution operate **only on the rendered recommendation section's text**; they never delete, mutate, or reorder the underlying `report_finding_instances` rows (Chapter 2) or their own `recommendation_code`/criticality data. Each finding instance still independently computes its own `RenderedAbnormality.recommendation` value exactly as before; only the merged canvas representation collapses duplicates and orders lines. This is the same "row is truth, canvas is a projection" discipline Chapter 2 establishes for finding text, applied without exception to recommendation text.

**As-built mechanics (today).** `RenderedAbnormality { finding, impression, technique, recommendation }` is computed per instance by `renderAbnormality`/`fillTemplate` (`abnormalityEngine.ts`); the report's live `ReportTextState.recommendation` is currently a **flat string**, merged with the same `mergeBlock`/`removeBlock` paragraph-append helpers used for `rawFindings` and `technique` (`quickFindingsMerge.ts`, re-exported through `renderEngine.ts`). `mergeBlock` appends a rendered recommendation as its own paragraph unless the exact trimmed block already exists anywhere in the string (`existing.includes(trimmedBlock)`); `removeBlock` removes the first exact verbatim match and collapses the resulting blank-line gap. This is adequate for identity-level dedup (an exact-duplicate paragraph is silently never added twice) but has no concept of a `rec.<code>` identity, a family, or a severity tier — it can only compare literal rendered text.

**Target representation (this chapter's required build item).** `ReportTextState.recommendation` is promoted from `string` to `string[]`, reusing the array-based `mergeImpression`/`removeImpression` helpers that already exist for the impression section (byte-for-byte equivalent logic, zero new merge primitives) instead of `mergeBlock`/`removeBlock`. Each array entry carries, alongside its rendered text, the originating `rec.<code>` and the originating finding instance's criticality tier (`critical` / `significant` / `none`, from Chapter 2's finding-instance data) as line metadata (not part of the persisted report prose — the same way impression bullets are structured internally but rendered as a flat block at the presentation layer). This is what makes code-identity dedup, family-level dedup, and tiered ordering possible without fuzzy text matching, which the design deliberately avoids (fuzzy matching risks silently discarding a radiologist's edited clinical wording — exact-match-or-leave-alone is the only safe primitive, per Chapter 2's manual-edit-protection discipline).

**Ordering.** The recommendation block orders **critical-tier lines first, then significant-tier, then routine (criticality `none`) lines**, each tier in firing order within itself — the same worst-first discipline the impression-lifecycle chapter applies to impression fragments (B-14), extended to recommendations for the same reason: a stray follow-up-imaging line must never visually bury an urgent escalation line. Reordering is implemented as insertion-point selection (new critical-tier lines insert after the last existing critical-tier line, not a full resort of the array on every render), so an already-rendered array of lines is never silently reshuffled by an unrelated edit elsewhere in the report — reshuffling is exactly the kind of surprise motion Chapter 2's "canvas never moves due to unrelated activity" invariant (B-18) forbids.

### Deduplication

Three distinct dedup rules apply, at three different strengths, and an implementer must keep them separate:

1. **Identity dedup (silent, always safe).** Two or more finding instances (or a finding instance and a combo) that resolve to the **same `rec.<code>`** collapse to one rendered line.
