This is safe because it is literally the same text — no clinical judgment is lost, and nothing is suppressed that wasn't already redundant. `rec.urgent` is the one exception to plain code-identity dedup: its dedup key is **(code, specialist, communication event)**, not code alone, because two escalations to two different specialists — e.g., neurosurgery for an ICH and thoracic surgery for a tension pneumothorax found on the same study — are genuinely two distinct communications and must never collapse into one line that obscures which specialist maps to which finding. Two `rec.urgent` instances that resolve to the *same* specialist and the *same* communication event (e.g., one phone call covering two related critical findings) do collapse to one line.

2. **Family-level dedup with strongest-variant selection (silent, but only where the resolution direction is provably always the safer one).** A `rec.*` entry carries an optional `family` tag in the shared library (a MINOR catalog addition, not string-prefix inference in application code — prefix-matching on code spelling is fragile and not what governs this). Two ratified families exist at v1:
   - **Follow-up-interval family** (e.g., the `rec.fup_*` variants that recommend the same modality's follow-up at different intervals, and `rec.fup_mmg6m` within mammography). When two or more findings in the same report independently fire different-interval members of the same family, the merge engine renders **only the single shortest (most conservative) interval** and suppresses the longer ones. Shorter-interval-wins is safe to auto-resolve without a radiologist prompt because more frequent monitoring is never the clinically wrong direction — this is the one case in this chapter where auto-resolution requires no radiologist gesture.
   - **Fleischner multi-nodule family.** When a report contains more than one `rec.fleischner`-tagged nodule finding, Fleischner 2017 itself specifies that the *dominant* nodule (largest / highest-risk) governs the surveillance interval for the whole recommendation — this is not an implementation convenience, it is the guideline's own rule. The merge engine selects the dominant nodule's finding instance as the `rec.fleischner` slot-fill source and renders one line; the non-dominant nodules' own measurements remain fully present in the Findings section (dedup here touches only the recommendation line, never the finding text, per the governing invariant above).

   Strongest-variant selection never deletes the suppressed finding instances or their own `recommendation_code` data (governing invariant, above); the suppressed variant is always reconstructable from the finding-instance records already retained by Chapter 2 — no separate audit table is required for this, because the resolution is deterministic and reproducible from data the system already keeps forever.

   - **Structural-management/coded-recommendation collision (D-16(c)).** `rec.fup_mmg6m` is annotated `derived_from: sev.birads."3"` in the catalog, and the importer fails loud if its fixed text ever drifts from the `sev.birads` category-3 management line's text (a governance guarantee that the two are always byte-identical). Because they are byte-identical, a report that carries both a BI-RADS 3 category (whose management line renders structurally, per Generation path 2, and carries no `rec.<code>` identity at all) and a finding that independently fires `rec.fup_mmg6m` would otherwise show the same sentence twice: plain code-identity dedup (rule 1) cannot catch this pair, because one side of it has no code to match against. The merge engine resolves this with a single narrow exception to the "compare by code, never by text" discipline: before a `rec.fup_mmg6m` line is added to the array, it is checked for an exact-text match against the report's already-rendered `sev.birads` management line, and suppressed if one matches. This is the only place in the merge engine where literal-text comparison, rather than code identity, is the dedup key, and it exists solely because catalog governance (D-16(c)) already guarantees the two texts can never legitimately differ.

3. **What dedup never touches.** Manually typed recommendation text (a radiologist writing prose directly into the recommendation section, or editing a rendered line so it no longer exact-matches its source template) is invisible to both identity and family dedup — it carries no `rec.<code>`, so it cannot collide with anything the engine tracks. If a radiologist pastes the literal same sentence twice, `mergeBlock`'s/`mergeImpression`'s substring/line-equality check happens to prevent the second literal duplicate, but this is an incidental property of the merge primitive, not a dedup *rule* — free text is never subject to family reduction, tier reordering, or code-identity collapse.

### Conflict resolution

Not every disagreement between two recommendations is safe to resolve automatically. The dividing line is exact: **auto-resolve only when the resolution direction is provably always the safer one** (Section A's stricter-reading-wins principle, applied here); everything else is surfaced, never silently decided by the system.

| Scenario | Resolution | Radiologist involvement |
|---|---|---|
| Identical `rec.<code>` fired by two+ findings/combos | Auto-collapse to one line (Dedup rule 1) | None — silent, deterministic |
| Same interval-family, differing intervals | Auto-select shortest interval (Dedup rule 2) | None — silent, deterministic, always the more conservative direction |
| Fleischner multi-nodule, differing dominant nodule | Auto-select per Fleischner 2017's own dominance rule (Dedup rule 2) | None — silent, guideline-mandated, not an implementation shortcut |
| `rec.urgent` to distinct specialists | Never collapsed (Dedup rule 1's exception) | Both lines render; radiologist confirms each escalation independently (Chapter 2) |
| A free-text or AI-proposed recommendation contradicts a **structurally mandatory management line** (BI-RADS-style, B-05) | **Never auto-resolved. ✕-blocking lint mark; sign gate blocks.** | Radiologist must edit the contradicting text or remove the conflicting finding before sign — this is the one recommendation-level case that is genuinely internally-false, the same bar the normal-study contradiction meets in Chapter 2, and it is treated identically: a jump-linked blocking item at the sign gate, never a silent auto-pick. |
| Any other cross-family semantic contradiction (e.g., two independently-authored recommendations that are in tension but do not contradict a structurally mandatory line) | **Never auto-resolved. △ non-blocking lint mark, dismiss-with-reason required.** | Radiologist edits, or dismisses with a recorded reason (audit-trailed, B-11); does not block sign. |
| Manually typed recommendation text duplicating or contradicting a coded recommendation | Not lint-checked as a coded contradiction (the engine cannot compare free text to a `rec.<code>` semantically) — left entirely to the radiologist | Full ownership; the engine takes no action |

The `ai.contradiction_checks` rules already declared per study pack (as-built `study_ai_rules`) are the mechanism that detects the cross-family cases above at the semantic level — this chapter defines the two possible *outcomes* (✕ vs △) and their precondition (does the contradiction conflict with a structurally mandatory line, or not); the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter owns how a contradiction check renders as a gutter mark and how dismissal is captured (B-11). No LLM-evaluated contradiction check may ever be the sole gate that *creates* a ✕-blocking state on its own initiative without matching this precondition table — that would let model behavior, not a structural rule, decide what blocks sign, which C-04's binding constraint on blocking rules forbids.

### Manual edits and regeneration

Recommendation text is subject to the identical exact-match discipline Chapter 2 establishes for finding text, because it flows through the same `applyRenderedTransition` remove-then-merge reducer: on any re-render of a finding instance (a parameter edit, a severity change, a deselect/reselect), the engine exact-removes what that instance previously contributed to the recommendation section, then merges in whatever it newly renders. If the radiologist has edited the previously-rendered recommendation line so it no longer matches verbatim, **removal silently no-ops** — the edited line is never deleted out from under the radiologist, exactly as with finding and technique text.

**A sharp edge engineers must know, not "fix."** Because removal is skipped when the old text has been edited, and the merge step for the newly-rendered text checks only "does this exact new string already exist" (not "was this instance's old contribution edited"), a parameter change on an instance whose recommendation line was previously hand-edited can result in **both** the radiologist's edited line and the freshly re-rendered line appearing in the block simultaneously. This is accepted, documented behavior, not a bug to be patched with fuzzy matching — fuzzy matching would reopen the exact risk this discipline exists to prevent (silently discarding edited clinical wording). The mitigations available to an implementer are: (a) treat an edit to a recommendation line as moving the owning finding instance from Draft-Unconfirmed to Draft-Confirmed (C-12's confirmation semantics apply to recommendation edits identically to finding-text edits — an edit is an edit, regardless of which of the four rendered sections it touches); (b) once an instance is Draft-Confirmed, a follow-on parameter edit that would re-render its recommendation should be treated as a fresh instance-level decision requiring the radiologist to notice and reconcile the duplicate manually, exactly as the radiologist already reconciles any other rendering surprise before sign, via the lint gutter marks (a leftover duplicate line is exactly the kind of divergence a contradiction/consistency check should flag as △, not something the merge engine tries to auto-repair).

**Regeneration triggers, exhaustively.** The recommendation block is recomputed (via the transition described above) on exactly the same events that re-render finding text: a Quick Select tile deselect/reselect, a parameter-chip patch (side/severity/chronicity/level/value), an AI ghost accept/reject, and macro/combo fire or removal. There is no separate "regenerate recommendations" action or endpoint — recommendation text has no lifecycle independent of the finding instance that produced it. Practice-level wording-overlay edits (C-24, catalog-lifecycle chapter) to a `rec.*` code's central text do **not** retroactively rewrite recommendation lines already rendered into an in-progress draft — an overlay change takes effect only for instances rendered *after* the overlay is saved; an open draft is never silently rewritten mid-edit by a content change happening elsewhere, matching the same "nothing touches the canvas from outside the radiologist's own actions" invariant (B-18) that governs every other surface in this system. A signed report's recommendation text is, in any case, frozen at the sign-time snapshot (Chapter 2, Signed section) and is never subject to any of this regeneration machinery again.

### Acceptance criteria

- No recommendation text renders in a report without a traceable `rec.<code>` anchor, except text the radiologist typed directly; an importer or an AI code path emitting free-text advice as a "recommendation" without a code is a defect, not a variant.
- Exactly two `rec.*` codes are runtime slot-filled (`rec.urgent`, `rec.fleischner`); every other code renders fixed text with no slot logic; adding a third templated code requires an explicit Chief Clinical Architect + clinical-owner decision recorded as a register amendment.
- `rec.fleischner`'s `{interval}` and `rec.urgent`'s `{specialist}`/`{time}` are never auto-inserted into report text at any AI confidence level; insertion is always an explicit radiologist gesture.
- A BI-RADS category can never exist in a draft or a signed report without its bound `sev.birads.management` line; the same structural binding is the only sanctioned pattern for any future mandatory-management scale.
- The recommendation merge engine never deletes, mutates, or reorders `report_finding_instances` rows or their `recommendation_code` values; dedup and conflict resolution act only on the rendered text projection.
- Identical `rec.<code>` duplicates collapse to one line; `rec.urgent` dedups on (code, specialist, communication event), never on code alone.
- Follow-up-interval-family and Fleischner multi-nodule duplicates auto-resolve to the single most conservative/guideline-dominant variant, silently, with the suppressed variant's source data always reconstructable from retained finding-instance rows.
- A `rec.fup_mmg6m` line is suppressed whenever it exact-text-matches a rendered `sev.birads` category-3 management line already in the block, so a BI-RADS 3 report never shows the same management sentence twice (D-16(c)).
- A recommendation contradiction that conflicts with a structurally mandatory management line is ✕-blocking at sign; every other cross-family contradiction is △ and requires a recorded dismiss-with-reason; neither is ever auto-resolved.
- An edited (non-exact-match) recommendation line is never silently removed by a later regeneration cycle, under any circumstance, including the documented dual-line edge case.
- A practice wording-overlay change to `rec.*` text never retroactively rewrites recommendation lines already present in an open draft.

### Build order

1. **Catalog dependency (Chapter 1).** `rec.*` code identity, family tags, and templated/fixed status must exist as importable shared-library data (Strand H) before code-identity and family-level dedup can be built against anything but the interim quick-findings text field; until then, `recommendation_code` remains the interim per-finding text association described in Chapter 2's Structured section.
2. **Data-shape refactor.** Promote `ReportTextState.recommendation` from `string` to `string[]`, switching from `mergeBlock`/`removeBlock` to `mergeImpression`/`removeImpression` — a small, low-risk change reusing existing tested primitives, and the prerequisite for every rule below.
3. **Tier and code metadata on rendered lines.** Carry `rec.<code>` identity and the originating instance's criticality tier alongside each array entry (not in persisted report prose) so ordering, identity dedup, and family dedup have something structured to compare.
4. **Ordering.** Implement critical-first/significant-next/routine-last insertion-point selection (no full resorts).
5. **Dedup.** Identity dedup first (simplest, no clinical judgment involved), then the `rec.urgent` specialist/event-keyed exception, then the two ratified families (follow-up-interval shortest-wins, Fleischner dominant-nodule), then the narrow `rec.fup_mmg6m`-vs-BI-RADS-management-line exact-text collapse (D-16(c)).
6. **Conflict lint wiring.** Ties into the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter's ✕/△ gutter-mark mechanics and the pack `ai.contradiction_checks` rules (C-04); implement the structurally-mandatory-line precondition table before wiring any LLM-evaluated contradiction check, so a model can flag but never independently create a blocking state outside that table.
7. **Templated-code UI.** `rec.urgent` slot-fill ties into Chapter 2's escalation workflow; `rec.fleischner` slot-fill ties into the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter's margin-card Insert/Explain/Dismiss anatomy and the Fleischner 2017 guideline-version chip (C-21).
8. **Sign-time freeze.** No new work specific to this chapter — recommendation text inherits Chapter 2's sign-time snapshot and version-stamping wholesale; verify recommendation text is included in whatever the snapshot copies.

Steps 2–5 are independent of Strand H and should land first; they deliver ordering, identity dedup, and the two ratified family reductions against the current interim data model without waiting on the catalog importer. Step 6 should not land ahead of step 5 — a contradiction check needs deduplicated, tiered lines to reason about, or it will flag the family-reduction's own suppressed variants as false contradictions.

---

## Chapter 5 — Measurement lifecycle

A measurement is a typed numeric value — dimension, index, score, duration — bound to a `meas.<key>` shared-library slot (Chapter 3) and, once attached, to a finding instance (Chapter 2). It differs from every other structured element in this handbook because it has four distinct capture paths that converge on the same slot: a radiologist can type it, a machine-console screenshot can be OCR'd for it, a model can propose or derive it, or a modality can emit it as a coded DICOM Structured Report object. This chapter follows a measurement from whichever of those four gestures creates it, through the shared measurement pool, into a finding instance, through auto-calculation and severity derivation, and out to longitudinal follow-up tracking across studies. Every path converges on one non-negotiable contract: no source, however precise or however confident, ever writes a value into report text without an explicit radiologist gesture (D-21) — this chapter states how each source is staged for that gesture, and how it is proven, provenanced, and audited regardless of whether the radiologist ever accepts it.

### The measurement library and the measurement pool

The `meas.*` shared library (Chapter 3) defines the ~31 seeded fields, each with a unit drawn from the fixed whitelist — mm, cm, ml, g, %, ratio, seconds, degrees, HU, weeks — and, where applicable, an `auto_calc` formula and a `critical_at`/flag threshold (D-10). No field may accept a unit outside the whitelist; no code path composes a unit from free text. Multi-dimensional fields (e.g. `param.size`) carry 1–3 dimensions rendered as `"~{v} mm / {a} x {b} cm"`; per-site fields (kidney length per side, vein diameter per site, PSV per Doppler station with ratios, per-vertebral-level canal/cord/disc dimensions) carry a site or level qualifier alongside the `meas.<key>` — the qualifier is part of the instance's identity, not a separate key, mirroring the existing `spinal_measurements` per-level shape.

Physically, every measurement value — regardless of source — lands first in a **measurement pool** row before it is attached to any finding. This is the deliverable of Strand C (`ff_radiology_measurement_pool`, "finding-independent measurement pool + attach/detach," currently off) and is this chapter's answer to CTO finding §4.5/§4.7 (no canonical measurement entity; 12+ disjoint measurement tables joined by lowercased label strings; `usg_measurements` mixes `text` and `real` typing in the same row). The pool table (new; follows the identity rules of D-37 — `bigint` identity PK, nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id`, no `MAX(seq)+1` keys) carries, per row: the owning study/worklist reference; the resolved `meas.<key>` (nullable until resolved — see OCR and DICOM SR below); the raw extracted label/value as captured, kept verbatim even after resolution; the typed numeric value plus its whitelist unit (never mixed text/real for the same logical quantity); a coded concept where one exists (code, coding-scheme designator, code meaning — nullable, populated primarily by DICOM SR, prospectively adopted per CTO C2); `source` ∈ manual / ocr / ai / derived / dicom_sr / viewer (OHIF/Weasis measurement tools) — `derived` is the deterministic auto-calculation path (see AI §1 below), kept distinct from `ai` because it carries no confidence band and needs no accept-gesture review beyond the generated sentence around it; confidence band plus the internal raw score (band absent for `manual` and `derived`); provenance (engine/model/version, extraction job id, key-image or SOP-instance reference); `status` ∈ pending / confirmed / attached / rejected / superseded — `pending` is any unresolved row; `confirmed` is a row that has passed its source's confirmation gate (OCR/DICOM SR Attention-band field-level review, or an explicit `approve`) and is staged insertable but not yet in report text; `attached` is copied into a finding instance's slot; `rejected` is an explicit radiologist dismiss-with-reason gesture on a pending or confirmed row (skip/dismiss in the Measurement Assistant), preserved for audit rather than deleted; `superseded` is set automatically, never deleted, when a newer pool row for the same `meas.<key>`/source/study arrives before the prior row was ever attached (e.g. a corrected OCR re-sweep) — the newer row becomes the pending candidate and the old row's audit trail is retained; `attached_finding_instance_id` (nullable FK into `report_finding_instances`, set on attach, cleared on detach — attach/detach is the pool's namesake operation); `lesion_id` (nullable, populated only through the explicit-link gesture described under Follow-up). This table is additive-only (B-23): it ships empty, existing per-modality stores (`usg_measurements`, `spinal_measurements`, doppler/echo/fetal measurement tables) keep writing exactly as they do today, and each becomes a producer into the pool on its own migration ticket rather than all at once — the ordering of that migration is Chapter 9's territory (two sanctioned migration paths, additive-only doctrine); this chapter defines only the pool's shape and lifecycle.

A pool row's only path into a report is **attach**: a radiologist gesture that copies its value into a finding instance's structured slot (the `value` field of today's `AbnormalityInstance` shape, Chapter 2) and flips `status` to `attached`. Detach is the inverse and is itself a gesture, never automatic. A pool row that is never attached is not an error — an OCR sweep that surfaces twelve candidate values when only four are clinically relevant is expected, and the nine unused rows simply age out with the draft.

Rendering follows one rule everywhere: a measurement field shows its reference band and prior-trend dots **on the field itself** — never a separate panel (§6.4/§4.3-8). The radiologist decides whether a flagged value becomes a finding; the field never asserts that decision for them.

### Manual entry

Manual entry is the base case: the radiologist types (or dictates, per the ten-verb voice contract owned by Chapter 7) directly into a Measurement Assistant field that surfaces beside the sentence being written, in the Measure stage, the moment a measurable finding appears (typing "Evans" or dictating a ventricle finding raises the Evans Index field; the field is also reachable by name through the unified command line, e.g. `/evans`). A manually typed value is definitionally already radiologist-authored ground truth: it is written to the pool with `source: manual`, `status: attached` immediately (manual entry writes directly to the target slot — there is no separate accept gesture for one's own keystroke), and no confidence band chip is ever shown on it — band display exists to communicate machine uncertainty, and a manual value carries none.

Unit handling has exactly one hard rule: the input field for a given `meas.<key>` accepts only that key's whitelisted unit; there is no free-unit text box anywhere. This is a client-side, blocking constraint (not a lint tier — it never reaches a state where the wrong unit could be saved).

Validation ranges are not numerically specified by any source document — plausibility bounds are clinical content, not architecture, and belong in catalog governance (D-40/§15.2-10, content-only doctrine), never hardcoded in application code. This chapter fixes the **mechanism**, decided here because no register entry covers it and an engineer needs one to build the field: a `meas.<key>` entry may optionally carry a governance-set plausibility range (min/max, catalog-versioned like any other governed value, B-27 quarterly-reviewed). Violating it is a **△ non-blocking** lint mark ("value outside typical range for this field"), dismissible with reason exactly like every other △ — it never blocks save or sign, because a genuinely unusual patient is not a data-entry error. A field with no configured range simply has none; absence of a range is not itself a lint condition.

A measurement mentioned in report text but absent from the Measurement Assistant (or vice versa) is the "text vs. measurement-field divergence" △ check (§4.3-15/§3.5) — its lint mechanics belong to Chapters 3 and 7; this chapter's obligation is only that every attached pool row is discoverable by that check.

### OCR (viewer/console screenshot extraction)

OCR is, today, **the primary automated measurement source** in production — not DICOM SR (risk-governance §4.5: `usg_measurements.source` defaults to `'ocr'`). The pipeline: a sonographer/technologist captures a machine-console screenshot (stored via `radiology_report_key_images`, source `PACS_SCREENSHOT`); `POST /api/usg-extraction/extract` runs OCR against it; the client polls `GET /api/usg-extraction/study/:studyInstanceUID` every 5 seconds while the study is active; results land as pool rows (today, `ai_extraction_results`/`usg_measurements`), scoped to modality US/USG/Echo/Fetal, grouped by category (Fetal, Pelvic, Abdominal, …) in the Measurement Assistant.

The as-built label resolution is a known defect, not a target: today's stores join OCR output to a field by **lowercased label-string equality** across 5+ disjoint tables (CTO §4.5 MED). The target behavior for this chapter is a maintained **label-alias crosswalk** (an additive table, e.g. every OCR label variant a console vendor emits mapped to exactly one `meas.<key>`) that resolves the pool row's `meas_key` deterministically; an OCR label with no crosswalk entry is never guessed into the nearest-looking field — it lands in the pool with `meas_key: null`, its raw label and value preserved verbatim, flagged for content-editor triage. Silent mis-mapping is worse than an unresolved row; both are worse than dropping the value, which is why nothing is ever dropped.

**Confidence.** OCR carries the extraction engine's internal High/Medium/Low tier and raw score. Per D-22, that internal vocabulary never reaches the reading surface: it maps to the three canonical bands — High → Routine, Medium → Worth a look, Low → Attention (Low → Attention is explicit in the register; the other two follow the same ordinal mapping) — with the grey/amber palette, no percentages, no green, no "High/Medium/Low" label anywhere in the Measurement Assistant. The raw score stays available one click deep.

**Confirmation UX contract.** This is where the as-built and Roadmap v2 designs are explicitly superseded. Roadmap v2 Phase 6 specified that medium+ confidence values "may auto-insert." Per **D-21 (BINDING)**, that is removed: **no confidence band auto-inserts, ever.** The corrected contract:
- Extraction results land as **pending** pool rows, never as report text.
- Routine and Worth-a-look (former High/Medium) rows render as **insertable**: a per-row Insert, or a batch "Insert All" that inserts every currently-pending Routine/Worth-a-look row — "Insert All" is itself one explicit radiologist gesture and one atomic undo step (P10/B-08), not an automated action.
- Attention (former Low) rows render **flagged, not insertable**. They must be opened and field-level reviewed — edited or explicitly confirmed — before an Insert control even appears for that row. This is stricter than "insertable but discouraged": the UI must not offer the gesture at all until review has happened.
- `PATCH /api/usg-extraction/measurements/:id/approve` marks a pool row confirmed; it stages the value as insertable, it does not itself write into report text. A single "Approve & Insert" button press is compliant (it is one explicit gesture), but the system must never chain approve→insert without that press, and must never do so at all for an Attention-band row before its field-level review.

**Provenance/audit.** Every OCR-origin pool row records source engine/version, extraction job id, the key-image reference it was read from, band, raw score, and disposition (accepted / accepted-then-edited / skipped / dismissed-with-reason / overridden) — the same unconditional, undisableable, per-suggestion audit doctrine as any AI-originated proposal (B-10, table shape owned by Chapter 7); OCR is a machine proposal in the same sense the audit doctrine already covers, and this chapter extends that doctrine to it rather than defining a second, weaker trail. `UsgMeasurementReview.tsx` remains in the product, demoted to a corrections-only surface for values that need a full re-key rather than a one-field edit.

### AI (model-proposed and derived measurements)

Three distinct things are called "AI measurements" in the sources, and they carry different obligations:

1. **Deterministic auto-calculation.** A fixed formula computes one `meas.<key>` from others already on the report (Evans Index, ABC/2 volume, midline shift, CTR, prostate volume, PVR%, Meyerding, nodule average diameter, CTSS, ABI, reflux duration — the pinned list below). This is not a model inference and needs no confidence band — it is arithmetic, always reproducible, and per **C-04 (BINDING)** any measurement feeding a ✕-blocking or critical rule must stay on this deterministic path, never an LLM's sole gate. Auto-calc results are written to the pool with `source: derived`, carrying the formula id/version and the input pool-row ids it was computed from, so a later correction to an input can never silently rewrite an already-signed derived value out from under it (the derived value's own snapshot at sign, Chapter 2, is what a signed report shows forever after). The numeric value itself displays live, unlatched — but any generated **sentence** built around it (e.g. "Evans 0.34 — above 0.30 upper limit") is still AI-authored ghost text and requires the same explicit accept gesture as any other AI sentence (B-08).

   | `meas.<key>` | Formula / rule | Threshold |
   |---|---|---|
   | `meas.evans_index` | frontal horn width / max internal skull diameter | flag > 0.30 |
   | `meas.abc2_volume` | A × B × C / 2 | — |
   | `meas.midline_shift` | direct measurement | **critical ≥ 5 mm** → `crit.midline_shift` (hard trigger, C-04 tier 1) |
   | `meas.ctr` | cardiac width / thoracic width, PA film | cardiomegaly > 0.50 |
   | `meas.prostate_volume` | L × W × H × 0.52 | derives `sev.prostate` grade |
   | `meas.pvr` | (pre − post) / pre × 100 | flag default > 50 ml, practice-configurable |
   | `meas.meyerding` (via `sev.meyerding`) | derives from `meas.listhesis_pct` | — |
   | `meas.nodule_avg` | (long + short) / 2 | feeds `rec.fleischner` (Chapter 4) |
   | `meas.ctss` | sum of 5 lobe scores (0–5 each) | /25 |
   | `meas.abi` | ankle systolic / brachial systolic, per side | ≥0.9 normal, 0.5–0.89 claudication, <0.5 critical ischemia; derives `sev.abi` |
   | `meas.reflux_duration` | direct measurement | significant > 0.5 s superficial / > 1.0 s deep |

   These thresholds are pinned by the Master Spec and must not drift anywhere in code (Acceptance criteria, below). Other threshold-flagged (non-derived) fields follow the same pattern without deriving a severity: `meas.adi` (>3 mm adult / >5 mm child), `meas.liver_span` (>15.5 cm), `meas.spleen_span` (>12–13 cm), `meas.cbd` (>6 mm; >8–10 mm post-cholecystectomy), `meas.portal_vein` (>13 mm), `meas.gb_wall` (>3 mm fasting), `meas.appendix_diam` (>6 mm with probe tenderness), `meas.node_short_axis` (>10 mm).

2. **Prose-harvested field completion.** When the radiologist has already written a measurement in prose ("left kidney 9.8 cm") and the corresponding `meas.<key>` field is unmet, the copilot offers a confirmable mapping ("→ fill Renal length L?"). This is Feature 7 of the AI experience spec: every harvested mapping is confirmable, never silently auto-filled — it is functionally an AI-sourced pool row (`source: ai`) staged for the same attach gesture as any other. Because the value is parsed from free text rather than read off a device or an OCR engine's scored extraction, it carries no raw extraction score of its own; it renders at **Worth a look** band by default (never Routine, since it is not radiologist-typed ground truth, and never auto-elevated to Attention absent a specific low-parse-confidence signal from the harvesting model) and follows the same three-band, offer-never-act, attach-gated contract as any other AI-sourced row.

3. **Measurement interpretation.** When any value lands in the Measurement Assistant (typed, dictated, OCR'd, or auto-calculated), the field self-annotates: value → reference band for age/sex → trend vs. priors ("Evans 0.34 — above 0.30 upper limit"; "was 0.31 in 2024 → enlarging"), and a ghost interpretation sentence is offered at the cursor in Findings, carrying the ⌗ (measurement-derived) origin tick. Reference-band values are catalog-governed config attached to the `meas.<key>` entry (versioned under catalog semver like any other governed value, MINOR bump for a new or changed band), sourced from a cited published reference recorded in the entry's metadata, and reviewed on the same quarterly calibration cadence as confidence-band thresholds (B-27) — never hardcoded in application code.

**Canal-stenosis grading is the load-bearing example of what AI must never do to a measurement (D-08, BINDING).** `sev.canal` (qualitative mild/moderate/severe, radiologist-graded) is the only ratified stenosis scale. The canal AP diameter is recorded as a plain `meas.*` value and may power a *suggested* grade ghost — offer-never-act, exactly like any other AI sentence — but it must never assert a grade. The historical hardcoded thresholds in `radiologySmartEngine.ts` (<5 mm severe / 5–8 mm moderate) are removed from the spine builders entirely; any future numeric banding is a `derives:` rule on the shared library, entered under clinical-owner sign-off, never a code constant. This is the mandatory pattern for every future numeric-derived severity scale.

**Missing-input behavior (C-04 default extends to every `derive_from` field).** If a scale's `derive_from` measurement is absent — `sev.prostate` with no volume entered, `sev.meyerding` with no listhesis % — the derived grade is simply **unavailable**. The radiologist may pick a grade manually; doing so raises a **△ non-blocking** "grade asserted without measurement" mark, never a block. No code path fabricates a default grade to fill the gap.

Image-derived direct measurement proposals (a model measuring pixels itself, rather than deriving from typed values) are **Future-tier**, gated on measured image-AI precision at a practice-acceptable false-nudge rate (§17.2) — none ship today. When one does, it follows the identical pool → confidence-band → offer-never-act → audit contract as OCR; there is no separate, lighter-weight path for image-derived measurements.

**Provenance/audit.** Every AI-sourced pool row and every AI-generated sentence built around a measurement (deterministic auto-calc's interpretation sentence, a prose-harvested mapping, a reference-band/trend ghost) is recorded under the same unconditional, undisableable, per-suggestion audit trail as OCR — engine/model/version, formula or extraction lineage, band where one applies, and disposition (accepted / accepted-then-edited / skipped / dismissed-with-reason / overridden) — per B-10; this is not a second, lighter trail, it is the same one OCR's provenance/audit paragraph describes, applied to the AI source value.

### DICOM SR (structured measurement objects from modalities)

DICOM SR is explicitly **not** today's automated measurement source (risk-governance §4.5/§4.10: "OCR, not DICOM SR, is the primary automated source"). The verdict on export/import of coded SR content is 🟠 "lossy-by-construction" — the only inert stub in-tree is `dicom_sr_export_queue`, and where incoming SR handling exists at all, **its concept codes are discarded** on the way in. This chapter's job is to state the target ingestion design so that gap closes without repeating the OCR label-matching mistake in coded form.

**Target pipeline.** When a modality (echo worklist, mammography CAD, an ultrasound worksheet, or any TID 1500 Measurement Report-emitting device) delivers a Structured Report object via PACS/Orthanc, a bridge-service parser extracts every contained numeric measurement together with its coded concept: code value, coding-scheme designator, and code meaning. Each is written to the measurement pool with `source: dicom_sr`, the coded concept populated verbatim (this is the CTO C2 "coded concept" prospective adoption, applied first to the one source that actually carries codes), and the originating SOP Instance UID recorded for medico-legal traceability (retained per the same schedule as the report itself, ≥7 years, C-11).

**Mapping to catalog slots.** Concept-code → `meas.<key>` resolution goes through a maintained **coded-concept crosswalk** (additive table, distinct from the OCR label-alias crosswalk — SR concept codes are semantically precise where OCR labels are not, so the crosswalk here is authored once per code and does not need fuzzy matching). A code with no crosswalk entry is **never** silently discarded and never guessed onto the nearest-looking slot: the pool row keeps `meas_key: null`, preserves the coded concept and device-reported value, and is flagged for content-editor triage — structurally the same non-discard rule as unmapped OCR labels, and the direct fix for the flagged lossy-by-construction behavior.

**Confidence and confirmation.** A DICOM SR value is device-measured, not guessed, so a mapped item with a known concept code renders at Routine band — but it is **not** exempt from the attach gesture. Precision of measurement is not the same thing as certainty that this SR object belongs to this patient/study/timepoint; transcription and study-mismatch risk survive device precision. Unmapped-code rows render at Attention and require triage before ever becoming insertable — same two-tier gate as OCR. No source, including a modality's own coded output, auto-inserts (D-21 admits no exception by data quality).

**Provenance/audit.** Every DICOM SR-origin pool row records the same unconditional, undisableable, per-suggestion audit fields as OCR (B-10): source device/parser version, the coded concept and originating SOP Instance UID, band, and disposition (accepted / accepted-then-edited / skipped / dismissed-with-reason / overridden) — the crosswalk resolution (or its absence) is itself part of that provenance record, so a later crosswalk correction can never obscure what a signed report actually used.

**Build status.** The bridge-service parser and both crosswalks are net-new build (not yet assigned a lettered strand in any source); Chapter 9 owns how the crosswalk tables are created under the two sanctioned migration paths. Until it ships, DICOM SR ingestion does not exist in production and OCR remains the primary automated path — this chapter does not claim otherwise.

### Follow-up (longitudinal linkage, comparison, and threshold-triggered recommendations)

**Lesion registry.** A tracked lesion is a radiologist-curated identity (`radiology_lesions`, extended rather than replaced — the existing manual-only `POST` into `viewer_measurements` and the inert OHIF/Weasis measurement-import bridge, CTO §4.5 MED, are exactly the gap this closes going forward) that a measurement pool row can be linked to via a `lesion_id`. The copilot may **propose** a lesion-identity match across studies — same organ, comparable location, size trajectory consistent with the same target — but it writes only a non-authoritative `suggested_lesion_id`. Promoting a suggestion to an actual `lesion_id` link, or unlinking one, is **always an explicit human act, never silent** (§4.3-13/§12.7) — this is the one place in the whole measurement pipeline where a wrong automatic decision (misregistration: attributing today's lesion 2 to yesterday's lesion 3) is more dangerous than a missed one, so it gets the strictest gate in the chapter, stricter even than ordinary offer-never-act.

**The story.** For every tracked lesion, the Measure stage's stage rail shows one row: name, a sparkline of size/measurement across every prior study, current value, trend arrow, and a response-criterion chip (RECIST-style, where applicable). Every sparkline point is source-linked back to the study and report line it came from; clicking one opens that line (and the archived image, where available). Today's measurement updates the story live as it lands in the pool.

**Comparison and delta computation.** Delta arithmetic is deterministic, never LLM-estimated (extending C-04's principle: anything expressible from structured data is compiled to a predicate, not asked of a model) — `delta = current − previous`, `%change = delta / previous × 100`, computed directly from stored pool rows linked to the same `lesion_id`, ordered by study date. Multi-lesion response-criterion arithmetic (e.g. a RECIST-style target-lesion sum against baseline and nadir) follows the same rule: it is computed, not generated, and its arithmetic is inspectable on a second `E` press (the same explainability contract as any AI artifact, B-13/Chapter 7). Criterion definitions and their numeric cutoffs are named, versioned, catalog-governed config with a version chip on the criterion name — the same pattern already pinned for Fleischner 2017 (C-21) — never hardcoded, so a practice can adopt a different or updated response-criterion version without a code change.

**Delta brief and longitudinal ghost.** On study open, pre-staged by the Zero-Click loop (Chapter 15), the copilot shows a three-line delta brief in the Orient stage — *unchanged* (collapsed), *changed* ("lesion 8 mm → was 6 mm, +33% in 14 months"), *new/resolved* — each line linking to the prior's exact sentence; this lives at the top of the prior-timeline rail. While writing, if the radiologist addresses a tracked lesion, a ghost offers the longitudinal sentence ("Target lesion 3: 22 mm, previously 26 mm (−15%); overall course regressing since 2025-01") carrying the ↺ (prior-derived) origin tick, dual-sourced and one keystroke from both original values (Law 5) — like every AI sentence, it requires the explicit accept gesture before it becomes report text.

**Completeness.** Any tracked lesion left unaddressed at sign raises a **◌ non-blocking** completeness mark (feature 4's gutter mechanics, owned by Chapters 3/7) — it never blocks sign by itself, consistent with the small-and-strict sign gate (D-20).

**Threshold-triggered recommendations.** This chapter supplies the deterministic measurement inputs; Chapter 4 owns the recommendation mechanics themselves. `meas.nodule_avg` feeds `rec.fleischner`'s `{interval}` slot (AI-computed, radiologist-confirmed, B-06). `meas.midline_shift ≥ 5 mm` is a hard, deterministic critical trigger firing `crit.midline_shift` → `rec.urgent` (never LLM-gated, C-04 tier 1). Other follow-up codes trigger off finding/scale state rather than a raw measurement (ring lesion → `rec.fup_3m`; BI-RADS 3 → `rec.fup_mmg6m`, bound to the `sev.birads` management line per D-16c) and are Chapter 4's content, not this chapter's.

**As-built backend and flag status.** `/api/radiology-tumor` (follow-up CRUD plus a timeline grouped by `tumorLabel`) is the existing Tumor Follow-up panel backend and is the canonical target for the lesion-registry timeline described above — `tumorLabel` becomes the lesion-identity key both AI-proposed matches and human links resolve to; extend it rather than building a parallel store. The Organ-tab surface that currently exposes this is gated by the legacy client-only `tumorFollowup` `localStorage` flag (alongside `spineIntelligence`/`brainIntelligence`); per **D-24**, this migrates to a server-side `ff_radiology_*` key the next time the Follow-up Comparison surface is touched — track this as this chapter's dependency on Chapter 8, not a reason to keep shipping a client-only flag.

**Prior-report identity.** Longitudinal linkage across studies for the same patient depends on the study set being correctly identified as the same patient's history in the first place; that matching is by `patientId` primary key, never by name (B-25, owned by Chapter 11) — restated here only because Follow-up's entire premise depends on it holding.

### Acceptance criteria

- Every measurement that reaches report text — manual, OCR, AI-derived, or DICOM SR — carries a `source` tag, a confidence band where one applies, and an audit record, and reached the report only through an explicit radiologist gesture; no code path auto-inserts at any confidence tier (D-21).
- Every `meas.<key>` field accepts only its whitelisted unit; no free-text unit entry ships anywhere in the Measurement Assistant.
- Auto-calc formulas and thresholds match the pinned table above exactly (Evans >0.30, midline shift ≥5 mm critical, CTR >0.50 PA, prostate ×0.52, CTSS /25, ABI 0.9/0.5 bands, reflux 0.5 s/1.0 s) — no alternate numbers exist anywhere in code.
- The reading surface shows only the three canonical confidence bands (Routine / Worth a look / Attention) with the grey/amber palette; no percentages, no High/Medium/Low labels, no green, anywhere a measurement's confidence is displayed.
- `radiologySmartEngine.ts` contains no numeric mm-threshold stenosis grading; canal stenosis renders only the qualitative `sev.canal` scale, with any numeric measurement offered strictly as a suggested-grade ghost.
- A `derive_from` scale with a missing input measurement renders its grade unavailable rather than a fabricated default; a manual override raises a non-blocking △, never a block.
- Neither the OCR label-alias crosswalk nor the DICOM SR concept-code crosswalk ever silently discards an unmapped value; every unmapped item is preserved in the pool with its raw label/value/coded-concept and flagged for content-editor triage.
- Lesion identity linking and unlinking across studies is always an explicit human gesture — no automatic linking ships regardless of AI match confidence.
- A tracked lesion left unaddressed at sign raises a non-blocking ◌ completeness mark and never blocks sign by itself.
- Delta and response-criterion arithmetic are deterministic, reproducible from stored pool rows, and inspectable on a second `E` press — never generated by a model.
- A signed report's measurement values are frozen at sign (per Chapter 2's snapshot semantics) and never re-derived from a live catalog, crosswalk, or reference-band change after signing.

### Build order

1. Land the `meas.*` catalog import (Chapter 1 dependency) so units, auto-calc formulas, and thresholds exist as governed, versioned data rather than hardcoded constants.
2. Build the measurement pool table (Strand C, `ff_radiology_measurement_pool`) with bigint identity PK and nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id` per D-37, the `source` enum, confidence/band columns, coded-concept columns, and `attached_finding_instance_id`/`lesion_id` FKs; wire attach/detach against `report_finding_instances` (Chapter 2 dependency).
3. Wire manual entry through the Measurement Assistant against the pool and the unit whitelist; add the optional plausibility-range △ lint.
4. Fix the OCR pipeline: replace lowercased label-string matching with the label-alias crosswalk; remove any medium-plus auto-insert path (D-21); wire the three-band confidence mapping; gate Attention-band rows behind mandatory field-level review before Insert appears.
5. Wire deterministic auto-calc as pool-row derivations recorded with formula id/version and input row lineage; remove the hardcoded canal-stenosis thresholds from `radiologySmartEngine.ts` and replace with the `sev.canal` suggestion-only ghost (D-08).
6. Build the DICOM SR bridge parser and coded-concept crosswalk behind a new flag; wire the same non-discard/triage behavior as step 4's OCR crosswalk.
7. Build the lesion registry and Follow-up Comparison surface on `radiology_lesions` + the extended `/api/radiology-tumor` timeline; wire the delta brief, longitudinal ghost, and response-criterion chip — depends on Chapter 7 (ghost text/card/origin-tick mechanics) and Chapter 4 (`rec.fleischner`/`rec.urgent` triggering); migrate the `tumorFollowup` client flag to `ff_radiology_*` (Chapter 8 dependency) in the same pass.
8. Wire measurement-instance sign-time snapshot and version stamping alongside Chapter 2's finding-instance stamping, in the same transaction as `final_report_id`.

---

## Chapter 6 — Impression lifecycle

The Impression is the report's clinical verdict, and unlike Findings/Technique/Recommendation it has two independent generation paths that must never be confused: a **deterministic path**, where each fired finding or combo contributes its own `impression_fragment` directly into the live `ReportTextState.impression` array, tier-ordered critical-first; and an **AI-drafted path**, a separate synthesized narrative offered only as ghost text, sentence-accepted, never auto-inserted. Both paths terminate in the same array and are subject to the same manual-edit-survives-regeneration contract that Chapters 2 and 4 establish for Findings and Recommendations — including the as-built shadowing bug (Bug-001) whose fix is a permanent naming discipline every future contributor to this surface must honor. This chapter owns impression fragment assembly, worst-first tiering and grouping, AI grounding/hallucination guards for the ghost impression, the exact regeneration/dirty-state contract, impression-specific lint gates, and what freezes at sign. It depends on Chapter 2 (finding-lifecycle) for the finding-instance state machine, the criticality tag that drives tiering, and the sign-time snapshot mechanics; on Chapter 4 (recommendation-lifecycle) as the sibling mechanism sharing the same merge primitives (do not conflate the two — a `rec.*` code and an `impression_fragment` are different fields on the same finding, rendered into different report sections); and on the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter for the general offer-never-act/provisional/audit-trail laws this chapter applies specifically to the ghost impression.

### Quick Select

Every catalog finding entry carries an `impression_fragment` field as one of its six elements (Master Spec §5.1; D-13 canonical field name), using the identical `{slot}` grammar as `default_sentence`: slot names must match a bound parameter or measurement key exactly, and unfilled optional slots collapse without leaving dangling punctuation. A combo tile's members contribute their own fragments the same way; a combo that shares a laterality slot across members (e.g. a side-linked renal-colic combo) fills that shared slot once and every member's fragment reflects it consistently — there is no path by which a combo can render two fragments disagreeing on side.

**Firing an impression fragment is a live insertion, not a ghost offer.** When a radiologist fires a Quick Select tile or a combo, the click itself is the explicit gesture (P6/offer-never-act is satisfied by the click); the fragment merges directly into `ReportTextState.impression: string[]` via the same `mergeImpression`/`removeImpression` primitives (`quickFindingsMerge.ts`, re-exported through `renderEngine.ts`) that Findings/Technique/Recommendation use, driven by `applyRenderedTransition`'s remove-then-merge cycle (Chapter 2, Rendered). This is deliberately distinct from the AI section's ghost-drafted narrative, which is never inserted without a second, separate acceptance gesture — the two must not be implemented as one code path with a flag, because their sign-gate consequences differ (see Finalization).

**The `impression_fragment: null` case — the primary suppression mechanic.** A finding may declare a null fragment, meaning it contributes nothing standalone to the Impression and instead folds under a header finding's own fragment (the canonical example: a disc-desiccation finding folds under the spondylosis header finding it accompanies, per Seed Spec §3.2). A null-fragment finding still renders its own sentence in Findings — folding is an Impression-only suppression, never a Findings-section suppression. Where the header relationship is structural (a combo whose header member's fragment has a slot the folded member's parameter fills), the folded information surfaces inside the header's rendered line; where no such slot exists, the folded finding is present in Findings and simply absent from Impression by design — this is not a bug, and lint must never flag a null-fragment finding as an impression-completeness gap (the same way `normal_variant` entries never raise a ◌, B-17's sibling rule for this field).

### Rules

Impression assembly is deterministic and code-owned, not model-owned, for the same reason the sign gate's blocking checks are deterministic (C-04): a clinical verdict's structure must not depend on model behavior. Four rules apply, in this order of authority:

1. **Worst-first tiering (B-14).** Fragments order **critical > significant > rest**, using each contributing instance's `criticality` tag (Chapter 2) as the sort key — `significant_if` (C-13) is the only thing that promotes a fragment into tier 2; `critical_if`/registry membership is what promotes into tier 1. Tiering is implemented as **insertion-point selection**, exactly as Chapter 4 implements it for recommendations: a newly-rendered critical-tier line inserts after the last existing critical-tier line, not via a full array resort on every render. This matters for the same reason it matters for recommendations (B-18): an unrelated edit elsewhere in the report must never visibly reshuffle lines the radiologist has already read or edited.
2. **Structural grouping.** Multi-level or multi-organ disease groups into a single line only where the grouping is structural — a level-grid combo (`shared_slots` across disc levels) or an organ-composite combo renders one fragment for the whole group by construction, because it is one finding instance, not several. Free-text regrouping of independently-fired, separate instances into one prose line is not a deterministic operation (there is no safe machine rule for how to phrase "L3-4 and L4-5" into a single clinical sentence from two unrelated instances) — that kind of grouping belongs to the AI-drafted narrative (below), offered as ghost text, never silently performed by the deterministic merge engine.
3. **Suppression of redundant fragments.** Two distinct suppression mechanisms apply, and they are not the same rule: (a) null-fragment folding (Quick Select, above) suppresses a fragment before it is ever a candidate line; (b) exact-text collapse, the same incidental protection `mergeImpression` already provides for Findings/Recommendation — if two different instances happen to render the byte-identical fragment string, the second is not re-added as a duplicate line. This is not semantic deduplication (two fragments that say materially the same thing in different words both render); only literal duplicate text collapses.
4. **Normal-study exclusivity is a hard constraint on this assembly, not a lint afterthought.** A `*.normal_study` instance's fragment (`{{tpl.normal.<study>}}`) can be a Draft-state candidate only when no other non-`normal_variant` instance is also Draft-state; if both are present the renderer does not choose for the radiologist (P6 forbids that) — both lines render, and the resulting internally-false Impression is caught as a ✕-blocking contradiction at the sign gate (Finalization, below; mechanism defined fully in Chapter 2, Rendered).

**The BI-RADS special case.** Mammography's Impression line format is fixed and structural: `"BI-RADS {category} — {bound management line}"`. Overall BI-RADS is always the worse of the two sides' category assessments, auto-assembled with per-side lines beneath it; a category can never render without its bound `sev.birads` management text (B-05) — there is no seed path, insert path, or sign path that produces the numeral without the sentence. A suspicious descriptor (e.g. spiculated margin) paired with a BI-RADS category ≤ 2 is a hard BLOCK, not a lint suggestion, per the mammography study's `hard_rules` (§3.10). This pattern is mandatory for any future TI-RADS/PI-RADS-class scale entering the catalog (B-05); an implementer adding such a scale must bind its management text on the scale value the same way, never as a separate omittable `recommendation_code`.

### AI

The AI-drafted impression is a second, independent generation path, triggered when the cursor enters Impression **and** the report already has written findings (Conclude stage, §9.2). It synthesizes a full narrative from the report's current findings, significance-ordered, rendered in the radiologist's historical impression style (`radiologist_style_preferences`) as dimmed ghost text — never inserted, per Law 1. Acceptance is strictly sentence-by-sentence: `Tab` accepts one sentence, `Shift+Tab` rejects one, typing overwrites; each accepted sentence is one atomic undo step (Law 8), and each accepted sentence carries a provisional marker cleared only by an explicit edit or explicit section confirmation — never by dwell or viewport time (C-12, binding). The legacy "Insert into Impression" block-transplant flow is retired; any AI output that isn't the inline ghost draft (a refinement, a differential paragraph) lands in the standard right-rail margin card with its canonical Insert / Explain / Dismiss anatomy, never a bespoke Impression-only widget.

**Grounding requirement (the hallucination guard).** This is the load-bearing rule for this section, stated once, precisely: **every ghost-impression sentence must be traceable to one or more Draft-state finding instances already present in this report** — their fragments, measurements, severities, and lateralities as currently rendered. The drafting layer may compress, reorder for narrative flow, and rephrase into house style, but it may never introduce a diagnosis, a measurement, a laterality, or a level not backed by an existing instance. This is the same "must cite or don't render" gate (B-13) the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter owns generally, applied here concretely: a candidate sentence that cannot be grounded to a specific instance does not render as ghost text at all — it is silently dropped from the draft, not rendered with a caveat. `E` on a ghost sentence shows which instance(s) it derives from (the evidence view); a second `E` shows model/provider/version/prompt lineage. Every rendered ghost sentence — accepted or not — is recorded in the unconditional AI audit trail (B-10: band, raw score, sources, engine/version, disposition), because grounding failures are exactly the kind of defect the quarterly calibration review (B-27) needs visibility into even when nothing was ever accepted.

**`/polish` refinement carries its own hard constraint.** Running `/polish` on an already-drafted or radiologist-written Impression returns a **tracked-changes diff**, individually acceptable per change, never a side-by-side block rewrite. The refiner must preserve every clinical assertion in the text it touches; it may not add a finding, soften certainty language, or change a laterality or a measurement value. A refinement that would do any of these is blocked outright with "cannot refine without changing meaning" rather than silently producing a weaker or altered clinical claim — this constraint is enforced on the refiner itself, not left to radiologist review, because a certainty-softened or laterality-flipped Impression is exactly the failure mode P8 (must-cite) and the grounding rule above exist to prevent.

**Adjacent Conclude-stage features, referenced not owned here.** A differential paragraph (`/differential` or an ambient pattern match) is a separate margin-card feature, capped at five candidates, insertable into Impression on `Enter` in the radiologist's phrasing style; recommendation cards trigger off Impression content once written. Both are owned by the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter (differential mechanics, margin-card anatomy) and the recommendation-lifecycle chapter (recommendation triggering) respectively — this chapter's territory stops at the Impression text itself.

**Reproducibility of generated impression text.** The as-built `PATCH /impression-rules`-style mutable rule/template store (mutating `conditions`/`generatedText` in place with no version history) is a CTO §4.3 LOW finding: without a versions table, a previously generated impression cannot be reproduced against the rule state that produced it. This mechanism, along with the legacy `/api/smart-radiology/ai-impressions` → `ai_impressions` table it is associated with, is **frozen** under the same posture as any other legacy AI surface (D-30): no new writes, no new rule-editing UI built against it. Reproducibility going forward comes from two things this chapter already requires, not from a new rules-version table: (a) deterministic fragment content is catalog data, governed by catalog semver (Chapter 1, B-03) — the fragment wording used to render an instance is always attributable to a specific `catalog_version`; (b) the sign-time snapshot (Signing, below) freezes the exact rendered text and stamps `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` on the signed row (D-18, C-02). A signed report's Impression is reproducible because it is a frozen copy plus its generating versions, not because the generation *rules* are separately versioned — no engineer should build a parallel rules-version table to answer this finding.

### Manual editing

Free-text editing of the Impression — typing directly into the section, overwriting a Quick-Select-fired line, or editing an accepted ghost sentence — is first-class and permanent: an edited line is never a special case requiring a different code path, and it survives every later regeneration by construction, not by exception-handling.

**The exact regeneration/dirty-state contract.** This is the precise mechanism every engineer touching this surface implements, stated exhaustively:

1. **Trigger.** Any re-render of a finding instance — a parameter/severity/laterality change, a Quick Select deselect/reselect, a combo fire or un-fire, an AI ghost sentence accepted or rejected — invokes `applyRenderedTransition(state, prev, next)` for that instance.
2. **Removal step.** The reducer computes the instance's *previously* rendered impression line and calls `removeImpression(lines, previousLine)`, which removes the **first exact line match**. If the radiologist has edited that line — even a single character — it no longer matches verbatim, and removal silently no-ops. The edited line is never deleted out from under the radiologist.
3. **Merge step.** The reducer then calls `mergeImpression(lines, newLine)` with the instance's newly rendered line, which appends it unless an identical line already exists verbatim in the array.
4. **The documented dual-line edge case.** Because step 2 can no-op while step 3 still runs unconditionally, a parameter change on an instance whose impression line was previously hand-edited can produce **both** the radiologist's edited line and the freshly re-rendered line in the array simultaneously. This is accepted, documented behavior — identical in shape to Chapter 4's recommendation edge case — and it is never to be "fixed" with fuzzy or semantic text matching; fuzzy matching reopens exactly the risk this exact-match discipline exists to close (silently discarding a radiologist's edited clinical wording because a heuristic judged it "close enough" to the old generated text). The available mitigation is procedural, not algorithmic: editing an impression line moves the owning instance from Draft-Unconfirmed to Draft-Confirmed (C-12); a further parameter edit against a Draft-Confirmed instance is a signal the radiologist must reconcile manually, surfaced as a △ inconsistency mark at the lint gutter, never auto-repaired by the merge engine.
5. **AI ghost sentences follow the identical contract once accepted.** An accepted ghost sentence becomes an ordinary line in the same array, subject to steps 1–4 exactly as a Quick-Select-fired line is — there is no separate merge path for AI-originated impression text. This is what makes "provisional until touched" (Law 3) and "edit survives regeneration" (this contract) compose correctly: touching an accepted ghost sentence simultaneously clears its provisional marker (C-12) and enrolls it in the exact-match protection above.

**`removeImpressionShadowing` — the as-built regression this chapter must permanently guard against (Bug-001).** A local function `function removeImpression(index)` was once declared inside `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx` as the by-index UI handler for Quick Select tile deselection. Because JavaScript/TypeScript lexical scoping resolves a local declaration over an import of the same name, this local function **shadowed** the imported pure `removeImpression(lines, line)` from `quickFindingsMerge.ts` — deselecting a tile silently called the wrong function, and the tile's generated impression bullet was never removed. The fix is a permanent, non-negotiable naming contract, not a one-time patch:

- The local by-index UI delete handler in `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx` is named `deleteImpressionLineAt` — it must never be renamed `removeImpression` or any name colliding with a `quickFindingsMerge` export.
- `renderEngine.ts` re-exports the pure merge/removal functions under **aliased names** (e.g. `_removeImpression`) specifically so that a future local declaration of the unaliased name cannot silently shadow the import again — this is a structural prevention, not a lint rule that could be disabled or missed.
- `removeImpressionShadowing.test.ts` is the permanent regression guard: because the Workspace component itself is not unit-testable under the current vitest config (the root config cannot resolve `@/` aliases), this test combines a **source-text structural check** (a regex over the component's source asserting no local `removeImpression` declaration exists) with a **behavioral round-trip test** against the pure functions directly. Any future change to the merge/removal primitives, or to the Workspace component's handler names, must keep this test passing without modification to its shadowing assertion — if the assertion itself needs editing to make a change pass, that change is very likely reintroducing the shadow.

An engineer extending Quick Select, the impression array, or `renderEngine.ts` must treat this naming discipline as load-bearing: it is the entire reason manual-edit protection and deselect-removal both keep working simultaneously.

### Finalization

The Impression participates in the Verify & Sign stage's lint summary exactly as the other three sections do (Chapter 2), rendered as a keyboard-navigable jump-list of unresolved items, never a modal wall. Impression-specific lint checks, by tier:

| Tier | Check | Blocks sign? |
|---|---|---|
| ✕ contradiction | Normal-study fragment present alongside any positive-finding fragment (Rules, rule 4) | Yes |
| ✕ contradiction | Laterality mismatch between a Findings-section sentence and its corresponding Impression fragment | Yes |
| ✕ contradiction | A BI-RADS category rendered without its bound management line, or a suspicious descriptor with BI-RADS ≤ 2 | Yes (structurally prevented at assembly per Rules; still checked as a defense-in-depth gate) |
| ✕ contradiction | Any untouched-provisional AI ghost sentence remaining in the Impression array | Yes (Law 3/C-12 — an accepted-but-unedited-and-unconfirmed ghost sentence is exactly an "unreviewed provisional span" per the sign gate's own definition) |
| △ inconsistency | Impression phrasing diverges from a measurement-field value it references (text-vs-field mismatch) | No — dismiss-with-reason |
| ◌ completeness | Not applicable to null-fragment or `normal_variant` findings by design (Quick Select, above) | No — never raised for these |

Mammography carries one additional study-level hard rule enforced before sign: composition (`sev.acr_density`) must be recorded and the study must end with a BI-RADS assessment, overall = worst side — both are pre-conditions for the Impression to even reach a signable state, not post-hoc lint. No batch finalization exists for any of this (D-20): every report's Impression is locked one at a time, by the signing radiologist, never by a bulk operation.

Locking itself does nothing special to the Impression beyond what Chapter 2 already does uniformly to all four rendered sections — there is no separate "lock impression" action. Once the ✕ tier is clear (or every ✕ item has been resolved by editing), `Ctrl+Enter` or voice "sign" (identical gate, D-09/master spec §11.1) proceeds.

### Signing

At sign, the Impression is covered by the same snapshot-and-stamp transaction Chapter 2 defines for structured findings generally (C-02, D-18) — this section states only what is specific to Impression text, not the mechanics again.

- `radiology_report_drafts.impression` is a JSON array serialized into a **TEXT column** (not `jsonb` — a documented quirk; do not assume "all JSON on drafts is jsonb" when writing migrations or readers against this column). This array is a **working representation only**. At finalize, the assembled Impression text is folded into `patient_reports.body`, the sole authoritative, append-only, content-hashed record (D-19) — the signed artifact is prose within `body`, not a queryable bullet array; any future feature needing structured post-sign access to individual impression lines must be built against the `report_finding_instances` snapshot rows (Chapter 2), not against a resurrected array on the signed side.
- `catalog_version`, `template_version`, and `render_engine_version` are stamped on `patient_reports` in the same transaction that sets `final_report_id` (D-18) — this is what makes a signed Impression's exact wording reproducible against catalog history without a separate impression-rules version table (see AI, above).
- **Immutability.** Once signed, Impression text is frozen exactly as the rest of `body` is; there is no in-place edit path. Any correction — including a missed critical finding discovered post-sign, a wording correction, or a laterality fix — goes exclusively through `report_amendments` (D-20), one repeatable chain, never a second amendment mechanism specific to Impression.
- **AI never signs (B-25).** There is no confidence threshold, flag, or code path that allows an AI-drafted ghost sentence to reach a signed report without having first passed through the Finalization gate above — by construction, every sentence in a signed Impression has been through at least one radiologist edit-or-confirm gesture, because an untouched-provisional span is structurally sign-blocking, not merely discouraged.
- **Authorship.** The Impression carries no separate authorship record of its own; it is covered entirely by the report-level, server-derived authorship stamp (D-33) applied at finalize.

### Acceptance criteria

- Quick-Select-fired and combo-fired impression fragments insert live into the Impression array on the firing gesture itself; the AI-drafted narrative is a wholly separate path that never inserts without a distinct sentence-level acceptance gesture.
- A finding with `impression_fragment: null` never produces a standalone Impression line and is never flagged as an impression-completeness gap by lint.
- Impression tiering is critical > significant > rest, implemented as insertion-point selection, never a full resort; grouping of separate (non-combo) instances into one prose line happens only in the AI-drafted narrative, never in the deterministic merge.
- A normal-study fragment can never coexist, unflagged, with a positive-finding fragment; the conflict is a ✕-blocking lint mark, never silently resolved by the renderer.
- No BI-RADS category can render in an Impression without its bound management line, under any code path.
- Every rendered ghost-impression sentence is traceable to a specific Draft-state finding instance; an ungroundable candidate sentence does not render, with no exception, and is still audit-recorded.
- `/polish` on Impression text never adds a finding, softens certainty, or changes a laterality/measurement; a refinement that would do so is blocked with an explicit "cannot refine without changing meaning" message, not silently altered.
- An edited (non-exact-match) Impression line is never silently removed by a later regeneration cycle, including in the documented dual-line edge case.
- No local declaration named `removeImpression` (or any name colliding with a `quickFindingsMerge` export) exists anywhere in `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx`; `removeImpressionShadowing.test.ts` passes unmodified in its shadowing assertion.
- No untouched-provisional AI ghost sentence can remain in a signed Impression; sign is structurally blocked while one exists.
- A signed report's Impression text is immutable; every post-sign correction goes through `report_amendments`.

### Build order

1. **Prerequisite: Chapter 2's finding-instance state machine and `applyRenderedTransition`.** Impression assembly is a specialization of the same reducer; it cannot be built before the general remove-then-merge cycle exists.
2. **Deterministic array assembly.** Wire `impression_fragment` rendering into `mergeImpression`/`removeImpression` with tier-based insertion-point selection (Rules rules 1 and 3); this alone (no AI, no null-fragment folding yet) delivers a working, ordered, manually-editable Impression section.
3. **Null-fragment folding and structural grouping.** Wire the fold-under-header behavior for null-fragment findings and combo-level structural grouping; both are catalog-data-driven (Chapter 1 dependency) and require no new merge primitives.
4. **Shadowing regression guard.** Land `removeImpressionShadowing.test.ts` (or verify it) before any refactor of `RadiologyReportingWorkspace.tsx`'s deselect handling or `renderEngine.ts`'s export aliasing — this is a prerequisite gate for touching this surface at all, not a follow-on task.
5. **BI-RADS special-case rendering.** Implement the `"BI-RADS {category} — {management line}"` fixed format and the mammography hard-block rules; depends on Chapter 4's mandatory-management-binding pattern (B-05) already existing on the scale side.
6. **AI ghost-drafted narrative.** Build the Conclude-stage trigger, sentence-by-sentence acceptance, and — before this ships to any radiologist — the grounding/hallucination guard (must-cite-or-don't-render) and the `/polish` hard-constraint refiner; these two guards are prerequisites for enabling the feature flag, not hardening work done after launch.
7. **Lint wiring.** Implement the impression-specific ✕/△/◌ table against the ai-interaction-lifecycle chapter's gutter-mark mechanics; the untouched-provisional-span check depends on step 6 already tagging ghost-originated lines.
8. **Sign-time integration.** No new mechanism — verify the Impression array is included in whatever assembles `patient_reports.body` and that the TEXT-vs-jsonb column quirk is documented for the finalize path's own tests.

Steps 2–4 should land together and are independent of the AI feature flag; a fully manual, deterministic Impression section is shippable and safe on its own. Step 6 must not ship ahead of its grounding guard — an ungrounded ghost impression is a medico-legal hazard, not a rough edge to smooth out post-launch.

---

## Chapter 7 — AI interaction lifecycle

Every AI surface in the Reporting Workspace — ghost text, margin card, gutter mark, interrupt banner, and the voice layer riding on top of them — obeys exactly one lifecycle: a suggestion renders, the radiologist accepts, modifies, or rejects it, that disposition feeds a strictly firewalled learning loop, and every step writes to an unconditional audit record regardless of outcome. This chapter is the single normative source for that lifecycle: the eight laws that bound all AI behavior, the four fixed surfaces and their gestures, the confidence-band and explainability contracts, the provider-routing and prompt-template conventions that produce a suggestion, and the exact audit-record shape a signed report must be able to defend in a medico-legal review. Nothing here weakens Part 0 §0.6's safety rules — this chapter is where most of those rules get their enforcing mechanism. Chapters 3–6 own how AI proposes into a specific catalog object (finding, parameter, recommendation, measurement, impression); this chapter owns how any such proposal is offered, actioned, learned from, and audited, independent of what it contains. Voice dictation is treated throughout as a channel that produces the same suggestion/acceptance/audit events as any other AI surface, not a separate system.

### Suggestion

**The eight laws.** Every AI-originated artifact, on every surface, obeys all eight without exception:

1. **Offer, never act.** AI output is a proposal in visually distinct provisional styling; it never inserts, deletes, scrolls, moves the cursor, or changes report state without an explicit radiologist gesture.
2. **Silence is a first-class output.** Below the usefulness threshold the AI renders nothing — no empty panels, no "no suggestions found."
3. **Provisional until touched.** Accepted AI text carries a provisional marker until edited or the section is explicitly confirmed (C-12: dwell/viewport time never clears it); sign-off is structurally blocked while any span remains untouched-provisional.
4. **Three-band confidence, no raw percentages at reading level** (D-22): *Routine / Worth a look / Attention*; the raw score is one click deep and in the audit trail.
5. **Every claim is traceable.** A suggestion must answer "why do you say that?" in one interaction; a suggestion that cannot cite a source does not render at all.
6. **The radiologist's correction outranks the model.** Edits, rejections, and overrides are the highest-value signal; the copilot visibly stops re-suggesting what a given radiologist rejects.
7. **Interruption budget.** Only a confirmed-pattern critical finding may use the interrupt channel; nothing AI-related fires during open-mic dictation except critical escalation.
8. **Reversible, always.** Every AI acceptance is one atomic undo step.

These are the operative form of Part 0 §0.6 rules 1, 3, 7, 8, 10, 11, 12 as they apply to AI specifically; where this chapter states a mechanism, that mechanism is the enforcement referenced by §0.6's acceptance criteria.

**Exactly four surfaces.** Every one of the AI features owned by Chapters 3–6 renders through one of these four; no fifth surface may be added without amending this table.

| Surface | Where it renders | Accept gesture | Reject/dismiss gesture | Explain gesture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Ghost text** | inline at the cursor's natural insertion point, dimmed, with an origin tick (⌗ measurement-derived, ↺ prior-derived, ◉ image-derived) | `Tab` (accepts one sentence at a time) | `Shift+Tab` (skip, logged as suppression signal) or type/dictate over it | `E` |
| **Margin card** | right stage rail, one card anatomy (claim → confidence band chip → source chip(s) → Insert/Explain/Dismiss) | `Enter` or the Insert control | `X` (dismiss; reason required only where the card is safety-relevant, e.g. escalation decline) | `E` (evidence), second `E` (model/version/prompt lineage) |
