| **Gutter mark** | left margin of the offending line, one of three glyphs ✕ (blocking contradiction) / △ (non-blocking inconsistency) / ◌ (completeness) | `F8` cycles marks, `Enter` jumps; **fix** where deterministic | dismiss-with-reason (one tap, mandatory reason, audit-trailed) | — (the mark's tooltip states what it checked) |
| **Interrupt banner** | full-weight overlay, reserved for confirmed critical-pattern escalation only | one keystroke launches the critical-results workflow (Chapter 3 owns the workflow itself) | decline, mandatory reason | — |

Voice-originated text (domain-normalized dictation, template-slot fills, ghost sentences produced from a voice command) is never a fifth surface: it renders through ghost text with the same `Tab`/`Shift+Tab`/type-over contract, distinguished only by amber provisional styling instead of grey dimming for corrections beyond trivial unit/vocabulary normalization (D-9). Voice "sign" and the keystroke `Ctrl+Enter` hit the identical sign gate (D-9, D-38) — there is no separate, looser voice path to a signed report.

**The copilot panel has exactly three states**, applying to the margin-card home specifically (ghost text, gutter marks, and the interrupt banner are always-on and are not gated by this state machine):

- **Quiet** — collapsed, one slim presence line ("Copilot: watching · nothing to add").
- **Offering** — 1 to 3 margin cards, never more; overflow is not queued behind the cap, it is simply not generated for that render cycle — the ranking rule below decides which candidates survive.
- **Asked** — an explicit request (`/ask`, `/differential`, `/polish`, `/literature`, `/history`, or their voice equivalents) is in flight; a visible thinking state, cancellable with `Esc`.

The panel never exceeds one screen-height; anything that would overflow goes to the ledger (`F9`), not to a second scroll region. The panel's persistent footer line always names the answering engine (e.g. "Local · on-device") — routing is invisible to the radiologist, but the answering engine is never hidden (D-31).

**Ranking and per-surface caps.** Where more candidates exist than a surface's cap allows, ranking and truncation are fixed as follows — this chapter is the owning decision point because no source document specifies it:

- **Margin cards (Offering state, cap 3):** rank by descending confidence-band severity (Attention > Worth a look > Routine) — a card the model is least sure about is more useful to see than one it is confident about, since confident suggestions are cheaper to verify by eye — with ties broken by recency of the report edit that triggered the candidate. Truncated candidates are dropped for that render cycle, not queued; a later edit that keeps the same candidate relevant will re-offer it.
- **Ghost text:** exactly one finding sentence offered at a time, at the natural insertion point; the copilot does not pre-stage a queue of pending ghosts.
- **Differential candidates (within an expanded differential card):** capped at 5, ranked by the same descending-severity rule.
- **Literature entries (`/literature`, explicit-only):** capped at 3, ranked by evidence hierarchy (guideline > society statement > peer-reviewed review), most recent year first within a tier.
- **Gutter marks:** uncapped — every open ✕/△/◌ is shown; `F8` cycles them in document order, not severity order, so the radiologist reads the report the way it will be read, not the way the model ranks it.

**Grounding is a rendering gate, not a quality signal.** A candidate suggestion that cannot cite a specific, on-screen-inspectable source (a report sentence, a measurement field, a prior-report line, an image region, a guideline row) is discarded before render — it never reaches Quiet-to-Offering transition, never becomes a ghost, and is not logged as a rendered suggestion (only as a discarded-candidate event in the generation pipeline's own internal log, which is not part of the per-suggestion audit trail defined under Audit below, since nothing was ever offered to a radiologist). Unverifiable citations never render; a generated-prose justification ("the pattern is consistent with...") is never treated as a source — only concrete, dereferenceable evidence counts (Law 5, B-13).

**Interruption budget — the three notification channels (D-23).** AI events reach the radiologist through exactly one of three channels; there is no fourth "toast" channel and no AI code path may introduce one:

1. **Interrupt** — full-weight, acknowledge-required. Reserved for a confirmed-pattern critical finding recognized in the radiologist's own written text (Chapter 3's escalation workflow); nothing else in the AI system may use this channel.
2. **Edge glow** — ambient, ignorable, self-clearing. "AI draft ready," "measurements extracted," "report delivered" are edge-glow events.
3. **Ledger** (`F9`, pull-only) — accumulates everything else: overflowed margin cards, completed background asks, suppressed-suggestion notices. Nothing in the ledger ever animates.

During dictation (mic open) and within 5 seconds of active typing, channels 2 and 3 are silent by rule — zero non-critical AI interruptions during this window is enforced as a bug class, not a best-effort target. An open mic silences all non-critical AI output outright, including ghost text generation for sections other than the one being dictated into.

**Standing refusals and scope boundary.** The AI never renders prognosis to a patient, never communicates externally without an explicit radiologist action, and returns a standing refusal with the stated reason for any out-of-scope ask (e.g. "write the whole report"). These refusals are not configurable per practice.

**Model routing.** Every AI call that produces a suggestion resolves its answering provider through the existing `ai_model_routes` mechanism (`task_key` unique, resolved via `resolveTaskRoute(taskKey)` / `getDefaultProviderName()` / `generateAiForTask()` in `lib/ai-providers/src/index.ts`), with this precedence, which must never be violated by a new call site: **explicit caller override → active task route for that `task_key` → global default provider.** `AI_TASK_CATALOG`/`AI_TASK_KEYS` is the source of truth for what task keys exist; a new AI-producing feature registers a task key there rather than hardcoding a provider anywhere. Per D-31, the default posture is **local-first**: Ollama (via `/api/radiology-ollama` and `clinic_settings`) answers unless a task's route or a provider-enable flag (Chapter 8) sends it to a cloud provider (Gemini/OpenAI/Claude). `/api/ai-reporting/query` — the primary interactive drafting endpoint (D-30) — is a documented exception to calling `generateAiForTask` directly: it resolves the `radiology_draft` route inline, replicating the identical three-step precedence. Any future endpoint that needs the same shortcut must replicate the precedence exactly, not approximate it; the two background/legacy endpoints (`/api/internal/radiology/ai-draft` on `ai_job_queue`, and the frozen `/api/smart-radiology/ai-impressions`) are unaffected and eventually present through the same four-surface copilot rather than a separate UI.

**Prompt templates.** A suggestion's prompt is resolved from `ai_prompt_templates` by **name**, DB-first, falling back to the legacy hardcoded `AI_PROMPT_TEMPLATES` map only when no active DB row matches. The name is the resolution key and is enforced unique case-insensitively three ways — a plain unique index on `name`, a functional unique index on `lower(name)`, and an API-layer 409 on create/update — because a silent case-collision would resolve two different templates to the same request non-deterministically. A blank name or blank content is rejected (400). The resolution query orders by `id` as defense-in-depth against non-deterministic row selection when more than one row could otherwise match. A template's `version` auto-bumps only when its content actually changes — editing metadata without changing the prompt text does not bump the version, and the audit record (below) always stamps the version that was actually used, never the current one. Requests answered by the legacy fallback map stamp a reserved sentinel template id/version so that the audit record's prompt-lineage field is never null.

### Acceptance

**Explicit gesture only.** A suggestion becomes accepted through exactly one of: `Tab` on ghost text, `Enter` or the Insert control on a margin card, a `fix` action on a gutter mark, a per-row Insert or "Insert All" on a measurement pool row (Chapter 5), voice "accept," or `Enter` on a recommendation/impression ghost. There is no implicit acceptance path — a suggestion left on screen while the radiologist moves on is never treated as accepted; it is either superseded (a later edit makes it stale and it is silently withdrawn, never inserted after the fact) or ages into the ledger.

**What accepting does, in order, atomically:**

1. The suggested text or value is written into the report/finding/measurement at the exact point it was offered — never anywhere else, never reformatted, never combined with other pending suggestions unless the gesture was explicitly the batch one ("Insert All").
2. The inserted span is marked **provisional** (Law 3) — visually distinct, and structurally tracked so the sign gate can enumerate it later.
3. The action becomes **one atomic undo step** (Law 8): a single `Ctrl+Z` immediately after acceptance removes exactly that span and nothing else, restoring the ghost/card as if it had never been accepted.
4. The suggestion's audit row (Audit, below) is updated in the same transaction: `disposition = accepted`, `actioned_at` stamped, `user` stamped from the authenticated session (never a client-supplied identity, per D-33's posture extended to every actor field this chapter defines).

**"Insert All" is one gesture, not an automation.** Where a surface offers a batch action (all currently-pending Routine/Worth-a-look measurement rows, per Chapter 5's D-21 contract), pressing it is the single explicit gesture that satisfies "offer, never act" for every row it touches; the system must never chain a prior gesture (e.g. `approve`) into an automatic insert without this press, and an Attention-band row is never eligible for the batch action regardless of how it is invoked.

**Recommendation and templated-slot acceptance.** Accepting `rec.urgent` or `rec.fleischner` (Chapter 4) inserts both the sentence and its filled slot(s) in the same gesture; the radiologist confirms the filled value as part of accepting, they do not separately edit it in after the fact for it to count as a plain acceptance — editing the slot value after insertion is Modification, below.

**Voice acceptance.** Voice "accept" is a first-class equivalent of `Tab`/`Enter` for whatever surface currently has focus; it carries the identical atomic-undo and audit obligations. Voice "sign" is not an acceptance gesture for a single suggestion — it is the sign-off act itself, and is gated exactly as `Ctrl+Enter` is (Part 0 §0.6 rule 2; D-9, D-38): it fails the same way if any span remains untouched-provisional.

### Modification

**Accept-then-edit is a distinct, tracked path**, not a variant of acceptance. The moment a radiologist edits an already-accepted AI span — one keystroke of insertion, deletion, or dictation-over inside its bounds — three things happen together:

1. The provisional marker on that span clears (Law 3; C-12: only edit or explicit section-confirm clears it, never dwell time).
2. The audit row's disposition transitions from `accepted` to **`accepted-then-edited`** — a one-way transition; there is no path back to plain `accepted` for that suggestion once edited.
3. The edit itself becomes a **preference signal** for the Learning loop below — the diff between what was offered and what the radiologist kept is exactly the training material the personalization layer is allowed to use.

**Diff capture.** Every `accepted-then-edited` audit row carries, in addition to the fields defined under Audit: the original suggested text/value (already stored as the row's `output`), the **final text/value as it stood at the next save or at sign**, and the edit as an offset range (start/end character positions within the accepted span, at the time of the first edit) so a later reviewer can see precisely what was kept and what was changed without diffing the whole report. This is the mechanism, not a new state machine — `accepted-then-edited` remains a single disposition regardless of how many further edits the radiologist makes to that span; only the first edit trips the transition, and the stored final text is refreshed at each subsequent save so the audit row always reflects what the radiologist actually kept.

**The refiner is a bounded special case.** `/polish` (Chapter 6 owns its full behavior) offers a tracked-changes-style diff against text the radiologist already wrote, not a wholesale rewrite; every individual edit in that diff is its own acceptable/rejectable unit and follows this same accept/accept-then-edit/reject contract per edit, not once for the whole diff. The refiner's hard constraint — it may not add findings, soften certainty, or change laterality or measurements, and any such change is blocked and surfaced as "cannot refine without changing meaning" — is enforced before the diff ever renders, so a blocked refinement never becomes an audit row at all (it was never offered, per the Suggestion-stage grounding-and-scope gate).

**Modification never regenerates.** Editing an accepted AI span never triggers a second AI pass over the edited text within the same interaction — the copilot does not "helpfully" re-suggest a correction to a correction. A new suggestion in that location can only arise from the same triggers that produced the first one (new measurement value entered, cursor re-entering a field, an explicit new ask).

### Rejection

**Explicit rejection** happens through the surface-specific dismiss gesture: `Shift+Tab` on ghost text (skip), `X` on a margin card, dismiss-with-reason on a gutter mark or an escalation-card decline. Disposition is recorded as **`skipped`** (ghost text only, no reason required — rejecting a one-sentence ghost by moving past it is low-friction by design) or **`dismissed-with-reason`** (cards, gutter marks, escalation declines — a reason is mandatory and is itself audit-trailed verbatim, not just as a boolean).

**`overridden`** is a distinct fifth disposition, reserved for the case where the radiologist's final report text directly contradicts what a suggestion asserted (e.g. the ghost proposed "no acute infarct" and the signed report contains "acute infarct present" in that location) — it is detected by the same lint/consistency machinery Chapter 3 defines for contradiction checks, not by a separate AI-specific comparison pass, and it is recorded even though the radiologist never explicitly interacted with the original suggestion's controls.

**Implicit rejection** covers the cases where a suggestion disappears without any dismiss gesture: typing or dictating over ghost text (equivalent to `skipped`, since the radiologist's own text replaces it in one motion); a margin card superseded because the underlying trigger condition changed (recorded as `skipped`, since nothing was ever explicitly declined); a card that ages into the ledger unactioned when the panel's Offering cap displaces it (not a rejection at all — it was never actioned, so no disposition transition occurs, and the ledger entry itself is not a new audit row, only a pointer to the existing one).

**Cool-down / suppression behavior (Law 6).** A rejection — of either kind, and dismissal-with-reason especially — is the strongest available signal, and its visible effect is defined here since no source document fixes the mechanics:

- Suppression scope is **(radiologist, suggestion class)**, where "suggestion class" is the catalog key, recommendation code, or differential-candidate identity the suggestion was built from — never the literal generated sentence, since phrasing varies but the underlying claim does not.
- Suppression is **persistent, not time-boxed**: once a radiologist has dismissed the same class enough times to cross the copilot's suppression threshold (a calibration-owned config value, reviewed on the same quarterly cadence as confidence-band thresholds, C-10), that class stops being offered to that radiologist indefinitely — there is no automatic re-enable timer.
- Suppression is **visible and reversible**: every suppressed class appears in the radiologist's My Prefs "suppressed suggestions" list (distinct from, but adjacent to, the plain-language "teach" rules under Learning below); removing it from that list is the only way suppression lifts, and doing so is itself a logged action.
- Suppression is **per-radiologist only** — it never touches what the copilot offers to any other radiologist, and it never touches the underlying catalog entry, lint rule, or guideline anchor (the personalization firewall, Learning below, applies to suppression exactly as it applies to phrasing adoption).
- A `skipped` ghost contributes to the suppression count at a lower weight than a `dismissed-with-reason` card, since skipping a ghost is cheap and noisy while a reasoned dismissal is a deliberate signal; the exact weighting is calibration-owned config, not a code constant.

### Learning

**What is permitted to adapt, and how.** Every disposition — accepted, accepted-then-edited, skipped, dismissed-with-reason, overridden — feeds a per-radiologist personalization layer that may adjust exactly three things: **style** (which phrasing variant the copilot offers first), **phrasing** (adopting patterns from repeated accept-then-edit corrections), and **suggestion selection** (which candidate classes are offered at all, including the suppression behavior above). Nothing else is adjustable by usage signal.

**Two visible mechanisms, no invisible ones:**

1. **Passive adaptation.** The copilot stops offering repeatedly-dismissed suggestion classes (Rejection, above) and gradually favors phrasing patterns the radiologist has kept across multiple accept-then-edit corrections. This happens through ordinary gesture signal — there is never a "was this helpful?" prompt, at any point, on any surface.
2. **Explicit "teach" rules.** A per-item "teach" affordance lets the radiologist state a rule in plain language ("never suggest X for pediatric studies"); the resulting rule is shown back for confirmation before it takes effect, and it lives in My Prefs as a reviewable, deletable, plain-language list — never a black-box weight adjustment. A teach rule is scoped to suggestion selection and phrasing only, identically to passive adaptation; a teach rule that would touch lint, escalation, calibration, or a guideline anchor is rejected at confirmation time with the reason stated, the same way an out-of-scope ask is refused.

**The monthly digest.** A per-radiologist "Your copilot" summary appears in My Analytics: edit-rate trend, which phrasings have been suppressed, and an honesty check — "N suggestions you dismissed were later addressed in an addendum (review?)" — surfaced from the same audit trail defined below, not a separate tracking system. This is the mechanism that keeps the suppression behavior from silently degrading quality: a radiologist who is over-suppressing a genuinely useful class sees it here, and only here — the system never overrides the suppression on their behalf.

**The personalization firewall — what is NEVER learned automatically, by anyone, at any rate of signal:**

- **Lint rules** (✕/△/◌ definitions and thresholds) — never personalize; a contradiction is a contradiction for every radiologist.
- **Critical escalation** (which patterns trigger the interrupt banner, the `crit.*` registry, D-04/D-05) — never personalize; centrally governed with clinical sign-off only.
- **Confidence calibration** (the raw-score-to-band thresholds, C-10) — adjusts only through the quarterly calibration audit against outcome data, never from acceptance-rate signal, and the adjustment is a versioned governance-config change, not an online update.
- **Guideline anchors** (`rec.fleischner`'s interval logic, any future guideline-anchored recommendation) — never personalize; a guideline version is a governance-published fact, not a preference.
- **The model itself.** No suggestion, accept, edit, or rejection ever triggers fine-tuning, weight updates, retrieval-index changes, or any other modification to what a model will say to a *different* radiologist, a *different* practice, or the *same* radiologist on a *different* task. There is no online-learning loop from production interactions into the deployed model. A model version change is always a deliberate, versioned, audit-trailed governance event (D-18's `render_engine_version`/provider-version namespace; B-30) — never a side effect of usage.
- **Per-radiologist preference data never crosses practices** and is excluded from any cross-radiologist aggregation beyond the anonymized practice-level dashboards defined under Audit; double-read/multi-radiologist attribution in the provenance view is handled per-report, not by merging preference profiles.

This firewall is the single hardest boundary in this chapter: **personalization is a UI convenience layer over a fixed, centrally-governed safety and content layer, and no engineering shortcut may blur that line** — a suggestion-selection model is free to learn "this radiologist prefers passive voice"; it is never free to learn "this radiologist tends to skip the pneumothorax escalation card, so offer it less."

### Audit

**Recording is unconditional.** Every suggestion that renders on any of the four surfaces — including ghost text the radiologist skips, cards nobody clicks, gutter marks dismissed without a second look — is written to the audit trail. This cannot be disabled per study, per practice, or per feature flag; the recording obligation is orthogonal to whether the *feature* is flag-gated (Chapter 8 governs whether a suggestion is generated at all; if it is generated and rendered, it is audited, no exceptions).

**The canonical audit record — `ai_suggestion_audit`.** This chapter establishes one new table as the authoritative per-suggestion record, replacing the fragmented `.catch(()=>{})`-guarded writes in `aiReporting.ts` (five swallow sites: `aiReporting.ts:597,797,855,935,1077`, per the CTO finding this chapter answers, D-32) and the scattered partial records in `ai_quality_scores`/`ai_extraction_results`. It follows the identity rules for new tables (D-37: `bigint` identity PK, nullable `tenant_id`/`branch_id`, no `MAX(seq)+1` key) and ships additive-only (B-23). One row per rendered suggestion, carrying:

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `worklist_ref` / `study_ref` | which study the suggestion belongs to |
| `user` | the radiologist, server-derived from the authenticated staff session (`subjectName`), never client-supplied |
| `surface` | ghost_text / margin_card / gutter_mark / interrupt_banner |
| `suggestion_class` | the catalog key, recommendation code, or differential-candidate identity the suggestion was built from — the same identity used for suppression scoping |
| `confidence_band` + `raw_score` | the three-band value and the underlying model score, kept independently (Law 4) |
| `evidence_sources` | pointers to the specific sentence/measurement field/prior-report line/image region/guideline row the suggestion cited — never free text |
| `engine` / `provider` / `model_version` | which engine answered, resolved per the routing precedence above, and its exact version |
| `prompt_template_id` + `prompt_template_version` | the exact `ai_prompt_templates` row and version used to build the request (or the legacy-fallback sentinel) |
| `input_hash` | a SHA-256 hash of the canonicalized request payload (study context + report-text snapshot + measurement values + template id/version actually sent) — proves what the model actually saw without storing PHI-bearing raw payloads a second time |
| `output` | the suggested text or value exactly as rendered |
| `disposition` | accepted / accepted-then-edited / skipped / dismissed-with-reason / overridden |
| `dismiss_reason` | verbatim reason text, where the disposition requires one |
| `edited_text` + `edit_offsets` | populated only for `accepted-then-edited` rows (Modification, above) |
| `rendered_at` / `actioned_at` | two independent timestamps — a suggestion can render and never be actioned at all |
| `audit_log_id` | a mandatory foreign key into the tamper-evident `audit_logs` hash chain (D-34) — every `ai_suggestion_audit` write is paired, in the same transaction, with an `audit_logs` entry, so AI provenance is never a second, weaker trail sitting outside the chain the CTO review flagged |

Writes to this table **must fail loudly**: a failed insert aborts the transaction that would have marked the suggestion accepted/rejected, logs an alert, and never silently degrades to "the suggestion happened but wasn't recorded." This directly reverses the as-built `.catch(()=>{})` pattern; there is no code path in this chapter's scope that swallows an audit write.

**Explain (`E`) is a read path over this same record**, not a separate mechanism: the first `E` press on any suggestion renders its `evidence_sources` in place (the deriving sentence/field/region highlighted where it already lives on screen — never generated justification prose, Law 5); a second `E` press renders `engine`/`provider`/`model_version`/`prompt_template_id`+`version` — the full prompt lineage. Both presses read the audit row that already exists from render time; nothing is computed fresh for the explain interaction.

**Provenance in the signed report.** The clinical body of `patient_reports.body` (D-19) stays clean — no inline markers beyond the governance-configured disclosure line applied verbatim (D-31's engine-disclosure obligation is separate: it is a UI footer, never inserted into report text). Provenance is a **view**, not a document feature: a per-report "Report provenance" mode shades AI-originated spans and, on click, shows that span's `ai_suggestion_audit` row. Shading is visible only in provenance mode; it is never present in the clinical document a referring physician or patient sees. Span-level provenance (which characters of the final signed text trace to which audit row) is captured at accept/edit time using the same offset mechanism as Modification's diff capture, and is stamped alongside the report at sign per D-18/C-02's snapshot-at-sign contract — a signed report's provenance view must always be reconstructible from what was true at the moment of signing, not from the live (and possibly since-edited) catalog or model state.

**Retention (C-11).** The audit trail and provenance spans are retained for as long as the report itself — a minimum of 7 years, matching Indian medico-legal report retention. Prompt history (the user-facing `/history` surface over a radiologist's own explicit asks) is retained 24 months and is deletable on request, **except** any entry referenced by a signed report's audit trail, which inherits the report's retention floor regardless of the radiologist's deletion request.

**Aggregation.** Practice-level dashboards (living beside My Analytics/QA) aggregate `ai_suggestion_audit` by feature (`surface`/`suggestion_class`), modality, and `model_version` — acceptance rate, edit rate, override rate — enabling regression detection when a provider or model version rolls over. These dashboards read the same table; there is no separate aggregation pipeline to keep in sync.

**Governance settings are themselves audited.** Any change to the disclosure policy text, feature enablement, retention configuration, or confidence-band thresholds is a versioned, audit-trailed governance action in its own right (§0.6 rule 12) — it does not go through `ai_suggestion_audit` (which is per-suggestion), but through the standard `audit_logs` chain like any other admin action, and Chapter 8 owns the flag-enablement half of that mechanism.

### Acceptance criteria

- Every rendered suggestion on every one of the four surfaces produces exactly one `ai_suggestion_audit` row, paired in the same transaction with an `audit_logs` chain entry; no code path can mark a suggestion actioned without that pairing succeeding.
- No AI code path writes report text, deletes text, moves the cursor, or changes report state without a preceding explicit radiologist gesture recorded as `accepted`; grep-level review of any new AI feature's insertion path is a required PR check.
- No suggestion renders without at least one `evidence_sources` entry; the generation pipeline discards ungroundable candidates before the Suggestion-stage render decision, and this is enforced in code, not by review discipline.
- No confidence value, band, or percentage other than *Routine* / *Worth a look* / *Attention* appears anywhere in the reading-surface UI; raw scores are reachable only via `E` and the audit trail.
- Sign-off is technically blocked while any AI-originated span is untouched-provisional, for both AI text and dictation-provisional amber text; the sign gate enumerates the remaining spans as jump-links, not a scolding modal.
- A `Ctrl+Z` immediately following any AI acceptance removes exactly that one accepted span and nothing else.
- The suppression list, teach-rule list, and monthly digest are all reachable from My Prefs / My Analytics, are per-radiologist, and never affect what any other radiologist sees.
- No lint rule, escalation trigger, confidence-band threshold, or guideline anchor has ever been modified by a personalization/learning code path; this is verifiable by the fact that those four systems have no write path that originates from `ai_suggestion_audit` disposition data.
- Zero non-critical AI interruptions occur during open-mic dictation or within 5 seconds of active typing, verified by an automated test that asserts this as a bug class, not a manual QA pass.
- `ai_prompt_templates.name` uniqueness (plain + `lower(name)` + API 409) and version-bump-on-content-change-only are both enforced and covered by tests.
- The five `.catch(()=>{})` swallow sites in `aiReporting.ts` no longer exist in any form; every AI audit write failure is loud (logged, alerted, transaction-aborting).

### Build order

This chapter depends on Chapters 1–6 for the catalog objects (findings, parameters, recommendations, measurements, impressions) that suggestions propose into, and on Chapter 9 for the two sanctioned migration paths used to create `ai_suggestion_audit`. Build in this order, honoring the trust-chassis sequencing doctrine (Part 2 of the AI Experience Spec, carried forward here): audit and authorship ship before any content feature earns the right to suggest anything.

1. **Provider routing and prompt-template resolution** (Suggestion, model routing / prompt templates) — verify the existing `ai_model_routes`/`generateAiForTask` precedence and the `ai_prompt_templates` name-uniqueness enforcement are both intact; this is largely as-built verification, not new construction, and every other step depends on it producing a `model_version`/`prompt_template_id` pair to record.
2. **`ai_suggestion_audit` table and its transactional pairing with `audit_logs`** (Audit) — this must exist and be fail-loud before any suggestion surface ships or is re-enabled, per the trust-chassis doctrine; retrofitting audit onto a live suggestion surface is the failure mode this build order exists to prevent.
3. **Confidence-band mapping layer** (Suggestion) — the internal High/Medium/Low-or-raw-score to Routine/Worth-a-look/Attention mapping, as a single shared component consumed by every surface, so no surface can drift to its own vocabulary.
4. **The four surfaces and their gesture handlers** (Suggestion, Acceptance) — ghost text, margin card, gutter mark, interrupt banner, each wired to: render → grounding check → `ai_suggestion_audit` insert (`rendered_at`) → gesture → disposition update (`actioned_at`).
5. **Provisional-marker and atomic-undo mechanics** (Acceptance) — the span-tracking structure the sign gate will later enumerate; build this before wiring any specific feature's accept path, since every feature reuses it identically.
6. **Modification diff capture** (Modification) — offset-based original/final tracking, shared by every accept-then-edit path including the impression `/polish` diff (Chapter 6 wires its feature-specific behavior on top of this).
7. **Rejection disposition handling and suppression store** (Rejection) — the per-(radiologist, suggestion_class) suppression table and its My Prefs surface.
8. **Learning-loop surfaces** (Learning) — passive adaptation, teach-rule capture and confirmation UI, the monthly digest — built last among the interaction mechanics, since it consumes the disposition history the earlier steps produce, and is the step most likely to be deferred to a Should-tier release without blocking Must-tier suggestion features.
9. **Provenance view and practice dashboards** (Audit) — read-only surfaces over the now-populated `ai_suggestion_audit` table; no new write paths, so these can ship incrementally per feature without re-touching the audit core.

Chapter 8 (Feature Flags) governs whether each of the above is *turned on* per the `ff_radiology_*` registry and the explicit-degradation banner when a provider or model is unavailable; this chapter governs what must be true every time one of them fires.

---

## Chapter 8 — Feature Flag Rollout Plan

This chapter is the owning chapter for the flag mechanism (D-24), the flags-default-off doctrine (B-24), explicit degradation (B-26), and production flag-state governance (C-17); it states each fully rather than pointing elsewhere. It then gives the complete, decisive inventory of the thirteen `ff_radiology_*` flags that constitute the roadmap of record (D-03), the dependency order between them, the five-stage rollout ladder (internal → pilot → department → hospital → multi-center) with entry/exit criteria per stage per flag, and the rollback contract: what turning each flag off mid-flight does to in-progress reports, and which flags are one-way doors. Every flag seeds `enabled=false`; nothing in this chapter authorizes a flag to default to true. The chapter does not re-derive clinical content rules (Chapters 1–7 own those); it only governs *when the code path that implements them is switched on*.

### 8.1 The flag mechanism (owning: D-24, B-24, C-17)

There is exactly **one** sanctioned feature-flag mechanism for radiology: the server table `feature_flags` — `key` is the text primary key, `enabled` is a non-null boolean defaulting to false, and `description`, `updated_by`, `updated_at` round out the row — read through `GET /api/feature-flags` (any authenticated staff) and mutated through `PATCH /api/feature-flags/:key` (`requireAdminRole` — admin/super_admin only, a hard role gate independent of per-user permission arrays). The table is authoritative **only** for keys prefixed `ff_radiology_`; it must never be repurposed for non-radiology flags without a prefix namespace of their own, because the client hydration guard (below) forbids server override of any other prefix.

Binding mechanics, non-negotiable:

1. **Flags are created only by migration, never by PATCH.** `PATCH` on an unknown key returns 404 — the API cannot create a flag. Every new `ff_radiology_*` key ships in a migration with `enabled=false`.
2. **Every flag seeds `enabled=false`** (B-24). No exceptions, including flags that gate low-risk, already-tested surfaces — the deployment sequence in §8.4 is the only sanctioned path from false to true.
3. **Unknown keys evaluate false and never throw.** `isFeatureEnabledServer(key)` is a total function over any string; a typo in a flag key degrades silently to "off," never to an error.
4. **Server-side cache is a 30-second TTL, single-process cache**, correct only because care-api runs as one container today. `PATCH` calls `invalidateFeatureFlagCache()` synchronously before responding, so an admin toggle is visible to that process immediately; the 30 s figure only matters for other read paths that haven't just PATCHed. This single-process assumption is a **build-order blocker for horizontal scaling** — see Build order, item 9.
5. **Client precedence** (`staffSession.ts`): `FEATURE_FLAG_DEFAULTS` (all `ff_radiology_*` false) < localStorage `featureFlags` < server-hydrated value, and the server hydration path (`setServerFeatureFlags`) accepts **only** `ff_radiology_`-prefixed keys — it can never leak into or override the pre-existing client-only `showX` flags (`showUnifiedReporting`, `showMeasurementPanel`, `showAiDraftPanel`, …). Those legacy flags are frozen: no new `showX` flags are created; each migrates to a server `ff_radiology_*` key the next time its surface is touched (D-24 migration impact). `/api/staff/feature-flags` (Roadmap v2's proposed path) does not exist and must not be built — `/api/feature-flags` is final.
6. **Flag changes are audit-relevant admin actions.** Every PATCH must be attributable (`updated_by`, `updated_at` are populated, never null) and, per C-17, accompanied procedurally by a changelog entry naming the strand ticket being turned on. This chapter does not mandate a new audit table for flag changes — `updated_by`/`updated_at` on the row plus the changelog entry is the record — but a future ticket may fold flag PATCHes into the general admin-action audit log; until then, the two together are the minimum bar and are mandatory, not optional.
7. **Environment variables are never a feature gate** — they remain reserved for infrastructure config (DB URL, PACS host, AI provider keys) per B-28. A flag whose "off" state is implemented by reading an env var instead of `feature_flags` is a defect.
8. **Explicit degradation, not silent half-work (B-26).** Whenever a flag-gated AI surface is unreachable — the flag is on but the model/provider is down, or a model is under quality review — the workstation shows exactly one banner, "Copilot offline — full manual mode," and every AI-dependent feature falls back to its manual equivalent. This is a *runtime* state, distinct from the flag itself: `ff_radiology_ai_assist=false` means the surface never renders; `ff_radiology_ai_assist=true` with the provider unreachable means the surface renders the degradation banner instead of suggestions. Both must be silent about internals and loud about state — never a spinner that never resolves, never a suggestion that partially renders.

### 8.2 The flag inventory

The thirteen flags are the as-coded index of the Strand A–N roadmap of record (D-03). Strand B has no flag (folded into other strands' tickets). Every flag defaults `enabled=false`; the "Depends on" column is this chapter's dependency ordering, established in §8.3.

| Flag | Strand | Gates (UI surface / endpoint / job) | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ff_radiology_structured_core` | A | Dual-write of Quick Select `findings[]` into `report_finding_instances` inside `POST /api/radiology/report-generator/save-draft`; the A4 cache regeneration (`regenerateDraftStructuredJson`) that rebuilds `radiology_report_drafts.structured_json`; the admin drift-diagnostic `GET /api/radiology/report-generator/structured-json-drift` (returns a clean "disabled" response with no drift computed, when off). No radiologist-visible UI changes by itself — Quick Select tile behavior is identical on or off. | none (root) |
| `ff_radiology_measurement_pool` | C | The finding-independent measurement pool and its attach/detach surface in the Measurement Assistant (Chapter 5); pool endpoints under `/api/radiology/report-generator/*` (wired by this strand's own ticket). | `structured_core` |
| `ff_radiology_classification` | D | The data-driven grading engine (e.g. TI-RADS, NIHSS) that replaces hardcoded numeric-threshold grading (the D-08 precedent: `radiologySmartEngine.ts` canal-stenosis thresholds) with catalog `sev.*` derive rules; surfaces as a *suggested* grade ghost in severity pickers — offer-never-act, never an asserted grade (D-08). | `structured_core`, `modality_expand` |
| `ff_radiology_modality_expand` | E | The canonical modality vocabulary (adds XA/IR, NM, PT) on `report_finding_instances.modality`, `structured_report_templates.modality`, worklist and template-picker filters. | `structured_core` |
| `ff_radiology_render_v2` | F | Makes `renderEngine.ts` (F1a/F1b: `abnormalityEngine`, `sideSwap`, `quickFindingsMerge`) the **sole** producer of findings/impression/technique/recommendation text in the Reporting Workspace, retiring any remaining legacy text-generation path. Highest blast radius of the thirteen — it changes what text a radiologist actually reads and signs. | `structured_core`, `measurement_pool`, `hierarchy` |
| `ff_radiology_search_v2` | G | `pg_trgm` fuzzy/synonym search on patient-name and report-search columns (C-18) plus the client-side search index; surfaces in worklist and report search bars. | none |
| `ff_radiology_catalog_delta` | H | Versioned per-modality catalog bundles and delta sync — the mechanism that ships updates from the imported YAML content packs (Chapter 1's importer, C-01) to the running system without a full redeploy; a sync job plus an admin-visible catalog-version indicator. | `structured_core`, `modality_expand`; **also requires** the Chapter 1 catalog importer (C-01) to exist — not itself a flag, but a hard build-order prerequisite |
| `ff_radiology_hierarchy` | I | Population and use of `anatomic_zone_id` / `structure_id` on `report_finding_instances` (both columns already exist, nullable); the Zone/Structure anatomic drill-down UI. | `structured_core` |
| `ff_radiology_presets` | J | Doctor → Department → Hospital preset resolution order for template and preference defaults; surfaces in template selection and default-section behavior. | `structured_core` |
| `ff_radiology_voice_structured` | K | The push-to-talk voice contract (D-09's ten verbs) writing directly into `report_finding_instances` with `source="voice"`; supersedes the legacy batch transcription queue for structured capture (the queue remains, frozen, for legacy long-form dictation only). | `structured_core` |
| `ff_radiology_multiwindow` | L | The dockable multi-monitor Reporting Workspace layout, keyed by the C-20 monitor fingerprint (hash of sorted resolution/position/devicePixelRatio tuples). Pure client layout state — never touches report content. | none |
| `ff_radiology_ai_assist` | M | AI-generated suggestions with the human-confirmation gate: writes `source="ai"` rows into `report_finding_instances`, sets `confirmed`/`confirmedBy`/`confirmedAt` only on an explicit radiologist gesture (D-21). Surfaces: margin cards, ghost text, the three confidence bands (D-22) in the Reporting Workspace's copilot panel. Endpoints: `/api/ai-reporting/query` (D-30 primary interactive path) and `/api/internal/radiology/ai-draft` (PACS-driven background job hook via `ai_job_queue`). | `structured_core`, `render_v2`; **also requires** the D-32 audit-swallow fixes (the five `.catch(()=>{})` sites in `aiReporting.ts`) landed and verified — not itself a flag, but a hard BINDING prerequisite (patient-safety: no AI surface may render before its audit trail is provably unconditional) |
| `ff_radiology_scale_partition` | N | Partitioning of `report_finding_instances` by `created_at` plus read-replica routing. Pure infrastructure — no UI surface, no endpoint surface. Triggered by a data-volume sentinel (Chapter 9), never by clinical readiness. | `structured_core`; independent of every other flag's rollout stage (see §8.4) |

### 8.3 Dependency ordering

`ff_radiology_structured_core` (A) is the root of the graph: `report_finding_instances` is the physical home every other structured-data flag reads or writes, so **no other flag may reach even the internal stage until `structured_core` has reached the department stage** in the deployment ladder (§8.4). This is stricter than "must be on" — it requires `structured_core` to be *proven*, not merely enabled, before anything downstream is trusted with real patients beyond a single pilot reader.

Three dependency tiers follow from the table above:

- **Tier 1 (depends only on `structured_core`):** `measurement_pool` (C), `modality_expand` (E), `hierarchy` (I), `presets` (J), `voice_structured` (K), `search_v2` (G, no dependency at all — included in Tier 1 for scheduling convenience only). These may be developed and staged in parallel; none reads another's output.
- **Tier 2 (depends on Tier 1 outputs):** `classification` (D, needs `modality_expand`'s vocabulary to scope which grading scales apply to which modality), `catalog_delta` (H, needs `modality_expand` for per-modality bundling and the Chapter 1 importer as a non-flag prerequisite).
- **Tier 3 (depends on Tier 1 and 2 being burned in):** `render_v2` (F) — it must never become the sole text producer while the data it renders (`measurement_pool`, `hierarchy`) is still unproven, or radiologists will sign reports whose text silently omits enriched fields nobody caught in testing.
- **Tier 4 (last, highest risk):** `ai_assist` (M) depends on `render_v2` (its suggestions are inserted through the same render path) and on the D-32 audit-fix prerequisite. It is deliberately the last flag to reach hospital stage regardless of how quickly its own code lands — treat this as a floor, not a target.

`multiwindow` (L) and `scale_partition` (N) sit outside this data-dependency graph. `multiwindow` depends on nothing but is gated by hardware validation per site (§8.4). `scale_partition` depends only on `structured_core` existing (there must be a table to partition) and is never scheduled by clinical stage at all — see §8.4's note on N.

A flag may enter its own **pilot** stage only once every flag it depends on has reached at least **department** stage. A flag may enter **hospital** stage only once every flag it depends on has itself reached **hospital** stage. This rule is mechanical and must not be waived per-flag without Chief Clinical Architect sign-off, because the failure mode (a downstream flag outrunning an unproven dependency) is exactly the "silent stale cache" hazard described in §8.5.

### 8.4 Safe deployment sequence

Five stages, defined once and reused for every flag. Because the as-built `feature_flags` table has **no scope column** — `enabled` is one global boolean per key, with no `tenant_id`/`branch_id` (D-37 applies that pattern only to *new* tables; `feature_flags` predates D-37) — "pilot" and "department" below are **procedural**, not technical, scopes: the flag is live for every staff member the instant it is PATCHed on, and only training/communication limits who exercises the new surface during pilot and department stages. Engineers must not assume the mechanism enforces audience scoping; the runbook does.

| Stage | Who is instructed to use the surface | Entry criteria (MUST) | Exit criteria (MUST) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Internal** | Engineering + QA only, against synthetic or explicitly non-reportable studies | Strand ticket code-complete; full CI green including the five importer validations where the flag touches catalog data (B-16); typecheck baseline unchanged (C-19); for any flag writing to `report_finding_instances`, an A5 drift scan shows zero new drift after a full write/read cycle | Minimum 3 business days of flag-on internal soak with zero unhandled errors attributable to the flagged path in logs; changelog entry drafted naming the strand ticket (C-17) |
| **Pilot** | One named pilot radiologist, briefed that they alone are to exercise the new surface even though it is globally live | Internal exit criteria met; admin PATCH rehearsed including a rehearsed rollback PATCH (kill-switch drill, §8.5) completed successfully before go-live; coordinator briefed on what "off" looks like if the drill is needed for real | Minimum 5 reading days with the pilot radiologist; zero ✕-blocking lint regressions, zero missed critical-finding escalations (Chapter 3's B-07 contract unaffected), signed-report immutability unaffected (spot-check via D-19) |
| **Department** | Every radiologist and typist on the single Synology deployment | Pilot exit criteria met; every dependency flag (§8.3) at department stage or beyond | Minimum 10 reading days department-wide; turnaround-time metrics (Chapter 14) not regressed against the pre-flag baseline; drift/error rate at or below internal-stage baseline |
| **Hospital** | All hospital staff whose workflows touch the surface, including non-radiology roles the flag reaches (coordinators, referrers via notifications, technologists via worklist) | Department exit criteria met; for any **BINDING**-tagged dependency in the conflict register (D-19 through D-37 as applicable), explicit clinical-owner sign-off recorded in the changelog entry, not just an admin PATCH | One full release cycle (minimum 4 weeks) with no safety incident attributable to the flagged path; the flag is now considered load-bearing — see §8.5 for what that means for rollback |
| **Multi-center** | Applies only once a second hospital/site is onboarded | `feature_flags` gains a nullable `branch_id` column (additive migration, Chapter 9's two sanctioned paths) and `PATCH /api/feature-flags/:key` accepts an optional branch scope — **this is a build-order prerequisite for true multi-center rollout, not yet built**; until it ships, every flag is one global switch shared by all sites | Each new site runs its own internal → pilot → department → hospital progression from zero, scoped to its `branch_id`; a flag proven at hospital stage on site 1 does **not** carry over to site 2 — different staff, hardware, and case-mix invalidate the prior proof. Ongoing steady state is governed by the quarterly loop (Chapter 14, B-27), not a stage exit |

Ordering across flags follows the tiers in §8.3 layered onto this ladder: `structured_core` is the only flag permitted to reach hospital stage while every other flag is still at internal or below. Once `structured_core` is at department stage, Tier 1 flags may begin their own internal stage, in any order, in parallel. Tier 2 flags may not start internal stage until their Tier 1 dependencies are at department stage. `render_v2` may not start internal stage until every Tier 1 and Tier 2 dependency is at hospital stage — it is the one flag for which "internal" itself requires the rest of the fleet to already be mature, because its correctness can only be verified against fully-populated finding instances. `ai_assist` follows the same rule one level higher: it may not start internal stage until `render_v2` is at hospital stage and the D-32 audit-fix prerequisite has independently landed and been verified (this is a code-review gate, not a flag).

`search_v2` and `multiwindow` are exceptions to strict sequencing: both are low-risk and independent, and may run their own ladder in parallel with anything, with lighter entry criteria (index-build verification and query-latency check for `search_v2`; per-reading-room hardware validation against the C-20 fingerprint for `multiwindow`'s department-stage exit, since monitor configurations vary by workstation).

`scale_partition` (N) is explicitly **not** part of the clinical stage ladder. It is triggered by a data-volume sentinel on `report_finding_instances` (defined in Chapter 9), independent of pilot/department/hospital maturity, and executes as a maintenance-window operation with a mandatory pre-migration backup (D-35) — never a routine PATCH rollout. Do not gate it behind reading-day counts; gate it behind row-count/table-size thresholds only.

### 8.5 Rollback sequence

**Umbrella invariant, overriding every rule below:** once a report is SIGNED (D-19, D-20), `patient_reports.body` is frozen and append-only. No flag toggle, in any direction, may alter a signed report's body. Every effect described below is scoped to DRAFT state (and, for `ai_assist`, to the audit trail that outlives the draft). This is the stricter reading required by safety principle 1 and is not overridable.

Kill-switch mechanism, generic: an admin/super_admin issues `PATCH /api/feature-flags/:key` setting `enabled` to false, synchronous cache invalidation on that request, effective immediately in the current single-container deployment. No flag toggle deletes rows; every flag in this catalog is additive-write-gated (B-23), so "off" always means *stop writing/rendering going forward*, never *retract what already happened*.

| Flag | Effect of turning OFF mid-flight | One-way door? |
|---|---|---|
| `structured_core` (A) | Dual-write stops on the next `save-draft` call; existing `report_finding_instances` rows are untouched but stop being updated; the A4 cache (self-gated on the same flag) stops regenerating, so any open draft's `structured_json` cache goes stale (A5 status `stale_cache`) — draft continuation falls back to the legacy `findings_sections`/`impression` text fields, which remain the actual source for rendering as long as `render_v2` is off. | **Transitively yes** once any downstream flag (C/D/F/H/I/K/M) is enabled and has live data depending on `report_finding_instances` — turning A off then silently stales every downstream flag's data (dual-write/cache failures are caught-and-logged-only, per the as-built verbatim rule, so nothing errors loudly). Safe-off order: disable downstream flags in reverse dependency order (M, K, I, H, F, D, C) **before** disabling A. |
| `measurement_pool` (C) | Already-attached measurements keep their attachment (no delete); the attach/detach UI disappears; new pool operations stop. | No. |
| `classification` (D) | The suggested-grade ghost disappears; any grade a radiologist already accepted was written as an ordinary manually-asserted value (offer-never-act, D-08) — indistinguishable from manual entry, so nothing to roll back. | No. |
| `modality_expand` (E) | Rows already tagged with an expanded modality value (e.g. `"XA"`) keep that value — `modality` is a plain text column, not a DB enum; dropdowns/filters stop **offering** the expanded set going forward, but existing filtering/search on those values keeps working. | **Conditionally yes**: if `classification` (D) or `catalog_delta` (H) is enabled and keys its scales/bundles by the expanded modality set, turning E off while D or H is on orphans that matching logic. Disable D and H before disabling E. |
| `render_v2` (F) | The renderer stops being the sole text producer for the *next* edit action; text already inserted into an open draft's canvas is not retroactively rewritten. **Hazard**: if a draft has renderEngine-authored sentences (slot-based side/severity/chronicity/level/value phrasing) and the flag flips off before that draft signs, the legacy engine's exact-match removal logic (`mergeBlock`/`removeBlock`) may not recognize renderEngine's sentence shape, and a subsequent Quick Select toggle can leave an orphaned sentence in the draft. | **Operational one-way door within a draft's lifetime** (not permanent): never disable F while any draft that was created or edited under render_v2 remains open and unsigned. The rollback runbook (Chapter 13) MUST drain — sign or explicitly re-save under the reverted engine — every open render_v2-touched draft before the flag is flipped fleet-wide. Signed reports are never affected (umbrella invariant). A future ticket should stamp which render engine produced each draft's `rawFindings` so the client can refuse to continue a mixed-engine draft instead of relying on this manual runbook (tracked in Chapter 15). |
| `search_v2` (G) | Query path falls back to leading-wildcard ILIKE; zero effect on any report, draft, or signed content. | No. |
| `catalog_delta` (H) | Sync job stops; the last successfully synced bundle stays resident, so already-open drafts referencing already-synced entries are unaffected; new bundle updates stop landing. Any partial delta apply in flight at toggle time must be transactional (same idempotency discipline as migrations, Chapter 9) so a mid-apply toggle cannot leave a half-applied bundle. | No, provided delta application is transactional. |
| `hierarchy` (I) | `anatomic_zone_id`/`structure_id` stop being populated on new rows; already-populated rows keep their values (nullable, no destructive clear); the anatomic drill-down UI disappears. | No. |
| `presets` (J) | Preset resolution reverts to whatever precedence existed pre-flag; this is a resolution-order behavior over existing preference rows, not a data mutation. | No. |
| `voice_structured` (K) | PTT stops writing `source="voice"` rows; dictation reverts to the legacy transcription queue text path; already-inserted voice-sourced rows remain. Same mixed-draft hazard as `render_v2` if `render_v2` is simultaneously consuming voice-sourced instances mid-draft. | Same operational caution as F: drain open voice-touched drafts before disabling K if `render_v2` is on. |
| `multiwindow` (L) | Every open workstation reverts to single-window layout on next reload; pure client layout state, zero effect on report content. | No. |
| `ai_assist` (M) | New AI suggestions stop rendering (no new margin cards, no new ghost text). Any suggestion a radiologist already accepted is **already indistinguishable from manually-typed text** in the clinical body (D-32: "the clinical body stays clean") and is unaffected — disabling M does not retract, flag, or unwind previously accepted AI text. | **The suggestion surface is freely reversible; the audit trail is not.** The per-suggestion audit record (band, raw score, sources, engine/version, disposition) already written for suggestions rendered while M was on is a permanent record (B-10: unconditional, never disableable per study) and MUST remain readable via the provenance view regardless of M's later state. If a future refactor ever ties audit-write availability to the same flag as the suggestion surface, that is a defect, not a feature — the two must be architecturally independent gates even though they share one flag key today. |
| `scale_partition` (N) | Read-replica routing decisions stop being made for new queries; **the physical partitioning already applied to `report_finding_instances` is not undone by the flag** — partitioning is a schema change, not a runtime behavior. | **Yes, unconditionally.** Once the sentinel-gated partitioning migration has run, `scale_partition` must never be treated as a soft toggle — flipping it off only affects future read-routing, never the schema. Reversing partitioning requires a manual DDL migration under the same maintenance-window discipline as the forward migration (Chapter 9), not a flag flip. |

**One-way doors, called out explicitly (do not soften these):**

1. **`ff_radiology_scale_partition` is a hard one-way door.** The flag governs read-routing only; the partitioning DDL it triggers is permanent once applied. Treat "disable N" as "stop routing," never as "undo partitioning."
2. **`ff_radiology_structured_core` becomes a transitive one-way door** the moment any downstream flag (C, D, F, H, I, K, M) is live with real data. It may still be turned off, but only after every dependent flag has been turned off first, in reverse dependency order — never as the first move in an incident response.
3. **The AI audit trail is a permanent record independent of `ff_radiology_ai_assist`'s on/off state.** Disabling the flag stops new suggestions; it must never be implemented in a way that stops recording, and it never retracts provenance already attached to accepted suggestions.
4. **Signed reports (`patient_reports.body`) are outside every flag's reach**, per the umbrella invariant. No rollback procedure in this chapter ever touches a signed report; that boundary is enforced by D-19/D-20, not by this chapter's flags.
5. **`ff_radiology_render_v2` and `ff_radiology_voice_structured` are operational one-way doors for the lifetime of any open draft they touched** — not permanent, but mandatory to respect: drain open drafts before disabling either while the other engine could misinterpret the in-progress text.

### Acceptance criteria

- All thirteen `ff_radiology_*` flags exist as rows in `feature_flags`, seeded `enabled=false` by migration, with non-null `description` for each.
- `PATCH /api/feature-flags/:key` is reachable only by admin/super_admin, returns 404 for unknown keys, and never creates a row.
- `isFeatureEnabledServer` returns `false` for any key not present in the table, in a test that asserts no exception is thrown.
- Every legacy client-only `showX` flag either has a migration path to an `ff_radiology_*` key on record, or is documented as staying client-only with a named reason; no new `showX` flags exist after this chapter ships.
- For every flag, this chapter's dependency-ordering table (§8.3) matches what the code actually gates — verified by a reviewer reading each strand's wiring ticket against the table before that flag leaves internal stage.
- The rollback table (§8.5) is exercised at least once per flag, for real, during that flag's pilot-stage kill-switch drill (§8.4), with the drill outcome recorded in the changelog entry.
- No flag PATCH in production is missing a changelog entry naming its strand ticket (C-17).
- The AI audit-trail independence claim (finding 3 above) has a regression test: disabling `ff_radiology_ai_assist` after suggestions exist, then confirming the provenance view still renders those suggestions' full audit records.
- The multi-center prerequisite (`branch_id` on `feature_flags`) is either shipped or explicitly out of scope with a tracked ticket before any second-site onboarding is scheduled.

### Build order

1. Confirm the as-built mechanism (§8.1) matches production exactly — `feature_flags` table, `GET/PATCH /api/feature-flags`, client precedence guard — before touching any individual strand; this chapter assumes it, it does not (re-)build it.
2. Bring `ff_radiology_structured_core` through internal → pilot → department stage first and alone; nothing else starts internal stage until it is at department stage (§8.3, §8.4).
3. Build and stage Tier 1 flags (`measurement_pool`, `modality_expand`, `hierarchy`, `presets`, `voice_structured`, `search_v2`) in parallel once `structured_core` clears department stage. Each depends on Chapter 5 (Measurement Lifecycle, for C), Chapter 3 (Finding Lifecycle, for I and K's write target), and Chapter 7 (AI Interaction Lifecycle, for K's PTT contract) being implemented ahead of its own flag wiring.
4. Land the Chapter 1 catalog importer (C-01) as a non-flagged prerequisite before starting `ff_radiology_catalog_delta`'s (H) internal stage; land `modality_expand` (E) to department stage before starting `classification` (D) or `catalog_delta` (H).
5. Do not start `ff_radiology_render_v2`'s internal stage until every Tier 1 and Tier 2 flag is at hospital stage. This is the most-delayed flag by design.
6. Land the D-32 audit-swallow fixes (five `.catch(()=>{})` sites in `aiReporting.ts`) as an independent, non-flagged code-review gate before `ff_radiology_ai_assist`'s internal stage begins; do not start M's internal stage until `render_v2` is at hospital stage.
7. Schedule `ff_radiology_scale_partition` off the clinical ladder entirely, against the Chapter 9 data-volume sentinel; build the sentinel and the maintenance-window runbook (Chapter 13) before this flag is ever flipped, regardless of what stage every other flag has reached.
8. Before any multi-center onboarding (Chapter 15's future-roadmap territory), ship the `branch_id` column on `feature_flags` and the scoped-PATCH endpoint change (§8.4's multi-center row) — treat this as blocking, not optional, once a second site is on the calendar.
9. Before care-api is ever run as more than one container (any horizontal-scaling ticket, independent of the radiology strands), replace the 30-second single-process TTL cache (§8.1, point 4) with a mechanism that stays correct across processes — for example a shared cache with PATCH-triggered invalidation broadcast to every process, or dropping the cache in favor of a per-request read once request volume justifies it. Until that ships, horizontal scaling of care-api is blocked: a PATCH on one process would leave other processes serving a stale flag value for up to 30 seconds.

---

## Chapter 9 — Database evolution

The database does not get redesigned — the architecture is frozen (§0, CTO review) — but it does get *extended*, deliberately and in a fixed order, from the as-built radiology schema (~95 radiology-relevant tables across `lib/db/src/schema/`) to the target state the other chapters require: a real catalog store, a canonical measurement entity, an unconditional AI audit trail, and an analytics-events store, all landing additively behind the two sanctioned migration paths (D-36). This chapter is the owning chapter for what those new tables are called, what they relate to, what indexes their access patterns demand, how a schema change physically ships to the single Synology Postgres box without becoming the next `zz_schema_reconcile` scar, what grows unbounded and how it is partitioned on NAS-class hardware, and how signed clinical data is retained forever while everything else has an honest expiry. It does not restate the migration discipline already fixed in Part 0 §0.7 (protected files, the two-step migration process, the `drizzle-kit push` hazard, deployment container ordering) — it assumes that discipline and builds on top of it. It is the answer chapter for CTO findings C1 (identity strategy), part of C2 (canonical entity storage, split with Chapters 1 and 5), and the physical half of D5 (schema-mutation-path fragmentation).

### Tables

**As-built inventory — what exists today and what it means for new work.** Radiology/reporting tables split into four groups: (1) tables this handbook has already declared canonical and evolving, (2) tables declared canonical but frozen in shape (legacy consumers keep reading them, no new writers), (3) tables declared legacy and read-only, and (4) tables whose role this chapter must state because no other chapter owns them. Every row below cites the decision that settled it; where two source documents named different tables for the same job, the decision ID is the resolution, not this chapter.

| Table | Role | Status | Governing decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| `report_finding_instances` | Source of truth for structured findings | Canonical, evolving (Ch.2) | B-22 |
| `radiology_report_drafts` | Working draft; `structured_json` regenerated cache | Canonical, evolving (Ch.2) | A2/A4 as-built |
| `patient_reports` | Authoritative signed record (`body`); version-stamp columns | Canonical, evolving (Ch.1/2) | D-19, D-18 |
| `radiology_studies` | RIS/billing-linked study record; `final_report` is a denormalized projection, writes prohibited post-sign | Canonical for RIS identity; `final_report` frozen | D-19, D-28 |
| `radiology_worklist` | Operational worklist key (`:worklistId` routes key off it) | Canonical (as-built wins) | D-28 |
| `dicom_studies` | Canonical PACS/DICOM study identity | Canonical | D-28 |
| `teleradiology_assignments` | `final_report` is a denormalized projection | Frozen (writes prohibited post-sign) | D-19 |
| `report_amendments` | The one post-sign amendment chain | Canonical | D-19, D-20 |
| `feature_flags` | `ff_radiology_*` registry | Canonical, evolving (`branch_id` future column) | D-24, Ch.8 |
| `structured_report_templates` | Modality/bodyPart structured-template store; render_v2 consolidation target | Canonical | D-29 |
| `ai_normal_report_templates` | Normal templates, unique `(name, modality)` | Canonical | D-29 |
| `radiology_text_macros` / `radiology_smart_macros` | `/`-namespace macro store | Canonical | D-25 |
| `radiology_quick_favorites` | Tile pinning/starring (real FK, cascade) | Canonical | D-26 |
| `radiology_study_locks` | Heartbeat concurrency locks, reused for pre-stage (C-07) | Canonical | as-built |
| `audit_logs` | Tamper-evident hash chain | Canonical, hardening in progress | D-34 |
| `radiology_lesions` | Lesion registry, extended for Follow-up Comparison | Canonical, evolving (Ch.5) | Ch.5 |
| `ai_prompt_templates` / `ai_model_routes` | Shared AI provider/prompt infrastructure | Canonical, never forked per module | 🟢 PROTECTED_FILES |
| `ai_job_queue` | PACS-driven background AI draft hook | Canonical (secondary drafting path) | D-30 |
| `radiology_user_item_usage_logs` | Legacy tile-usage/recents log | Migration source, not the analytics target shape | Ch.2 (Analytics) |
| `radiology_structured_templates` | Legacy structured-template table | Frozen, read-only | D-29 |
| `radiologist_macros` | Legacy macro table | Legacy, read-only, one-time import into text macros | D-25 |
| `usg_measurements`, `spinal_measurements`, doppler/echo/fetal measurement tables | Per-modality measurement stores | Keep writing exactly as today; each becomes a *producer into* the measurement pool on its own migration ticket | Ch.5 |
| `pacs_settings`, `dicom_pull_jobs` | PACS/DICOM operational config and job queue | Canonical; missing indexes are backlog items (see Indexes) | audits-memory §5 |

**New tables required — named, described, owned, related.** Six new physical stores answer the mandated scope; two mandated categories (recommendations, impressions) deliberately get **no new table**, and that absence is itself the decision, stated here so no engineer goes looking for one.

1. **Catalog entities — `radiology_catalog_entries`, `radiology_catalog_libraries`, `radiology_catalog_aliases`, `radiology_catalog_combos`, `radiology_catalog_templates`.** Owner: Chapter 1 (catalog-lifecycle), content-owned by the named radiologist content editor per entry. Purpose: physical home of the imported YAML packs — one row per finding/shared-library entry/alias/combo/normal-template, each carrying `id_key`, `owner`, `version`, `review_date`, `status ∈ {draft, active, retired}` (C-01, §15.5 metadata). Key relationships: `report_finding_instances.finding_id` re-points here from `radiology_quick_findings.id` once the importer ships (Ch.1 Import); every reference is by `id_key` value, never a DB foreign key, matching the codebase's established catalog/config-by-value convention (as-built precedent: `radiology_quick_findings.study_type` linking to `radiology_study_tabs` by name) — this is a deliberate exception to normal FK discipline, made so a retirement or a re-seed never cascade-deletes a report's history (B-01).
2. **Finding instances — no new table.** `report_finding_instances` already exists and remains the physical home (B-22); the only schema evolution here is the eventual re-pointing of `finding_id` (item 1) and the population of the already-existing nullable `anatomic_zone_id`/`structure_id` columns once Strand I (`ff_radiology_hierarchy`) lands — both are additive, in-place semantic evolutions of an existing column, not new tables.
3. **Measurements — `radiology_measurement_pool`.** Owner: Chapter 5 (measurement-lifecycle), behind `ff_radiology_measurement_pool` (Strand C, seeded off). Purpose: the one canonical, finding-independent measurement entity answering CTO C2/§4.5/§4.7 — replaces the pattern of 12+ disjoint measurement tables joined by lowercased label strings. Columns (from Ch.5, restated here only for the schema-evolution record): owning study/worklist reference; resolved `meas.<key>` (nullable until resolved); raw extracted label/value, kept verbatim; typed numeric value + whitelist unit; nullable coded-concept triple (code, scheme, meaning); `source ∈ manual/ocr/ai/dicom_sr/viewer`; confidence band + raw score; provenance (engine/model/version, extraction job id, image/SOP reference); `status ∈ pending/confirmed/attached/rejected/superseded`; `attached_finding_instance_id` (nullable FK into `report_finding_instances`); `lesion_id` (nullable, explicit-link only). Key relationships: attach/detach against `report_finding_instances` (Ch.2); `lesion_id` against `radiology_lesions` (Ch.5). Ships empty; existing per-modality stores keep writing until each is migrated to be a pool producer, one ticket at a time (B-23).
4. **Recommendations — no new table.** Chapter 4 is explicit: recommendation text is a rendered projection of `report_finding_instances` rows plus `rec.*` catalog codes (item 1 above); dedup/conflict-resolution state lives in the in-memory/render-time array metadata, never persisted separately, because the suppressed variant is always reconstructable from the finding-instance rows the system already retains forever. Do not build a `recommendation_instances` table — it would be a second, driftable copy of data `report_finding_instances` already owns.
5. **Impressions — no new table.** Chapter 6 is equally explicit: the Impression is assembled at render time from `impression_fragment`s carried by Draft-state finding instances, and its reproducibility comes from `catalog_version`/`template_version`/`render_engine_version` stamping at sign (item below), not from a separate impression-rules-version table. The legacy `PATCH /impression-rules` mutable store and `ai_impressions` are frozen under the same posture as any other legacy AI surface (D-30) — no new writes, no new schema investment.
6. **AI audit — `ai_suggestion_audit`.** Owner: Chapter 7 (ai-interaction-lifecycle), per D-32. Purpose: the one authoritative per-rendered-suggestion record, replacing the five `.catch(()=>{})`-guarded swallow sites in `aiReporting.ts` and the scattered partial records in `ai_quality_scores`/`ai_extraction_results`. Columns (from Ch.7): `worklist_ref`/`study_ref`, server-derived `user`, `surface`, `suggestion_class`, `confidence_band`+`raw_score`, `evidence_sources`, `engine`/`provider`/`model_version`, `prompt_template_id`+`version`, `input_hash`, `output`, `disposition`, `dismiss_reason`, `edited_text`+`edit_offsets`, `rendered_at`/`actioned_at`, and a **mandatory** `audit_log_id` foreign key into the `audit_logs` hash chain — every write to this table is paired, in the same transaction, with an `audit_logs` entry, so AI provenance is never a second, weaker trail sitting outside the tamper-evident chain the CTO review flagged. Key relationships: FK into `audit_logs` (mandatory); logical references to catalog `id_key`s and finding-instance ids (by value, not FK, matching the platform convention).
7. **Analytics events — `radiology_analytics_events`.** Owner: this chapter physically, Chapter 14 (analytics) conceptually, per the quarterly content-governance loop (B-27) every content-owning chapter (1, 2, 4, 5) already promises to feed. Purpose: the single ingestion target for every state-transition event those chapters define — Chapter 2's `finding.fired`/`finding.confirmed`/`finding.removed`/`finding.signed`/`finding.escalation_*`/`finding.amended`, Chapter 1's tile-fire and free-text-repetition signals, Chapter 4's recommendation-deletion signal, Chapter 7's suppression/dismiss signals feeding the personalization firewall's suppression state. Columns: `event_type` (dotted name, e.g. `finding.fired`), `occurred_at`, `actor` (server-derived staff identity, D-33 — never client-supplied), `study_ref`/`worklist_ref`/`draft_id`/`report_id` (nullable, whichever applies), `catalog_key` (the stable join dimension — never a display label, per B-01), and a `payload` jsonb column for event-specific dimensions (specialist, communication timestamp, criticality tier, etc., per Chapter 2's event-dimension table) rather than a wide sparse column set — this table is explicitly a write-heavy, narrow-schema event log, not a normalized entity store, and its jsonb payload is read only by the analytics ingestion pipeline (Chapter 14), never by any clinical or safety-gating code path (C-04's determinism rule does not apply here because nothing in this table ever gates a blocking or critical rule). Key relationships: by value only, to `report_finding_instances`, `radiology_catalog_entries`, and `ai_suggestion_audit`, none of them a DB foreign key, for the same cascade-safety reason as item 1. As-built, `radiology_user_item_usage_logs` is the closest existing surface and is the migration source, not the target shape (Ch.2 Analytics), and is left in place, unmigrated, until this table ships.

Every new table follows the prospective identity rule (D-37, CTO C1): `bigint` identity PK, nullable `tenant_id` and `branch_id` (unused today, costs nothing), no `MAX(seq)+1` business keys — any new business/accession key generator is globally unique (UUID or site-prefixed). This is a schema-review checklist item, not a suggestion: a PR introducing a new table with a `serial`/`int4` PK is a review-block, full stop.

### Indexes

Indexes exist to serve a named access pattern, not by default — every index below is justified by a query this chapter or a dependent chapter actually issues.

| Table | Index | Access pattern served |
|---|---|---|
| `report_finding_instances` | `rfi_draft_idx` (draft_id), `rfi_report_idx` (report_id), `rfi_finding_idx` (finding_id) | Existing as-built; draft load, signed-report snapshot lookup, per-finding analytics rollups |
| `report_finding_instances` | Partial index on `report_id` `WHERE report_id IS NOT NULL` | Signed-only queries (provenance view, amendment lookups) skip scanning the much larger draft-scoped row set |
| `report_finding_instances` | `(anatomic_zone_id, structure_id)` composite, added when Strand I populates them | Zone/Structure drill-down UI (Ch.1 relevance note); index ships inert (nullable columns, no rows yet) until the strand wires writers |
| `radiology_catalog_entries` | Unique on `id_key` | Import upsert path, id_key-immutability enforcement (B-01) — a DB-level backstop behind the importer's own hard-error check, never the only guard |
| `radiology_catalog_entries` | `(study_key, status)` | The Copilot's `active`-only vocabulary read (B-03) and Quick Select's live-tile query — both hot, per-study, status-filtered reads |
| `radiology_catalog_entries` | `(pack_id, version)` composite | Idempotency check on re-import (Ch.1 Idempotency) — the importer compares a pack's declared version against the last-recorded version for that `pack_id` |
| `radiology_catalog_aliases` | Unique `(study_key, alias)` | DB-level backstop for the importer's alias-uniqueness validation (B-16) — concurrent imports must never race past the application-level check alone |
| `radiology_measurement_pool` | `attach_finding_instance_id`, `lesion_id`, `(source, status)`, `study_ref` | Attach/detach lookups, lesion-timeline assembly, the pending-review triage queue (Attention-band rows awaiting field-level review), per-study pool view |
| `ai_suggestion_audit` | `(study_ref)`, `(user, rendered_at)`, `(suggestion_class, disposition)`, `audit_log_id` (FK index) | Per-study provenance view, practice-dashboard acceptance/edit/override rollups by feature/modality/model-version (Ch.7 Aggregation), the mandatory chain-join |
| `radiology_analytics_events` | `(event_type, occurred_at)`, `(catalog_key, occurred_at)` | Time-bounded rollups for the quarterly governance loop (never-fired tiles, wording-review nominations) and the standing scorecard (§4.6) |
| `patients`/report-search columns | `pg_trgm` GIN indexes | Deferred to `ff_radiology_search_v2` (Strand G, C-18) — until it lands, leading-wildcard `ILIKE` is an accepted, bounded cost on the single box; btree indexes cannot serve this pattern and must not be added as false coverage |
| `pacs_settings` | Unique `(key, category)` | Missing today (audits-memory §5); prevents silent duplicate-key config drift |
